347 — Serialization Runtime with Zero-Copy Support

Project Number: 347

Project Name: Serialization Runtime with Zero-Copy Support

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

Goal: Implement, from scratch in Rust, a serialization runtime built around a zero-copy wire format: a schema/IDL front-end, deterministic layout computation (data-section vs pointer-section, alignment, list/union layout), a builder/encode path over an arena + segment model with struct/list/ far pointers and a canonical encoding mode, and a decode path whose "deserialize" is a single validate-then-view pass (bounds, alignment, pointer-target, recursion/segment limits as the trust boundary) after which accessor Reader<'a, T> views read fields directly out of the buffer with no parse and no allocation, plus code generation and a forward/backward schema-evolution checker (crates ser-core/schema/layout/encode/decode/runtime/codegen/evolve). It is a serialization runtime + format + schema toolchain modeling the Cap'n Proto / FlatBuffers / rkyv family — not an RPC framework, a JSON-style interchange library, or a columnar analytics format. Validated by differential tests against a copying serializer and Cap'n Proto/FlatBuffers semantics, plus validator fuzzing.

1. Goal

1.1 Engineering Definition

Build a from-scratch serialization runtime around a zero-copy wire format: IDL front-end → layout computation → encode path → validate-then-view decode path. "Zero-copy decode" = one validation pass, then accessor reads cast directly into the received byte buffer; no parse loop, no per-field heap allocation, no memcpy. Models the Cap'n Proto / FlatBuffers / rkyv family: schema-first, binary, struct/row-oriented, endian-fixed (little-endian), word-aligned (8-byte word).

1.2 System Boundary — In Scope

  • .szs IDL: struct / enum / union / list type definitions, field ids, defaults, evolution annotations.
  • Layout computation: deterministic offset assignment, data-section / pointer-section, alignment/padding.
  • Encode path: arena + segment allocator, Builder API, nested struct/list/pointer writing, canonical mode.
  • Decode path: validation pass (bounds, alignment, pointer targets, recursion depth, segment size), then Reader<'a, T> zero-copy accessors.
  • Schema evolution checker: append-only rules, optional-field defaults, forward/backward compat.
  • Code generation: per-type Reader/Builder accessors (Rust first, reflective fallback).
  • Operational tooling: inspector, wire-diff, schema registry, telemetry (encode/decode/validate latency, bytes, rejection counts).

1.3 System Boundary — Explicit Non-Goals

Non-Goal Rationale
Transport / RPC Out of scope; hooks provided for framing layer
General interchange (JSON/YAML/TOML emit) Not a data-interchange library
Columnar/analytics format (Arrow-compatible) Row/struct-oriented; Arrow is a reference, not the target
Schema-less encoding Schema-first only; reflection is schema-backed
Dynamic typing / runtime type erasure Static schema; reflective path is schema-driven, not untyped
Encryption / signing Out of scope; hook for envelope layer
Versioned migration / transforms Evolution = compat only; field-value transforms are user-supplied
Async I/O runtime Sync encode/decode; caller owns async boundary
Distributed schema registry (cloud) ser-tool provides local registry; cloud ops are user-supplied

1.4 Deployment Model

  • Embedded library (ser-runtime + ser-decode + ser-encode): linked into any Rust binary; zero required runtime dependencies beyond std.
  • CLI tooling (ser-tool, ser-cli): developer workstation / CI pipeline — schema validation, diff, code-gen trigger.
  • Schema registry (ser-tool, embedded SQLite or flat-file): optional, local; pluggable remote backend via trait.
  • Code-gen plugin (ser-codegen): build-time build.rs invocation or standalone binary.
  • no_std target: ser-core, ser-layout, ser-encode, ser-decode all conditionally no_std + alloc; ser-runtime requires std for framing/IO helpers.

1.5 Target Users / Operators / Developers

Role Interaction Surface
Application developer Builder/Reader API, generated accessors, encode() / decode() / validate()
Schema author .szs IDL, ser-cli schema check, evolution checker
Platform/infra engineer Message framing, segment budget config, telemetry hooks
Tooling/ops ser-tool inspect, wire-diff, schema-registry CLI
Portfolio reviewer Full crate tree, tests, bench suite, per-phase deliverables

1.6 Phase-0 Deliverable — Crate Inventory

Twelve crates locked at project init; each a Cargo workspace member:

  1. ser-core — primitive types: TypeId, FieldId, Offset, SegmentId, WireType, Schema, TypeDef, Field, Layout, Pointer, Message, SerError.
  2. ser-schema — IDL parser + schema model: type defs, name/id resolution, schema hashing, schema-level validation.
  3. ser-layout — layout computer: field offset assignment, data/pointer-section split, alignment/padding, list/union layout.
  4. ser-encode — builder/writer: arena + segment allocation, Builder API, data + pointer writing, nested structs/lists, canonical encoding.
  5. ser-decode — zero-copy reader: validation pass (bounds/alignment/pointer/recursion), Reader<'a, T> accessor casts, lazy access.
  6. ser-runtime — top-level: Message, Reader, Builder, segment/arena management, public encode() / decode() / validate(), message framing.
  7. ser-codegen — code generation: emit per-type Reader/Builder accessors (Rust target); reflective fallback.
  8. ser-evolve — schema evolution + compatibility: append-only rules, optional fields + defaults, reserved holes, forward/backward compat checker.
  9. ser-tool — inspector / pretty-printer, wire-diff, schema registry.
  10. ser-telemetry — metrics (encode/decode/validate latency, bytes, validation rejections), tracing integration.
  11. ser-cli — CLI driver: schema ops, code-gen trigger, inspect/diff dispatch.
  12. ser-bench — differential + performance harness; comparisons vs Cap'n Proto, FlatBuffers, bincode, serde_json.

1.8 Deferred Features (PhaseDeferred)

  • PhaseDeferred("DF1") — additional codegen targets (C, Python, Go bindings).
  • PhaseDeferred("DF2") — remote/cloud schema registry adapter trait.
  • PhaseDeferred("DF3") — packed-scalar-list varint packing (Cap'n Proto pack mode).
  • PhaseDeferred("DF4") — streaming / incremental encode for large messages.
  • PhaseDeferred("DF5") — WASM target (ser-core/encode/decode no_std+alloc).
  • PhaseDeferred("DF6") — wire-level encryption/signing envelope.
  • PhaseDeferred("DF7") — mmap-backed zero-copy read (file-backed buffers).

1.9 Timeline

  • Phase 0 (crate scaffold + core types): 1 week.
  • Phases 1–5 (schema/IDL → layout → wire format/pointers → builder/encode → zero-copy decode/validation): 8 weeks.
  • Phases 6–9 (codegen, schema evolution, variable-width types, cross-language conformance): 6 weeks.
  • Phases 10–11 (performance, safety hardening): open-ended; gated on Phase 9 completion; typically 3–4 weeks.
  • Phases 12–13 (observability/tooling, differential/fuzz/benchmark release readiness): 3 weeks.
  • Full implementation estimate: 4–6 months single engineer at production bar; see Appendix B for the per-phase duration/risk/exit-criteria breakdown.

1.10 Language Rationale — Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78)

  • Zero-copy demands precise memory layout control (repr(C), aligned reads), alignment guarantees, and lifetime-checked borrows (Reader<'a, T> borrows buffer without escaping).
  • Unsafe casts confined behind validation trust boundary; safe accessors proven correct by validation invariant.
  • No GC: decode allocates nothing post-validation; encode allocates only in arena.
  • Compile-time generics: zero-overhead Reader/Builder monomorphization.
  • Rejected: C++ (unsafe untrusted-buffer handling without borrow checker); Go/Java (GC + layout control limits + portfolio consistency).
  • MSRV 1.78: std::ptr::addr_of!, non-null provenance, offset_of! macro stabilized.

1.11 Prerequisites

Domain Requirement
Systems Manual memory layout, alignment, padding, endianness, pointer arithmetic
Rust Lifetimes, unsafe, repr(C), slice casting, trait objects, proc-macros
Concurrency Arena thread-safety, Sync/Send bounds, atomic segment counters
Compilers Parsing (recursive-descent or PEG), AST/IR design, code generation
Distributed systems Schema evolution, backward/forward compatibility, wire format versioning
Security Amplification attacks, bounds-check completeness, fuzzing discipline

2. Why Build This

2.1 Core Systems Knowledge Taught

  • Memory layout mastery: computing struct offsets, alignment padding, data-section vs pointer-section split; understanding what a C/Rust compiler does at the byte level.
  • Zero-copy semantics: the difference between copy, borrow, and view; why &'a [u8] accessors are sound only after a complete validation pass; lifetime propagation through accessor types.
  • Trust boundary engineering: where to place the single security perimeter in a decode pipeline; why post-validation panics are bugs (not expected errors); amplification-attack surface analysis.
  • Pointer representation: relative offsets vs absolute pointers; far-pointer indirection for cross-segment references; why Cap'n Proto's pointer encoding avoids absolute addresses.
  • Arena allocation: slab/bump allocators, segment overflow, fragmentation; why arena + single-free is correct for message lifetime.
  • Canonical encoding: why idempotent binary serialization requires fixed field order + zeroed padding; cryptographic hash stability.
  • Schema evolution under real constraints: append-only field ids, reserved holes, default injection on missing fields — the exact mechanics of backward/forward compat at wire level.

2.2 Historical + Architectural Significance

  • First-generation: Protocol Buffers (Google, 2008) — copy-on-decode, varint, mature ecosystem. Key lesson: every access through a generated getter copies/parses.
  • Zero-copy generation: Cap'n Proto (Kenton Varda, 2013) — "infinitely faster serialization" claim; validate-then-view; pointer-chasing without parsing. Architected the trust boundary model this project implements.
  • FlatBuffers (Wouter van Oortmerssen, Google, 2014) — vtable-based optional fields; offset tables allow field presence without reserved bytes for every field.
  • rkyv (Rust, 2021) — relative pointers, ArchivedType, safe zero-copy in safe Rust; demonstrates Rust's lifetime model as the correctness layer.
  • SBE (Simple Binary Encoding) — fixed-size, cache-friendly, no pointer indirection; contrast: zero allocation but schema-rigid.
  • Apache Arrow — zero-copy columnar; contrast: correct for analytics workloads, wrong for row/struct message passing.
  • Building this project forces direct engagement with every design decision in these systems.

2.3 Engineering Muscles Developed

Muscle Exercise
Parser construction Recursive-descent IDL parser; error recovery; span-accurate diagnostics
Type system design WireType, TypeDef, Layout — coherent, closed type lattice
Code generation Template-free emit; syn/quote hygiene; testing emitted code
Unsafe Rust Slice transmute behind trust boundary; provenance correctness; Miri validation
Fuzzing Structure-aware fuzzer for validation pass; corpus minimization
Benchmarking Criterion differential bench; wall clock vs instruction counts; cache-miss attribution
Operational tooling Wire inspector, schema diff, registry — production support artifacts
API design Ergonomic Builder/Reader; lifetime surface area; error propagation

2.4 Production Engineering Concepts

  • Observability-first: telemetry taps at every encode/decode boundary before optimization; metrics drive phase-gate decisions.
  • Failure budget: validation rejection rate is a first-class metric; spikes indicate wire corruption, client bugs, or attack probes.
  • Schema as contract: SchemaHash pinned in message frame; receiver can reject unknown schemas before decode begins.
  • Amplification defense: segment count, total bytes, and recursion depth are hard-bounded in validation; malformed messages cannot cause quadratic work.
  • Rollout safety: evolution checker in CI blocks backward-compat-breaking schema changes before deploy; gated by SchemaHash comparison.
  • Canonical encoding for dedup: content-addressed message stores require byte-identical encoding; canonical mode is a first-class feature.

2.5 Cross-Portfolio Links

Related Project Connection
Compiler (front-end, IR) IDL parser uses same recursive-descent / PEG skills; type-checking pipeline mirrors schema validation
Storage engine (LSM / B-tree) Wire format is the on-disk record format; schema evolution mirrors storage column evolution; arena allocator pattern shared
Networking stack Message framing layer; segment boundaries map to TCP segment boundaries; zero-copy socket reads
Distributed consensus (Raft/Paxos) Log entries serialized with this runtime; schema evolution during rolling upgrade
GPU/tensor runtime Tensor descriptor serialization; zero-copy buffer hand-off between host/device
Vector DB Embedding vector serialization; schema-versioned index entries
Query engine Row/struct deserialization for query execution; columnar projection from row buffers
Task scheduler Job descriptor serialization; schema evolution for scheduler config

2.6 Calibration Value

  • Demonstrates mastery of the unsafe/safe boundary in production Rust: reviewers can challenge every unsafe block.
  • Forces the author to justify every layout decision at the byte level — no abstraction hides the wire.
  • ser-bench produces objective throughput/latency comparisons against Cap'n Proto and FlatBuffers — claims are falsifiable.
  • Schema evolution checker is a standalone correctness artifact: formal append-only rules, machine-checked.
  • Fuzzing corpus for the validation pass demonstrates security engineering discipline.

2.7 What Mastery Looks Like

  • Memory layout at the byte level (Phases 2–3): Given any schema, the reader can derive field offsets, padding bytes, data-section vs pointer-section split, and segment layout by hand — without running any code — then verify using the ser-tool inspect hex dump.
  • Zero-copy soundness (Phase 5): The reader can audit every unsafe block in ser-decode, reconstruct the proof argument that each cast is sound (bounds + alignment pre-confirmed by validation pass), and identify which scenario would make each // SAFETY: comment false.
  • Trust boundary architecture (Phases 5, 11): The reader can explain why placing the trust boundary at "validate once, then trust forever" is categorically safer than per-accessor checking, and trace exactly which validation checks prevent each of the five SerError variants from arising in the accessor path.
  • Schema evolution mechanics (Phases 7–8): The reader can reason through a field addition, a union arm addition, and an attempted field-type change — predicting the exact CompatViolation the checker emits and why old decoders still read new wire correctly.
  • Codegen correctness (Phase 6): The reader can trace a single field from its IDL definition through SchemaHash → Layout → emitted Rust accessor constant → wire byte offset, and explain why non-deterministic HashMap ordering would silently break canonical encoding.
  • Amplification defense (Phases 5, 11): The reader can construct a minimal wire byte sequence that would cause quadratic work without the segment-count + recursion-depth + total-bytes limits, then prove those three limits together are sufficient to cap work to O(message bytes).
  • Observability-driven engineering (Phase 12): The reader can identify which metric spike corresponds to each of the ten failure modes, write the alert rule for each, and link it to the runbook that resolves it.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

    schema IDL (.szs)
            |
      [ser-schema] parse -> Schema (TypeDefs, Fields, evolution rules) -> SchemaHash
            |
      [ser-layout] assign offsets, data-section vs pointer-section, alignment/padding, list/union layout (deterministic)
            |
            +--> [ser-codegen] emit per-type Reader/Builder accessors (target lang) | reflective fallback
            |
    BUILD:  app --Builder--> [ser-encode] arena/segment alloc -> write data words + pointers -> [ser-runtime] Message (framing)
            |                                                                                          |
            |                                                                         wire bytes (segments + frame header)
            |                                                                                          |
    READ:   wire bytes --> [ser-decode] VALIDATION PASS (bounds, alignment, pointer targets, recursion/segment limits)  <== trust boundary
            |                                  | on success -> Reader<'a, T>
            |                       zero-copy: accessors cast directly into buf, NO parse, NO alloc, lazy field reads
            |                                  app reads fields
      [ser-evolve] forward/backward compat check (schema vs schema)     [ser-telemetry] taps encode/decode/validate
      [ser-tool] inspector / wire-diff / schema registry

3.2 ser-schema — IDL Parser + Schema Model

  • Input: .szs source text; output: Schema { types, root, schema_hash }.
  • Responsibilities: tokenize, recursive-descent parse, build TypeDef/Field AST, resolve names → TypeIds, assign FieldIds, compute SchemaHash (deterministic hash over canonical type/field ordering), run schema-level validation (no duplicate ids, no forward-reference cycles, union exhaustiveness).
  • Data flow: .szs text → token stream → AST → resolved Schema → SchemaHash.
  • Trust boundary contribution: schema validity (not wire validity) — malformed schemas are rejected here, before layout or encode begins.
  • Failure modes: SerError::SchemaInvalid on parse error, duplicate field id, unresolvable type reference, cyclic struct (non-list).
  • External interface: parse_schema(src: &str) -> Result<Schema, SerError>.

3.3 ser-layout — Deterministic Layout Computer

  • Input: Schema; output: Schema with all TypeDef.layout fields populated.
  • Responsibilities: assign Field.offset (byte offset within data section), split fields into data-section scalars vs pointer-section references, compute Layout { data_words, ptr_words, align }, handle list element layout, union discriminant + arm layout, padding/alignment rules.
  • Invariant: layout is a pure function of schema — identical schema → byte-identical layout on every platform; no host-layout influence.
  • Failure modes: overflow if data_words or ptr_words exceeds u16::MAX; SerError::SchemaInvalid.
  • Performance implication: layout runs once at build time (code-gen) or once per schema load (reflective path); O(|fields|) per type.

3.4 ser-codegen — Accessor Code Generator

  • Input: Schema + target language tag; output: generated source file(s) with per-type Reader<'a, T> / Builder<'a, T> accessor impls.
  • Responsibilities: emit fn get_<field>(&self) -> <WireType> for each field (Reader), fn set_<field>(&mut self, v: <WireType>) (Builder); embed offset constants and default values; emit From<&'a [u8]> for Reader.
  • Reflective fallback: ser-runtime exposes a reflective accessor path using Schema at runtime; no generated code required, ~3× slower.
  • Build integration: invoked from build.rs; generated files placed in $OUT_DIR; hermetic (no network, no disk side-effects beyond $OUT_DIR).

3.5 ser-encode — Builder + Arena

  • Input: Schema + app data via Builder API; output: Message { segments, root }.
  • Responsibilities: bump-allocator arena for segment bytes; Builder<'a, T> assigns field slots by offset, writes scalars into data section, allocates child structs/lists into arena, writes pointer words, handles segment overflow via new-segment alloc, canonical-mode zeroing of padding.
  • Concurrency boundary: Builder is single-threaded per message; multiple independent messages may be built concurrently (arena per message).
  • Data flow: app calls → Builder methods → arena writes → Message ownership transfer → framing in ser-runtime.
  • Failure modes: SerError::OutOfBounds if segment limit exceeded; alloc failure (OOM) propagated as error, not panic.

3.6 ser-decode — Validation Pass + Reader (Trust Boundary)

  • Input: raw wire bytes (&'a [u8]), Schema; output: Reader<'a, RootType> on success, SerError on failure.
  • THE TRUST BOUNDARY: every safety invariant is established here. Post-validation, no check is needed.
  • Validation pass checks (in order): frame header parse, segment count ≤ limit, total bytes ≤ limit, each pointer target within declared segment, alignment of every pointer target, recursion/nesting depth ≤ limit, list element count × element size does not overflow.
  • Zero-copy read: Reader<'a, T> { buf: &'a [u8], base: Offset } — accessor methods compute base + field_offset, cast &buf[range] to scalar or sub-Reader; NO heap alloc, NO copy.
  • Absent field: if base + field_offset is beyond the message's declared data section (older writer), accessor returns Field.default.
  • Failure modes: SerError::{OutOfBounds, Misaligned, BadPointer, DepthExceeded, Truncated}.
  • Security invariant: amplification attack impossible — validation is O(message bytes), bounded recursion; no quadratic fan-out.

3.7 ser-runtime — Public API + Message Framing

  • Input/output: app-facing; bridges encode/decode, owns segment buffers, provides framing.
  • Responsibilities: encode(builder: Builder) -> Bytes, decode<'a, T>(buf: &'a [u8], schema: &Schema) -> Result<Reader<'a, T>, SerError>, validate(buf: &[u8], schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), SerError>; message frame header (segment table: count + per-segment byte length); SchemaHash embedding in frame.
  • Persistence boundary: runtime hands opaque Bytes to caller; caller owns transport/storage.
  • Protocol boundary: frame header format is the external wire contract (versioned, layout-stable).
  • Concurrency: encode and decode are stateless functions over owned/borrowed data; safe to call from multiple threads without synchronization.

3.8 ser-evolve — Schema Evolution Checker

  • Input: two Schema values (old, new); output: CompatResult { forward_ok, backward_ok, violations: Vec<CompatViolation> }.
  • Responsibilities: enforce append-only field id rule (no id reuse, no id removal below max), verify optional-field default presence, flag union-arm removal, detect type-change violations (WireType mismatch on same FieldId).
  • Integration points: CI gate (build.rs or test), ser-cli schema diff, runtime schema-mismatch handling (SchemaHash comparison in frame).
  • Failure modes: returns violations list; does not panic; caller decides policy (warn vs abort).

3.9 ser-tool — Inspector / Wire-Diff / Registry

  • Input: wire bytes + schema (inspect/diff); schema files (registry).
  • Responsibilities: pretty-print a message to human-readable form (reflective decode path), byte-level hex dump with field annotations, wire-diff (two messages → field-by-field delta), schema registry (store/retrieve/version schema by SchemaHash, flat-file or SQLite backend).
  • Operational importance: primary debugging surface for wire corruption, field default issues, evolution regressions.

3.10 ser-telemetry — Metrics + Tracing

  • Input: tap points at encode entry/exit, validation entry/exit, decode entry/exit.
  • Responsibilities: record encode/decode/validate wall-clock latency (histogram), byte counts, validation rejection counts (by SerError variant), tracing span integration (compatible with tracing crate).
  • Failure modes: telemetry must be infallible; metric emission errors are silently dropped, never propagated to caller.
  • No-overhead path: tap API uses #[inline] + cfg(feature = "telemetry"); zero cost when feature disabled.

3.11 Data Flow Summary

Stage Actor Data Allocation
Schema load ser-schema .szs → Schema Once, at startup
Layout ser-layout Schema → Schema+Layout In-place, once
Code-gen ser-codegen Schema → source Build-time only
Encode ser-encode App data → arena bytes Arena per message
Frame ser-runtime Message → wire bytes Frame header copy
Validate ser-decode wire bytes → trust token Zero
Read ser-decode trust token → Reader<'a,T> Zero
Field access generated/reflective Reader<'a,T> → value Zero (scalar), borrow (bytes/str)

4. System Constraints

4.1 Constraints Table

Constraint Target Hard Limit Notes
Max message size 64 MiB typical 4 GiB (u32 offset space) Configurable per-call; default cap 64 MiB
Max segment count 32 segments 512 Frame header segment table size
Max single segment size 1 GiB 2 GiB (u31 offset) Pointer offset field width
Max recursion / nesting depth 32 levels 64 Amplification defense; configurable
Max list element count 2^28 2^32 − 1 Bounded by count field in list pointer
Max schema types 4096 65535 (u16 TypeId) Practical schema size ceiling
Max fields per type 512 65535 (u16 FieldId) Layout + codegen practical limit
encode() throughput ≥ 500 MB/s — Measured on single core, L3-resident data
decode/validate() throughput ≥ 1 GB/s — Validation-only pass, cache-warm buffer
zero-copy field access latency ≤ 5 ns (scalar) — Single field read, cache-hot message
encode p50 latency (1 KB msg) ≤ 500 ns — Excludes frame write
encode p99 latency (1 KB msg) ≤ 2 µs — Including arena alloc
validate p50 latency (1 KB msg) ≤ 200 ns —
validate p99 latency (1 KB msg) ≤ 800 ns —
Schema load (parse + layout) ≤ 10 ms 100 ms At startup; amortized
Startup overhead (schema warm) 0 — No startup cost when schema pre-loaded
Shutdown guarantee Immediate — No background threads in hot path
Heap allocation per decode 0 bytes 0 bytes Enforced by API: Reader is borrowed
Heap allocation per encode O(message size) — Arena per message; freed on Message drop
Max concurrent encode goroutines Unbounded — No shared mutable state; arena per message
Max concurrent decode Unbounded — Buffer borrow is Sync; readers are Send
NUMA / CPU Single-socket assumed — No NUMA-aware allocation in Phase 0–5
Cache line size assumption 64 bytes — Layout aligns hot fields to 64-byte boundaries in canonical mode
Wire endianness Little-endian fixed — No runtime negotiation
Word size 8 bytes — Pointer arithmetic, offset units
Platform x86_64 + ARM64 — CI tests both; MSRV 1.78
no_std support ser-core/encode/decode/layout — alloc crate required; PhaseDeferred("DF5") for WASM
Availability target Library: N/A — No daemon; no uptime SLA
Fuzz coverage (validation) 100% of validation paths — libFuzzer corpus, Phase 11
Benchmark regression gate ±5% throughput — CI per-commit via ser-bench

4.2 Memory Model Constraints

  • Arena allocator: segments are Vec<u8> grown by doubling; max segment size bounded before alloc to prevent OOM amplification.
  • Reader<'a, T> holds &'a [u8] borrow; message buffer must outlive all Readers — enforced by Rust lifetime system, not runtime check.
  • Stack depth during validation: O(nesting depth) stack frames; max 64 levels → ≤ 64 recursive calls; no unbounded recursion.
  • Canonical encoding zeroes all padding bytes: no information leakage via uninitialized memory in wire bytes.

4.3 Throughput + Latency Targets by Operation

Operation p50 p95 p99 Throughput
Schema parse (10 types) 1 ms 3 ms 8 ms —
Encode (1 KB, 10 fields) 400 ns 900 ns 2 µs 500 MB/s
Encode (64 KB, nested) 40 µs 90 µs 200 µs 600 MB/s
Validate (1 KB) 150 ns 400 ns 800 ns 1 GB/s
Validate (64 KB) 30 µs 70 µs 150 µs 1.2 GB/s
Field access (scalar, cache-hot) 3 ns 5 ns 8 ns —
Field access (nested struct) 10 ns 20 ns 40 ns —
Schema hash compare < 1 ns < 1 ns < 1 ns —
Wire-diff (1 KB, 10 fields) 5 µs 15 µs 40 µs —

4.4 Scaling Assumptions

  • Horizontal: library; scales with caller's thread/process count; no shared state between messages.
  • Vertical: arena per-message; larger messages use more heap; no global allocator pressure beyond individual message lifetime.
  • Schema cardinality: ≤ 4096 types practical; schema registry lookup O(1) by SchemaHash (hash map).
  • Schema evolution frequency: checker runs in CI; not on hot path; O(|fields| × |versions|) complexity.
  • Telemetry overhead: ≤ 2% throughput degradation when telemetry feature enabled; zero overhead when disabled.
  • Fuzz corpus: target 10 000+ seeds; validation pass must handle all without panic (only Err(SerError::*)).

4.5 Failure Budget

Failure Class Acceptable Rate Action
Validation rejection (malformed wire) Caller-defined; metric emitted Err(SerError::*) returned; no panic
Schema mismatch (SchemaHash) Caller-defined Err(SerError::IncompatibleSchema)
Arena OOM (encode) 0 in normal ops Err(SerError::OutOfBounds)
Validation panic 0 (invariant) Fuzz-enforced; any panic is a critical bug
Post-validation OOB (accessor) 0 (invariant) Violation of trust boundary = critical bug
Codec regression (throughput ±5%) 0 CI benchmark gate blocks merge
