Layout invariants:

  • All load-bearing Rust lives under crates/; nothing outside that subtree is compiled into release artifacts.
  • benchmarks/results/ committed baseline JSON → CI blocks merge on >5% regression.
  • conformance/suite/ vectors are version-stamped; adding a new wire-format version requires new vectors before CI passes.
  • fuzz/ is a separate Cargo workspace member to isolate libfuzzer-sys dep.
  • docs/adr/ is append-only; superseded ADRs are marked Superseded, never deleted.
  • papers/ and legal/ are never compiled; present for offline reference and license compliance.
  • scripts/publish.sh enforces topo-sorted crate publish order: ser-core → ser-schema → ser-layout → ser-encode → ser-decode → ser-runtime → others; prevents broken dep graph on crates.io.

11. Core Schema and Core Types

All types below are normative. ser-core re-exports all primitives; downstream crates import from there. Signatures are lightweight — no implementations, no method bodies.

11.1 Primitive Identifiers

Type Definition Ownership Mutation Concurrency Persistence Serialization Compat
TypeId struct TypeId(u32) Copy immutable after schema finalization Send+Sync stored in Schema; wire-stable serialized as u32 LE never reuse retired ids
FieldId struct FieldId(u16) Copy immutable after schema finalization Send+Sync stored in Field; wire-stable serialized as u16 LE append-only; max 65535 fields/type
Offset struct Offset(u32) Copy immutable post-layout Send+Sync stored in Layout/Field u32 LE; word-unit layout must recompute if schema changes
SegmentId struct SegmentId(u32) Copy immutable within a Message Send+Sync embedded in Far pointer u32 LE cross-segment pointer must resolve in same Message
SchemaHash struct SchemaHash(u64) Copy immutable post-hash Send+Sync transmitted in message header u64 LE FNV-128 folded; birthday bound ~2^32 schemas

Invariants:

  • TypeId(0) reserved = void/null; no user type may claim 0.
  • FieldId values are schema-scoped; same numeric id in two types are distinct fields.
  • Offset is word-indexed (multiply by 8 for byte offset); byte offsets never appear in public API.
  • SchemaHash collision → SerError::IncompatibleSchema; upgrade to 128-bit requires wire-format version bump.

11.2 WireType

pub enum WireType {
    Void,
    Bool,
    I8, I16, I32, I64,
    U8, U16, U32, U64,
    F32, F64,
    Enum,           // discriminant: u16; stored in data section
    Struct,         // pointer to struct (ptr section)
    List,           // pointer to list (ptr section)
    Union,          // discriminant u16 + active arm (data or ptr section)
    Text,           // pointer to UTF-8 bytes + NUL terminator
    Bytes,          // pointer to raw byte blob
    Pointer,        // generic untyped pointer (escape hatch; discouraged)
}

Sizing table:

WireType Data-section words Ptr-section words Notes
Void 0 0 no bits consumed
Bool 0 (bit-packed) 0 packed into nearest u8 slot
I8/U8 1/8 of a word 0 sub-word; occupies 1 byte lane
I16/U16 2/8 word 0 2-byte aligned
I32/U32/F32 4/8 word 0 4-byte aligned
I64/U64/F64 1 word 0 8-byte aligned
Enum 2/8 word 0 u16 discriminant
Struct 0 1 struct pointer in ptr section
List 0 1 list pointer in ptr section
Union 2/8 (discriminant) 0 or 1 arm may occupy data or ptr word
Text/Bytes 0 1 list pointer to byte content
Pointer 0 1 generic far/struct/list pointer

Invariants:

  • Wire endianness: little-endian for all scalar types; no platform-conditional branches.
  • Bool fields bit-pack within their byte lane in field-id order; cross-word spill not permitted.
  • Enum discriminant must reference a valid TypeId of kind Enum; unknown discriminant on read = default (evolution).
  • Pointer type is an escape hatch for dynamically-typed payloads; codegen warns on use.

11.3 DefaultVal

pub enum DefaultVal {
    Void,
    Bool(bool),
    I8(i8), I16(i16), I32(i32), I64(i64),
    U8(u8), U16(u16), U32(u32), U64(u64),
    F32(f32), F64(f64),
    Enum(u16),
    Null,           // absent pointer -> null reader
}

Invariants:

  • F32/F64 defaults: NaN not permitted (schema validator rejects); infinity permitted but warns.
  • Null default for pointer fields → reader returns empty/null view; no heap allocation.
  • Defaults are stored in Schema, not on wire; absent-field reads apply default at accessor time.
  • Default for a field may not change across schema versions (append-only rule; changing breaks old readers silently).

11.4 Layout

pub struct Layout {
    pub data_words: u16,   // size of data section in 8-byte words
    pub ptr_words:  u16,   // size of pointer section in 8-byte words
    pub align:      u8,    // required alignment in bytes (power of 2; max 8)
}

Ownership: Copy; embedded in TypeDef. Mutation: immutable after ser-layout assigns it; any schema mutation requires full recomputation. Concurrency: Send+Sync; read-only after build. Persistence: stored in compiled schema artifact (.szc); must round-trip exactly. Serialization: data_words u16 LE, ptr_words u16 LE, align u8. Compat: data_words/ptr_words may grow monotonically when fields are appended; never shrink.

Invariants:

  • Total struct size = (data_words + ptr_words) * 8 bytes; no implicit padding beyond field alignment.
  • align ≤ 8; wire format word-aligns all structs; no SIMD over-alignment in base format.
  • List element size for a struct = (data_words + ptr_words) * 8; list stride is fixed.
  • Empty struct (Void) = Layout { data_words: 0, ptr_words: 0, align: 1 }.

11.5 Field

pub struct Field {
    pub id:            FieldId,
    pub name:          Arc<str>,       // interned; schema-owned
    pub ty:            WireType,
    pub type_id:       Option<TypeId>, // Some(_) for Struct/Enum/Union/List referents
    pub offset:        Offset,         // word offset within data or ptr section
    pub default:       DefaultVal,
    pub since_version: u32,            // schema version when field was added
    pub reserved:      bool,           // true = removed field; hole preserved
    pub doc:           Option<Arc<str>>,
}

Ownership: borrowed from TypeDef; TypeDef is Arc-wrapped for shared schema access. Mutation: immutable post-schema-finalization; mutations require new schema version + hash. Concurrency: Send+Sync. Persistence: canonical field serialization: id u16, offset u32, ty u8, since_version u32. Compat: id + offset are wire-stable; name is advisory (reflective only); rename = no wire change.

Invariants:

  • reserved: true fields keep their offset/id permanently; data at that offset is zeroed on write.
  • Two fields in same type must not share offset unless one is reserved.
  • since_version must be monotonically non-decreasing within a type's field list in id order.
  • type_id must reference a TypeId present in the same Schema; forward references resolved at finalization.

11.6 TypeDef

pub struct TypeDef {
    pub id:     TypeId,
    pub name:   Arc<str>,
    pub kind:   TypeKind,
    pub fields: Vec<Field>,          // sorted by FieldId ascending
    pub layout: Layout,
    pub doc:    Option<Arc<str>>,
}

pub enum TypeKind { Struct, Enum, Union, List { elem: WireType, elem_type: Option<TypeId> } }

Ownership: Arc<TypeDef> for shared schema references across crates. Mutation: immutable after schema finalization (Schema::finalize()). Concurrency: Arc wrapping ensures Send+Sync; no interior mutability. Persistence: serialized in .szc compiled schema; TypeId is stable across versions. Compat: adding fields → new TypeDef version with same TypeId; layout grows.

Invariants:

  • fields is sorted by FieldId; binary search used for lookup; O(log n) per field.
  • kind: Enum → all fields are WireType::Void (arms carry no data; discriminant only).
  • kind: Union → discriminant field implicit (FieldId=0xFFFF, WireType::Enum); each arm is a Field.
  • kind: List → fields empty; element type encoded in TypeKind::List.
  • Recursive types (linked lists, trees) require pointer indirection; direct embedding not permitted.

11.7 Schema

pub struct Schema {
    pub types:       Vec<Arc<TypeDef>>,   // all types; index by TypeId
    pub root:        TypeId,
    pub schema_hash: SchemaHash,
    pub version:     u32,                 // monotonic schema evolution counter
    pub namespace:   Arc<str>,
}

impl Schema {
    pub fn finalize(&mut self) -> Result<(), SerError>;   // resolves refs, computes hash, freezes
    pub fn lookup(&self, id: TypeId) -> Option<&TypeDef>;
    pub fn lookup_by_name(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&TypeDef>;
}

Ownership: Arc<Schema> passed to encode/decode paths; no clone of inner data. Mutation: mutable during parse; finalize() transitions to immutable; finalize() is idempotent. Concurrency: Arc<Schema> is Send+Sync; after finalize() all access is read-only. Persistence: serialized as .szc (compiled schema binary); schema_hash authenticates wire messages. Compat: version increments on each field addition; breaking changes (remove, reorder) forbidden.

Failure modes:

  • finalize() with unresolved type_id → SerError::SchemaInvalid.
  • Duplicate TypeId in types → SerError::SchemaInvalid.
  • Cycle detection: recursive struct without pointer indirection → SerError::SchemaInvalid.
  • schema_hash collision (two distinct schemas same hash) → SerError::IncompatibleSchema at decode; mitigated by FNV-128→u64; monitor collision counter in ser-telemetry.

11.8 Pointer

pub enum Pointer {
    Null,
    Struct {
        offset:     i32,         // word offset from pointer location (signed; relative)
        data_words: u16,
        ptr_words:  u16,
    },
    List {
        offset:     i32,
        elem:       ListElemSize, // enum: Void|Bit|Byte|TwoBytes|FourBytes|EightBytes|Composite
        count:      u32,
    },
    Far {
        seg:        SegmentId,
        offset:     u32,         // word offset within target segment
        double_far: bool,        // true = landing pad is another pointer (two-hop)
    },
    Cap(u32),                   // capability index (reserved; transport out of scope)
}

pub enum ListElemSize { Void, Bit, Byte, TwoBytes, FourBytes, EightBytes, Composite }

Ownership: Copy; produced by decode validation, consumed by reader accessors. Mutation: immutable; produced once by validation pass. Concurrency: Send+Sync. Persistence: pointers are wire-format constructs; never stored independently; always relative to a buffer. Compat: Far with double_far=true is a two-hop landing pad; decoders must handle both hops; writers prefer single-hop.

Invariants:

  • Struct / List offset is relative to word after the pointer's own word; range ±2^30 words.
  • Far.offset is absolute within target segment; segment must exist in Message.segments.
  • Null pointer → accessor returns default value; no bounds check needed.
  • Cap pointer → decoded but accessor returns SerError (transport not implemented).
  • Pointer tag bits [1:0] encode type: 00=Struct, 01=List, 10=Far, 11=Cap.

11.9 Segment and Message

pub struct Segment {
    pub id:    SegmentId,
    pub bytes: Vec<u8>,   // owned; backing store for a builder-produced segment
}

impl Segment {
    pub fn as_slice(&self) -> &[u8];
    pub fn word_count(&self) -> u32;   // bytes.len() / 8; panics if not word-aligned
}

pub struct Message {
    pub segments: Vec<Segment>,   // segments[0] = root segment
    pub root:     Pointer,        // pointer into segments[0] to root struct
}

impl Message {
    pub fn segment(&self, id: SegmentId) -> Option<&Segment>;
    pub fn root_segment(&self) -> &Segment;   // segments[0]; always present
    pub fn total_bytes(&self) -> usize;
}

Ownership: Message owns Segments; Segment owns bytes (builder path); decode path uses MessageView<'a> (borrowed). Mutation: mutable during encode (builder appends segments); immutable after encode() seals the message. Concurrency: immutable Message is Send+Sync; mutable builder is !Sync (single-threaded build). Persistence: framed on wire as: u32 LE segment count, per-segment u32 LE word count, then segment bytes concatenated; 4-byte pad if segment count is even. Compat: segment count field is u32; max enforced at 512 segments (amplification bound).

Failure modes:

  • word_count() on non-word-aligned segment → panic in debug; SerError::Misaligned from validation.
  • Missing SegmentId referenced by Far pointer → SerError::BadPointer.
  • total_bytes() overflow u32 → SerError::OutOfBounds.
  • Segment count > 512 → SerError::OutOfBounds (amplification defense).

Invariants:

  • segments[0] always exists; root points into segments[0].
  • Segment bytes length always a multiple of 8 (word alignment invariant); validator checks before any cast.
  • No segment may reference another segment's interior via pointer except through Far pointers.

11.10 Reader

pub struct Reader<'a, T> {
    buf:    &'a [u8],    // borrowed slice over validated segment bytes
    base:   Offset,      // word offset of this struct's data section start
    schema: &'a TypeDef, // schema for type T (may be erased to DynReader)
    _type:  PhantomData<&'a T>,
}

impl<'a, T: Readable> Reader<'a, T> {
    // Accessor signatures (generated by ser-codegen per field):
    pub fn get_field<F: FieldAccessor>(&self, field_id: FieldId) -> F::Output<'a>;
    pub fn get_struct_field(&self, field_id: FieldId) -> Option<Reader<'a, DynStruct>>;
    pub fn get_list_field(&self, field_id: FieldId) -> ListReader<'a>;
    pub fn get_text_field(&self, field_id: FieldId) -> &'a str;
    pub fn get_bytes_field(&self, field_id: FieldId) -> &'a [u8];
    pub fn to_owned(&self) -> OwnedValue;   // explicit deep-copy (opt-in; allocates)
}

pub trait Readable: Sized { type Schema; }
pub struct DynStruct;  // type-erased reader target

Ownership: Reader<'a, T> borrows from the validated buffer; no owned data; 'a ties to buffer lifetime. Mutation: Reader is immutable; &mut Reader does not exist. Concurrency: Reader<'a, T>: Send+Sync when T: Send+Sync (no interior mutation). Persistence: not persisted; ephemeral over the validated buffer's lifetime. Serialization semantics: to_owned() is the only path to heap allocation; all other accessors are zero-copy casts. Compat: if schema evolution adds a field, old reader returns default; new reader reads the new field.

Invariants:

  • buf is always the post-validation slice; no re-validation at access time.
  • All pointer-following in accessors uses pre-validated Pointer targets; bounds already confirmed.
  • get_text_field → &'a str cast from validated UTF-8 region; UTF-8 validated during validation pass.
  • to_owned() is the sole allocation escape valve; must be explicit at call site; compile-time no implicit copies.
  • Reader created only by ser-decode validation path; no public constructor.

11.11 Builder

pub struct Builder<'arena, T> {
    arena:   &'arena mut Arena,
    seg:     SegmentId,
    base:    Offset,
    schema:  &'arena TypeDef,
    _type:   PhantomData<T>,
}

impl<'arena, T: Writable> Builder<'arena, T> {
    pub fn set_field<V: FieldValue>(&mut self, field_id: FieldId, val: V);
    pub fn init_struct(&mut self, field_id: FieldId) -> Builder<'arena, DynStruct>;
    pub fn init_list(&mut self, field_id: FieldId, count: u32) -> ListBuilder<'arena>;
    pub fn set_text(&mut self, field_id: FieldId, s: &str);
    pub fn set_bytes(&mut self, field_id: FieldId, b: &[u8]);
    pub fn finish(self) -> Message;   // only on root Builder; seals arena
}

pub struct Arena {
    segments: Vec<Vec<u8>>,
    current:  usize,
    limit:    usize,   // max total bytes; enforced on alloc
}

pub trait Writable: Sized { type Schema; }
pub struct DynStruct;

Ownership: Builder mutably borrows Arena; single active builder per arena (borrow-checked). Mutation: set_* / init_* write into arena segments; no undo. Concurrency: !Sync; single-threaded build pattern enforced by &mut Arena. Persistence: finish() produces a Message with owned Segments; Arena is consumed. Serialization: emits little-endian data words + pointer words; pointer offsets computed at init_* time. Compat: builder for schema version N may omit optional fields (appear as zeroed → default on read).

Invariants:

  • Arena.limit enforced per alloc(); exceeding limit → SerError::OutOfBounds.
  • Nested init_struct returns child builder borrowing same arena; parent pointer written after child finish.
  • set_field on a reserved field id is silently ignored (zeroed).
  • finish() on non-root builder is a compile error (only root Builder exposes finish()).
  • All segments in Arena are word-aligned; Arena::alloc() panics on non-word-aligned request (internal invariant).

11.12 SerError

pub enum SerError {
    SchemaInvalid { reason: Cow<'static, str> },
    OutOfBounds   { offset: u64, limit: u64 },
    Misaligned    { offset: u64, required_align: u8 },
    BadPointer    { kind: PointerKind, seg: Option<SegmentId>, offset: u64 },
    DepthExceeded { depth: u32, limit: u32 },
    Truncated     { expected: u64, found: u64 },
    IncompatibleSchema { expected: SchemaHash, found: SchemaHash },
    Io(std::io::Error),
}

pub enum PointerKind { Struct, List, Far, Cap }

pub enum SchemaError {
    ParseError    { span: Span, message: Cow<'static, str> },
    DuplicateName { span: Span, name: Arc<str> },
    DuplicateFieldId { type_id: TypeId, field_id: FieldId },
    UnresolvedType { span: Span, name: Arc<str> },
    CyclicEmbedding { type_id: TypeId },
    DuplicateDiscriminant { type_id: TypeId, value: u16 },
    NestingTooDeep { span: Span, depth: u32 },
    InvalidDefault { span: Span, field_id: FieldId },
    InvalidUnion  { type_id: TypeId },
    InvalidEmbedding { type_id: TypeId, field_id: FieldId },
}

pub struct Span { pub line: u32, pub col: u32 }

Ownership: Clone; cheaply copyable except Io variant. Concurrency: Send+Sync; Io variant wraps Arc<std::io::Error> internally. Persistence: not persisted; ephemeral diagnostic; telemetry counters record variant frequencies. Compat: new variants may be added in minor versions; callers must use #[non_exhaustive] match arms.

Invariants per variant:

  • SchemaInvalid → emitted only by ser-schema finalization or ser-evolve compat check; never by decode path.
  • OutOfBounds → emitted by validation pass only; accessor path never OOBs post-validation.
  • Misaligned → emitted if segment byte length not multiple of 8, or pointer target not word-aligned.
  • BadPointer → emitted if Far pointer references missing segment, or pointer tag bits invalid.
  • DepthExceeded → emitted if recursion counter exceeds DecodeConfig.max_depth (default 64).
  • Truncated → emitted if framing header claims more segment bytes than buffer contains.
  • IncompatibleSchema → emitted if message SchemaHash mismatches decoder's loaded schema hash.
  • Io → emitted by framing read/write only; not by in-memory encode/decode.

SchemaError (Phase 1, ser-schema): distinct from SerError — raised by parser.rs/resolver.rs/validator.rs while building a Schema from .szs source, never by the encode/decode hot path. Every variant carries a Span (line/column) from lexer.rs's token stream except the two that are purely structural (CyclicEmbedding, DuplicateFieldId, InvalidUnion, InvalidEmbedding, DuplicateDiscriminant), which carry the offending TypeId/FieldId instead. ser-tool (Phase 9) renders SchemaError with file + line + column for human consumption.

11.13 Validation and Decode Configuration

pub struct DecodeConfig {
    pub max_depth:        u32,   // default 64; recursion + pointer-follow limit
    pub max_segments:     u32,   // default 512; amplification defense
    pub max_message_size: u64,   // default 64 MiB; total bytes across all segments
    pub schema:           Arc<Schema>,
    pub strict_utf8:      bool,  // default true; Text fields validated as UTF-8
}

Ownership: Clone; cheaply shared across concurrent decode invocations. Concurrency: Send+Sync; immutable after construction. Invariants:

  • max_depth = 0 is rejected by DecodeConfig::new() → SerError::SchemaInvalid.
  • max_message_size checked before validation pass begins; early-reject oversized messages.
  • strict_utf8 = false allows binary data in Text fields; accessor returns &[u8] not &str in that mode.

11.14 Evolution Types (ser-evolve)

pub struct CompatReport {
    pub result:    CompatResult,
    pub violations: Vec<CompatViolation>,
}

pub enum CompatResult { FullyCompatible, BackwardOnly, ForwardOnly, Incompatible }

pub struct CompatViolation {
    pub kind:    ViolationKind,
    pub type_id: TypeId,
    pub field_id: Option<FieldId>,
    pub message: Cow<'static, str>,
}

pub enum ViolationKind {
    FieldRemoved,
    FieldTypeChanged,
    FieldOffsetChanged,
    FieldDefaultChanged,
    TypeKindChanged,
    SchemaHashMismatch,
}

Ownership: CompatReport is owned; Vec<CompatViolation> heap-allocated (diagnostic path only; not on hot path). Concurrency: Send+Sync; produced by ser-evolve::check(old: &Schema, new: &Schema) -> CompatReport. Persistence: may be stored in schema registry alongside schema versions for audit. Compat: new ViolationKind variants added in minor versions; #[non_exhaustive].

Invariants:

  • CompatResult::FullyCompatible → violations is empty.
  • FieldOffsetChanged is always fatal (wire incompatible); cannot be overridden.
  • FieldRemoved → CompatResult::Incompatible; removed fields must be replaced with reserved: true holes.
  • FieldDefaultChanged → CompatResult::BackwardOnly (old readers see old default; new readers see new default).

11.15 Telemetry Handles (ser-telemetry)

pub struct TelemetryHandle {
    pub encode_bytes:        Counter,
    pub encode_duration_ns:  Histogram,
    pub decode_bytes:        Counter,
    pub decode_duration_ns:  Histogram,
    pub validate_duration_ns: Histogram,
    pub validation_errors:   Counter,  // per SerError variant label
    pub segment_allocs:      Counter,
}

pub trait MetricsSink: Send + Sync + 'static {
    fn record_counter(&self, name: &'static str, delta: u64, labels: &[(&str, &str)]);
    fn record_histogram(&self, name: &'static str, value_ns: u64, labels: &[(&str, &str)]);
}

Ownership: Arc<TelemetryHandle> shared across encode/decode call sites; registered at runtime init. Mutation: lock-free atomic counters; histogram uses hdrhistogram (no lock on hot path). Concurrency: Send+Sync; designed for concurrent encode/decode callers. Persistence: not persisted by library; exported via MetricsSink impl (Prometheus, OTLP, etc.). Compat: new fields on TelemetryHandle are additive; MetricsSink is a trait (implementer-stable).

Invariants:

  • encode_duration_ns + decode_duration_ns measured with std::time::Instant; monotonic.
  • validation_errors labeled by SerError variant name string; enables per-error-type alerting.
  • No telemetry path allocates on the encode/decode critical path; label strings are 'static.
  • TelemetryHandle::noop() returns a handle backed by atomic no-ops; default if no sink registered.

12. Phase 0 — Skeleton — crates, core types, a hand-coded fixed struct: build into a byte buffer and read fields back via a Reader cast (no schema, no pointers, single segment)

0.1 Objectives

  • Establish workspace Cargo.toml with all 12 crates as members; each crate compiles with zero warnings on MSRV 1.78 (edition 2021).
  • Define the complete canonical type lattice in ser-core: TypeId, FieldId, Offset, SegmentId, SchemaHash, WireType, Pointer, Layout, Field, TypeDef, Schema, Message, Segment, SerError; all #[repr(C)] or #[repr(u8)] as required.
  • Define Reader<'a, T> and Builder<'a, T> marker-generic shells in ser-core; borrow-checked lifetime anchor established.
  • Prove the zero-copy read model works end-to-end on a hand-coded 2-field struct (one u32, one i64) without schema, pointers, or multiple segments: encode into a raw Vec<u8> via manual offset arithmetic, validate with trivial bounds check, read back via pointer cast.
  • Scope constraints: NO schema parsing, NO codegen, NO layout computer, NO pointer types, NO multi-segment messages; single data-section struct at offset 0; all alignment correctness verified by test.
  • Architectural invariant introduced: Reader<'a, T> holds buf: &'a [u8] and base: Offset; every field access is buf[base + field_offset..][..size] cast — no copy, no alloc.

0.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P1 (binary-native), P3 (arena allocation), P14 (MSRV discipline), ADR-01 (Rust), ADR-02 (Cargo workspace), ADR-17 (unsafe policy); B1 §1 (systems programming mindset), B2 §2 (memory layout fundamentals).

Workspace (Cargo.toml root):

  • [workspace] members = ["ser-core","ser-schema","ser-layout","ser-encode","ser-decode","ser-runtime","ser-codegen","ser-evolve","ser-tool","ser-telemetry","ser-cli","ser-bench"]
  • resolver = "2", edition = "2021", rust-version = "1.78"
  • [workspace.dependencies]: thiserror = "1", byteorder = "1", tracing = "0.1", criterion = "0.5", proptest = "1", serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"], optional = true }

ser-core/src/lib.rs:

  • Module tree: types, error, layout, schema, message, reader, builder
  • types.rs: TypeId(u32), FieldId(u16), Offset(u32), SegmentId(u32), SchemaHash(u64) — all #[derive(Copy,Clone,Debug,PartialEq,Eq,Hash,PartialOrd,Ord)]
  • error.rs: SerError enum with thiserror — variants SchemaInvalid, OutOfBounds, Misaligned, BadPointer, DepthExceeded, Truncated, IncompatibleSchema; each variant carries a &'static str context
  • layout.rs: Layout { data_words: u16, ptr_words: u16, align: u8 } — word_count() -> u32, byte_size() -> usize
  • wire_type.rs: WireType — #[repr(u8)] enum with all 16 variants; wire_size(wt: WireType) -> Option<usize> returns Some(n) for scalar types, None for pointer/composite types
  • schema.rs: Field, TypeDef, Schema — all derive Clone, Debug; DefaultVal enum covering scalar/zero/none
  • message.rs: Segment { id: SegmentId, bytes: Vec<u8> }, Message { segments: Vec<Segment>, root: Pointer }
  • pointer.rs: Pointer enum { Struct{offset:Offset,data_words:u16,ptr_words:u16}, List{offset:Offset,elem:WireType,count:u32}, Far{seg:SegmentId,offset:Offset} } — #[derive(Copy,Clone,Debug,PartialEq,Eq)]
  • reader.rs: Reader<'a, T> { buf: &'a [u8], base: Offset, _ty: PhantomData<T> } — constructor unsafe fn from_raw(buf: &'a [u8], base: Offset) -> Self; safe helper fn read_u8_at(&self, off: usize) -> u8
  • builder.rs: Builder<T> { arena: Vec<u8>, _ty: PhantomData<T> } — fn alloc_words(n: u16) -> Offset, fn write_u32_le(off: Offset, v: u32), fn write_i64_le(off: Offset, v: i64), fn finish() -> Vec<u8>

Phase-0 integration test (ser-core/tests/phase0_skeleton.rs):

struct FooFixed { x: u32 @ offset 0..4, y: i64 @ offset 8..16 }
// 4-byte pad at offset 4..8 (align 8)
// Builder: alloc 16 bytes, write_u32_le(0, 42), write_i64_le(8, -7)
// Reader: from_raw(&buf, Offset(0)), read_u32_le(0) == 42, read_i64_le(8) == -7
// bounds check: buf.len() == 16, no OOB possible
  • All unsafe confined to reader.rs::from_raw and reader.rs::read_*_le; every call-site validated by explicit length assertion before cast.
  • ser-decode stub: fn validate_single_segment(buf: &[u8], expected_bytes: usize) -> Result<(), SerError> — checks buf.len() >= expected_bytes else SerError::Truncated.

Other crates (stubs): Each crate gets a lib.rs with pub fn placeholder() {} and a doc comment summarizing its future role; all compile, zero warnings.

0.3 Deliverable

  • cargo build --workspace succeeds on MSRV 1.78, zero warnings, zero errors.
  • cargo test --package ser-core runs phase0_skeleton test: encode FooFixed, decode, assert field values.
  • cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings passes clean.
  • All 12 crate stubs present and addressable as library dependencies.
  • Inspection: cargo doc --workspace --no-deps generates docs with no broken [doc] links.

0.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo build --workspace Green, MSRV 1.78
phase0_skeleton integration test Pass: x==42, y==-7
Clippy -D warnings Zero warnings
SerError variants All 7 defined, thiserror messages compile
WireType coverage All 16 variants, wire_size returns correct scalar sizes
Reader borrow check Compile error if 'a outlives buf (static test)
Unsafe block count in ser-core ≤ 4 (from_raw + 3 read_*_le helpers)
cargo test --workspace No test fails; stubs produce 0 test failures

0.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture File Purpose Expected output
foo_fixed.bin ser-core/tests/fixtures/ Hand-crafted 16-byte LE encoding of FooFixed{x=42,y=-7} Hex: 2a000000 00000000 f9ffffffffffffff
foo_fixed_truncated.bin same 12 bytes (truncated) validate_single_segment → SerError::Truncated
foo_fixed_zeros.bin same 16 zero bytes x==0, y==0 (default read)
wire_type_sizes.txt ser-core/tests/fixtures/ Expected wire_size output per WireType Tabular, compared in unit test

Malformed: foo_fixed_truncated.bin must return Truncated, not panic. Replay: foo_fixed.bin decoded twice from same &[u8] must yield identical values (idempotent read). Regression: if Offset arithmetic changes, hex fixture detects it.

0.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug / signal Recovery
Alignment UB in cast MIRI undefined behavior on read_u32_le buf.as_ptr().add(off) not 4-aligned, casting to *const u32 Run cargo miri test; misaligned access trap Use u32::from_le_bytes(buf[off..off+4].try_into().unwrap()) — no raw pointer cast needed
Padding mismatch y reads wrong value Assumed offset 4 for i64 but i64 needs align-8, so offset is 8 not 4 Assert offset_of! matches fixture hex Fix offset in builder + reader to 8; add explicit padding bytes
PhantomData<T> variance Compile error: lifetime not tied to T PhantomData<T> is invariant in T Rustc error message Use PhantomData<fn() -> T> or PhantomData<*const T> depending on variance need
Vec<u8> reallocate invalidates slice Dangling ref if Builder returned &[u8] before finish() push may realloc Rustc borrow checker catches; ensure finish() consumes Call finish() before taking any slice reference
LE/BE confusion x==0x2a000000 (BE) instead of 42 Missing to_le_bytes() Hex-dump fixture comparison Enforce _le suffix on all write/read helpers; CI checks fixture
Stub crates break workspace build Compile error on unused import Empty lib.rs with #[allow(unused)] missing cargo build --workspace output Add #[allow(dead_code, unused)] to each stub

0.7 Performance Concerns

  • Phase 0 is baseline; no hot paths yet. Relevant concerns to establish early:
  • Builder backs a Vec<u8>; initial capacity should be Layout::byte_size() to avoid realloc — set in constructor.
  • read_*_le uses u32::from_le_bytes([buf[b], buf[b+1], buf[b+2], buf[b+3]]) — compiles to a single mov on x86-64; verify with cargo asm.
  • Zero-copy read: Reader is 2 words (*const u8 + usize + u32) on stack; no heap alloc on read path — assert with std::mem::size_of::<Reader<FooFixed>>() <= 24.
  • PhantomData is zero-size; verify size_of::<Reader<()>>() == size_of::<(&[u8], Offset)>().

0.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
buf.len() == 0 validate_single_segment returns Truncated immediately
Offset overflow (u32::MAX + field_size) Checked add in read_*_at; return OutOfBounds
expected_bytes == 0 Allowed; Reader with zero-size struct is valid
WireType::Void wire_size returns Some(0); read returns ()
Multiple calls to Builder::finish() finish() consumes self; compiler enforces single-call
FieldId collision (duplicate id=0) Not enforced in Phase 0 (schema not present); flagged in Phase 1

0.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
UB in from_raw (misalign) MIRI in CI Memory corruption on read All reads from that buffer Fix to from_le_bytes; no raw ptr cast
Workspace does not compile CI gate blocks all phases All downstream phases Stub each crate's lib.rs with pub fn placeholder(){} Fix compile error; re-run CI

0.10 Operational Notes

  • No runtime metrics in Phase 0; ser-telemetry is a stub.
  • CI matrix: stable (MSRV 1.78) + nightly (for MIRI); cargo miri test --package ser-core mandatory.
  • Fixture files committed under ser-core/tests/fixtures/; checked in as binary blobs with git-lfs or raw bytes.
  • cargo doc --workspace output reviewed for missing docs on all public types.
  • Rollback: Phase 0 only adds stubs + core types; reverting is git revert; no persistent state.
  • Migration: all downstream phases import ser-core; any type rename here cascades — treat ser-core types as a public ABI from Phase 1 onward.

13. Phase 1 — Schema / IDL front-end — parse a .szs IDL (struct/enum/union/list), type model, name/id resolution, schema hashing, validation of the schema itself

1.1 Objectives

  • Implement ser-schema: full IDL parser for .szs files producing a validated Schema value.
  • Grammar coverage: struct, enum, union, list type kinds; scalar fields (WireType primitives), nested struct/list/pointer fields, union arms, enum variants; field @id(N) annotations, @since(V) evolution annotations, @default(expr) for scalars.
  • Name resolution: string names → TypeId / FieldId; detect duplicate names, duplicate IDs, forward references (allowed; resolved in second pass).
  • Schema validation: no cyclic non-pointer struct embedding, all referenced TypeIds defined, all FieldId values unique within a type, since_version monotone within a type, enum discriminants fit u16.
  • Schema hashing: SchemaHash(u64) — deterministic FNV-1a or SipHash-1-3 over the canonical binary serialization of Schema; identical schemas on different machines → identical hash.
  • Scope constraints: no layout computation (Phase 2), no code generation (Phase 3), no evolution diffing (Phase 8); schema is parsed and validated only.
  • Architectural invariant introduced: Schema is immutable after parse_and_validate(); all downstream crates hold Arc<Schema> references; schema hash is a content-addressed identity.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P2 (schema-first), P13 (deterministic schema hash), P15 (stability), ADR-07 (custom IDL), ADR-08 (xxHash3 SchemaHash); B5 §3 (language design), P11 (Cap'n Proto security advisory — validates canonical encoding requirement).

Module tree (ser-schema/src/): lexer.rs (hand-written tokenizer; no external grammar-generator dependency, per ADR-07), parser.rs (recursive-descent parser producing an unresolved AST — raw string names, literal offsets), model.rs (unresolved AST → Schema conversion types, incl. RawSchema), resolver.rs (name→TypeId/FieldId, forward-ref resolution), validator.rs (cycle/uniqueness/version-monotone checks), hasher.rs (canonical Schema→bytes→SchemaHash); SchemaError (parse/resolve/validate variants carrying line/column span info) is declared in ser-core::error alongside SerError (§11.12).

Grammar (.szs IDL, EBNF):

file ::= type_def*                                              type_def ::= struct_def|enum_def|union_def|list_def
struct_def ::= "struct" IDENT "@id""("u32")" "{" field* "}"     field ::= IDENT ":" type_ref "@id""("u16")" ann* ";"
ann ::= "@default""("literal")" | "@since""("u32")"             type_ref ::= scalar_ty|IDENT|"List""<"type_ref">"|"Bytes"|"Text"
scalar_ty ::= "u8"|"u16"|"u32"|"u64"|"i8"|"i16"|"i32"|"i64"|"f32"|"f64"|"bool"|"void"
enum_def ::= "enum" IDENT "@id""("u32")" "{" variant* "}"        variant ::= IDENT "=" u16 ";"
union_def ::= "union" IDENT "@id""("u32")" "{" arm* "}"          arm ::= IDENT ":" type_ref "@id""("u16")" ";"
list_def ::= "list" IDENT "@id""("u32")" "=" "List""<"type_ref">" ";"

Parser: lexer.rs emits a Token stream (single left-to-right scan, no backtracking); parser.rs consumes it via recursive descent (one function per grammar production in §8.1, one token of lookahead) and produces model::RawSchema (RawTypeDef{raw_id,name,kind}, RawField{raw_id,name,type_name,default,since}).

Resolution: resolver.rs runs two passes — pass 1 collects (name,TypeId) into a HashMap (duplicate → DuplicateName); pass 2 resolves each RawField.type_name via the map (unresolved → UnresolvedType), then builds Field{id,name,ty,offset:Offset(0)/*placeholder*/,default,since_version}, TypeDef{id,name,kind,fields,layout:Layout{0,0,0}/*placeholder*/}, and Schema{types,root,schema_hash:SchemaHash(0)/*filled by hasher.rs*/}.

Validation (validator.rs): struct-embedding cycles via DFS excluding pointer fields (CyclicEmbedding); per-type FieldId uniqueness (DuplicateFieldId); since_version monotone (field[i].since_version >= field[i-1].since_version in field-id order); enum discriminants fit u16 with no in-enum duplicates; TypeIds fit u32 with no schema-wide duplicates.

Hashing (hasher.rs): fn schema_hash(s: &Schema) -> SchemaHash serializes the schema to a canonical byte sequence — types sorted by TypeId, fields sorted by FieldId, strings length-prefixed, integers LE, never raw HashMap iteration order — then applies SipHash-1-3 keyed with a fixed zero seed [0u8;16] to a u64.

Test harness (ser-schema/tests/): parse_struct.rs/parse_enum.rs/parse_union.rs (grammar round-trips: type/field counts, discriminant extraction, union arm + tag presence), resolve_forward_ref.rs (struct A references B defined later in file), validate_cycle.rs (cyclic embedding → CyclicEmbedding), hash_determinism.rs (same schema, different parse order → identical hash), and a proptest over random valid struct schemas asserting parse_and_validate never panics.

1.3 Deliverable

  • ser-schema crate compiles; parse_and_validate(idl: &str) -> Result<Schema, SchemaError> is the public API entry point.
  • All 7 test files pass: struct, enum, union, list parsing; name resolution; cycle detection; hash determinism.
  • ser-schema binary (examples/parse_idl.rs): reads .szs from stdin, prints Schema debug + SchemaHash hex to stdout.
  • Forward references resolve without reordering the input file.
  • SchemaHash stable across debug/release builds on x86-64 and aarch64.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Parse struct / enum / union / list 4 grammar tests pass
Forward-ref resolution test Pass
Cyclic embedding detection CyclicEmbedding error returned, not panic
Duplicate FieldId within type DuplicateFieldId error
schema_hash stable Same hash on 3 re-parses of identical text
Proptest (1000 cases) No panics in parse_and_validate
Parse latency (100 types, 10 fields each) < 5 ms on dev laptop
cargo clippy -D warnings Clean

Fixture File Purpose Expected

1.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug / signal Recovery
HashMap iteration order in hash Different SchemaHash across runs Non-deterministic iteration hash_determinism test fails intermittently Sort by TypeId/FieldId before hashing; never hash from HashMap directly
Cyclic detection misses pointer-wrapped cycles CyclicEmbedding not raised for A { b: *B } where B contains A Pointer fields must be excluded from embedding graph Add test: pointer-wrapped cycle is VALID Skip WireType::Pointer/Struct pointer fields in DFS
Recursive-descent grammar left-recursion Stack overflow at parse time Hand-written descent functions disallow left recursion (same constraint as any top-down grammar) Crash on large files Restructure grammar production to right-recursive; track loop-based repetition instead of self-call for type_def*/field*
since_version monotone check uses insertion order False negatives Fields may be parsed in IDL order, not FieldId order Sort by FieldId before check Always sort fields by FieldId before monotone validation
TypeId(0) reserved / collision Root type inadvertently has id=0 conflicting with "null" sentinel No sentinel reserved Explicit test: TypeId(0) is valid user ID — or reserve it; decide now Reserve TypeId(u32::MAX) as invalid sentinel; document in ser-core
Large schema parse time > 50 ms for 1000-type schema lexer.rs/parser.rs allocate one Token/RawTypeDef per production; quadratic if union-arm scanning re-tokenizes Benchmark with 1000-type schema Pre-allocate model::RawSchema with capacity hint; single-pass tokenizer (no re-lex)
SchemaError loses span info Debugging parse failures hard Error constructed without Span Observe useless error message Carry a (line, col) Span (from lexer.rs's token stream) in every SchemaError variant

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Parser input: .szs files typically < 100 KB; parse is one-shot at startup, not hot path.
  • lexer.rs/parser.rs allocate Tokens and RawTypeDefs on the heap; for 100-type schema, expect < 1 MB allocation — acceptable.
  • schema_hash: iterating + hashing Schema is O(types * fields); for 1000 types × 20 fields = 20 K iterations — < 1 ms.
  • Arc<Schema> cloning is O(1) (atomic ref-count); all downstream crates share single allocation.
  • Forward-ref resolution: two-pass over Vec<RawTypeDef> — O(N); HashMap lookup O(1) amortized.
  • Avoid allocating String per field name in hot decode path — names are for debugging only; consider Arc<str> interning if schema is large.

1.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
Empty .szs file Schema { types: vec![], root: TypeId(0) } — valid; root unresolvable → caller error
Struct with zero fields Valid; Layout { data_words:0, ptr_words:0, align:1 }
@since(0) on all fields Valid; baseline version
FieldId value u16::MAX Valid; treated as any other ID
TypeId value u32::MAX Reserved as invalid sentinel; parser rejects
Unicode in identifiers Reject; ASCII identifiers only; lexer.rs IDENT rule accepts [A-Za-z0-9_]+ only
Extremely deep list nesting (List<List<List<...>>>) parser.rs tracks an explicit nesting counter (mirrors MAX_NESTING_DEPTH, I6); exceeding it emits SchemaError::NestingTooDeep rather than recursing unbounded
Duplicate struct names (different TypeId) SchemaError::DuplicateName
@default on pointer/struct field Reject; default only valid for scalar WireTypes; emit SchemaError::InvalidDefault

1.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Grammar regression (invalid .szs accepted) Fixture bad_token.szs test fails Wrong Schema built; downstream layout/encode silently wrong CI blocks release Fix grammar rule; add regression fixture
Hash collision (two different schemas, same hash) Probabilistic; no test can guarantee Incorrect schema-compat check in ser-evolve Cross-check hash + full structural equality Use 64-bit SipHash; collision probability 2^-64; accept as acceptable risk; document
Cycle-detection bug (false negative) Infinite loop / stack overflow in ser-layout DFS Root cause: layout computer recurses on schema graph Stack overflow in Phase 2 layout test Fix DFS; add test for all cycle topologies
Arc<Schema> refcount leak Memory grows without bound in long-running service Circular Arc references between schema and types Arc::weak_count metric; ser-telemetry schema_arc_count gauge Break cycle with Weak<Schema> where needed
Incorrect SchemaHash due to non-canonical serialization Hash changes after refactor; stored hashes become invalid Schema registry mismatches; IncompatibleSchema errors hash_canonical.szs regression test Pin serialization format; bump schema-hash version if format changes

1.10 Operational Notes

  • ser-telemetry hook: schema_parse_duration_us histogram; schema_type_count gauge; schema_hash logged at INFO on successful parse.
  • tracing::info!(schema_hash=?h, type_count=n, "schema loaded") in parse_and_validate.
  • Schema files are static assets; no hot-reload in Phase 1; add file-watch in Phase 8+.
  • Deployment: schema .szs files ship alongside binary; mismatch between compiled schema hash and runtime schema hash → startup rejection with IncompatibleSchema.
  • No migration needed for Phase 1 (schema is pure input); Phase 8 (evolution) handles schema version migration.
  • ser-tool in Phase 9 will call parse_and_validate for inspector; ensure SchemaError messages are human-readable with file + line + column.

14. Phase 2 — Layout computation — assign field offsets, data-section vs pointer-section split, alignment/padding, list + union layout, deterministic layout-from-schema

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement ser-layout: fn compute_layout(schema: &Schema) -> Result<Schema, SerError> — returns a new Schema with all TypeDef::layout and Field::offset fields populated.
  • Layout rules (normative): data section holds all scalar fields + union discriminant + bool fields (byte-aligned, not bit-packed — documented simplicity choice); pointer section holds all non-scalar fields (WireType::Struct, List, Text, Bytes, Pointer), appended after the data section; word = 8 bytes, data section padded to word boundary, pointer section always word-aligned (1 pointer = 1 word); data fields sorted by descending alignment to minimize padding, pointer fields assigned sequentially; union discriminant (u16) occupies the first 2 bytes with arms sharing an overlay sized to the largest arm; list element stride = wire_size(elem).unwrap_or(8), total bytes = stride * count; Layout{data_words,ptr_words,align} set per TypeDef, Field::offset set as byte offset (scalars) or word index (pointers).
  • Determinism invariant: compute_layout is a pure function of Schema; same schema bytes → byte-identical Layout on every platform; no randomness, no hash-map iteration order dependency.
  • Scope constraints: no encoding (Phase 3), no decoding (Phase 4); layout only; layout must be stable once set (no recompute during encode/decode).

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P4 (word-aligned layout), P5 (canonical encoding), I2 (layout determinism), ADR-04 (little-endian), ADR-05 (8-byte word); B2 §4 (struct packing and alignment), R1 (Cap'n Proto — struct layout specification).

Module tree (ser-layout/src/): compute.rs (top-level compute_layout(), recursive per-TypeDef), scalar.rs (wire_align/wire_size_scalar tables), struct_lay.rs (data-section packing, pointer-section assignment), union_lay.rs (discriminant + overlay layout), list_lay.rs (element stride, list Layout), validate.rs (post-layout invariant checks), error.rs (LayoutError wrapping SerError).

Scalar tables (scalar.rs): wire_align: Void→1, Bool/I8/U8→1, I16/U16→2, I32/U32/F32→4, I64/U64/F64→8; wire_size_scalar equals alignment for every primitive.

Struct layout algorithm (struct_lay.rs): partition fields into data_fields (scalar) / ptr_fields (composite); stable-sort data_fields by wire_align descending, ties broken by FieldId ascending (determinism); walk sorted fields assigning offset = align_up(cursor, wire_align(ty)), advance cursor += wire_size_scalar(ty); pad data_section_bytes to the 8-byte word boundary, data_words = bytes/8; walk ptr_fields in FieldId order assigning sequential word indices, ptr_words = ptr_fields.len(); align = max(data-field aligns, 8 if ptr_words>0 else 1); set typedef.layout = Layout{data_words,ptr_words,align}.

Union layout (union_lay.rs): discriminant u16 at offset 0 (size 2, align 2); each arm's data size is computed as a sub-struct, overlay_bytes = max(arm sizes), cursor = align_up(2, max_arm_align); total data section = discriminant + padding + overlay, padded to word boundary; ptr_words = max(arm ptr_words); arm field offsets are stored relative to the overlay start, never the struct start.

List layout (list_lay.rs): list_stride(elem, schema) -> u32 — scalar → wire_size_scalar(elem); Struct → layout.data_words*8 + layout.ptr_words*8; Pointer → 8 (1 word). The List TypeDef itself gets Layout{data_words:0,ptr_words:1,align:8} — addressed via pointer; element layout stored separately.

Top-level (compute.rs): topological sort of TypeDefs by embedding dependency (non-pointer struct fields define edges; pointer fields do not — Phase 1 already verified no embedding cycles); process leaves-first so nested struct sizes are known before the container; fn compute_layout(schema: &Schema) -> Result<Schema, SerError> clones Schema, mutates the clone's TypeDef layouts and field offsets, returns it; an embedded struct's data_words inline into the parent's data section, with the embedded fields' offsets relative to parent base + embedding offset.

Post-layout validation (validate.rs): every field.offset within its typedef.layout bounds; no two data-section fields overlap (offset + size <= next field offset); data_words*8 fits u32, ptr_words fits u16; union discriminant always at offset 0.

Test harness (ser-layout/tests/): basic_struct_layout.rs (3-field struct u8/u32/u64 — sorted placement, padding, data_words), pointer_section.rs (1 scalar + 1 nested struct → ptr_words==1), union_layout.rs (2-arm union — discriminant@0, overlay=max arm), list_layout.rs (List<u32> stride==4, List<Struct> stride=struct data size), determinism.rs (byte-compare two compute_layout runs on the same schema), padding_minimal.rs (sorted-by-align vs naïve insertion-order padding), and a proptest over random acyclic schemas asserting compute_layout never panics and all offsets stay in-bounds.

2.3 Deliverable

  • ser-layout crate compiles; compute_layout(schema: &Schema) -> Result<Schema, SerError> is public API.
  • All 7 test files pass.
  • ser-layout + ser-schema integration: parse .szs → compute_layout → print field offsets; examples/show_layout.rs.
  • Layout of any schema is reproducible across platforms: CI runs on x86-64 and aarch64 with identical fixture assertions.
  • ser-encode and ser-decode stubs updated to accept Arc<Schema> with populated layouts (no-op bodies).

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
basic_struct_layout u8@0, u32@4, u64@8, data_words=2, padding=3 bytes at offsets 1-3
pointer_section ptr_words==1, pointer field offset==Offset(0) (word index)
union_layout Discriminant at byte 0; overlay starts at byte 2 (or padded per align)
determinism test Bit-identical Layout on two runs
padding_minimal Sorted layout ≤ bytes vs insertion-order naïve layout
Proptest (500 cases) Zero panics; all offsets in-bounds
compute_layout latency (100-type schema) < 2 ms
