2.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug / signal Recovery
Non-deterministic field sort Layout differs across runs sort_by not stable on equal-align fields; tie-broken by HashMap order determinism test fails intermittently Stable sort; tie-break by FieldId ascending
Pointer field given byte offset instead of word index Decode pointer section misaligned Pointer section uses word indices, not byte offsets Fixture .layout mismatch field.offset for pointer fields = word index (0,1,2…); document in Field docstring
Union arm offsets relative to struct vs arm Wrong byte read in decode Arm offsets must be relative to overlay start, not struct start Decode reads wrong bytes for union arm Store arm_base_offset separately; add per-arm offset fixture
Embedded struct not topo-sorted data_words of inner struct unknown when computing outer Compute inner before outer Panic / wrong data_words=0 in outer DFS topo sort; process leaves first
Alignment of union overlay ignored Overlay starts at odd byte for u32 arm Max arm alignment not applied to overlay cursor Union fixture data_words wrong Compute overlay_align = max(arm field aligns); cursor = align_up(2, overlay_align)
data_words overflow (u16) u16::MAX words = 512 KB data section — legitimate limit Struct too large compute_layout panics on cast checked cast; return SerError::SchemaInvalid("struct too large")
Pointer field in embedded struct counted twice ptr_words double-counted Inline embedding logic copies ptr fields into parent's ptr section Layout test: ptr_words too large Only pointer fields of the TOP-LEVEL struct go in its pointer section; embedded struct pointers NOT inlined — embedding copies data section bytes only; pointer to embedded-with-pointers struct must use a Pointer field

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • compute_layout is startup-only (once per schema load); latency budget 2 ms for 100-type schema.
  • Topo sort: Kahn's algorithm, O(V+E); for 1000 types × avg 5 embedding edges = 5000 edges — negligible.
  • stable_sort on data_fields: O(N log N) per struct; N ≤ ~100 fields typical — negligible.
  • Clone of Schema in compute_layout: deep clone of all TypeDef + Field vecs; for 1000 types × 20 fields = 20 K Field clones — < 1 ms.
  • No locks; compute_layout is pure; result cached in Arc<Schema> after first call.
  • Cache locality: field offset table is small (≤ 1 KB for typical struct); fits in L1 cache during encode/decode field lookup.
  • Avoid per-field HashMap lookup during layout; use sorted Vec + binary search for type lookups.

2.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
Struct with 0 data fields, N pointer fields data_words=0, ptr_words=N; valid
Struct with 0 fields total Layout{0,0,1}; valid; zero-byte data section
Union with 1 arm Valid; discriminant + arm; treated as degenerate union
Union with 0 arms SchemaError::InvalidUnion from Phase 1; should not reach Phase 2
Embedded struct with pointer fields Reject embedding (pointer fields cannot be inlined); Phase 1 or Phase 2 emits SchemaError::InvalidEmbedding
List<List<T>> List element is Pointer type; stride=8 (pointer word); outer List layout normal
data_words exactly u16::MAX Checked; SerError::SchemaInvalid
Bool fields Byte-aligned (1 byte each); not bit-packed in Phase 2 (document as future optimization)
F32 / F64 fields wire_align(F32)=4, wire_align(F64)=8; handled same as U32/U64
Multiple structs with identical field layout Each gets independent Layout computation; may happen to be equal — no sharing required

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Wrong data_words for a type Fixture .layout mismatch All encode/decode for that type corrupted; field reads return wrong bytes Phase 2 test suite; fixture regression Fix sort/padding logic; re-derive fixture
Non-deterministic layout in production Wire incompatibility between encoder/decoder built from different runs Any message exchange Cross-arch CI; determinism test Fix tie-breaking; redeploy both sides
Topo sort cycle not caught (Phase 1 bug missed it) compute_layout infinite loop or stack overflow Hangs Phase 2 startup Timeout in CI (30 s limit) Add iteration count limit in topo sort; emit SerError::SchemaInvalid on cycle detection in Phase 2 as belt-and-suspenders
ptr_words miscounted Far-pointer segment overflow or missing pointer slot Encoding writes off-end of pointer section Phase 3 encode test (OOB write) Post-layout invariant check in validate.rs
Layout of evolved schema (new field added) incompatible Old readers crash on new layout Schema evolution violation ser-evolve Phase 8 checks; but layout must preserve old offsets Enforce append-only: new fields must not shift existing field offsets; compute_layout on evolved schema must match old offsets for existing fields

2.10 Operational Notes

  • ser-telemetry hook: layout_compute_duration_us histogram; layout_total_data_words / layout_total_ptr_words gauges per TypeId.
  • tracing::debug!(type_id=?id, data_words, ptr_words, "layout computed") per type.
  • Layout results are deterministic and static; no runtime re-computation; log once at startup.
  • ser-tool (Phase 9) will call compute_layout for the inspector; ensure show_layout example is the reference for operator debugging.
  • Deployment: layout is embedded in compiled Schema; a schema change (new field) must re-run compute_layout; old layout cached in schema registry must be invalidated by SchemaHash change.
  • Rollback: layout algorithm change = SchemaHash change (because field offsets are part of the canonical schema serialization); old encoders and new decoders detect IncompatibleSchema via hash mismatch.
  • Evolution safety: compute_layout on an evolved schema (v2) must produce the same offsets for v1 fields as compute_layout on v1 schema; validated by ser-evolve in Phase 8.

15. Phase 3 — Wire format + pointers — segment/arena model, struct/list/far pointer encoding, message framing, little-endian primitives, varint/tag for unions

3.1 Objectives

  • Define binary wire format spec completely: word size (8 bytes), little-endian scalars, message framing, segment table, pointer encoding (struct/list/far), union tag/discriminant, varint encoding for list element counts and union tags.
  • Implement ser-core primitive read/write helpers: get_u8/u16/u32/u64/i8..i64/f32/f64 all LE; put_* counterparts; word-aligned access guards.
  • Implement ser-runtime segment/arena model: Segment { id: SegmentId, bytes: Vec<u8> }, Message { segments, root: Pointer }, framing header (segment count + per-segment byte lengths).
  • Specify Pointer encoding: Struct { offset: i32, data_words: u16, ptr_words: u16 }, List { offset: i32, elem: ListElemSize, count: u32 }, Far { double_far: bool, seg: SegmentId, offset: u32 }.
  • Union discriminant encoding: u16 tag field in data section, associated data via pointer-section or inline data word; varint used for list-element-count > 29-bit saturation boundary.
  • Constraint: NO format decisions deferred past this phase; Phase 4 (encode) and Phase 5 (decode) must see a frozen, byte-level-stable spec.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P1 (Cap'n Proto — struct/list/far pointer types), ADR-04 (little-endian fixed), ADR-05 (8-byte word), ADR-06 (fixed-width scalars; varint reserved for union-tag/list-count overflow only); B5 §5 (word sizes, alignment, endianness); §7.4 Pointer Safety, §7.7 Little-Endian Wire Encoding.

Wire primitives (ser-core/src/wire.rs): WireWord = u64, WORD_BYTES = 8; read_u64_le(buf, word_offset) bounds-checks word_offset*8+8 <= buf.len() (else OutOfBounds) then u64::from_le_bytes; symmetric write_u64_le; scalar helpers read_i8/u8 (byte-indexed), read_i16/u16/i32/u32/f32 (half/single-word LE), read_f64/i64/u64 (full-word LE); lzero_pad(buf, to_len) zero-pads to word boundary.

Pointer encoding (ser-core/src/pointer.rs): tag bits [1:0] select 0b00 struct / 0b01 list / 0b10 far / 0b11 reserved. Struct pointer:

[63:48] ptr_words (u16)  [47:32] data_words (u16)  [31:2] offset (i30 words)  [1:0]=0b00

List pointer (elem_size_tag: 0=Void,1=Bit,2=Byte,3=TwoBytes,4=FourBytes,5=EightBytes,6=EightBytesPointer,7=Composite — count is then a word count followed by a struct tag word):

[63:35] count (u29)  [34:32] elem_size_tag (3-bit)  [31:2] offset (i30)  [1:0]=0b01

Far pointer:

[63:33] seg_id (u32)  [32] double_far  [31:3] offset (u29 words in target seg)  [1:0]=0b10

encode_struct_ptr(offset,dw,pw)/decode_struct_ptr; encode_list_ptr(offset,elem,count) validates count ≤ 2^29-1; encode_far_ptr(seg,offset,double_far); Pointer::decode(word) dispatches on the tag bits.

Varint (ser-core/src/varint.rs): LEB128 unsigned encode_varint/decode_varint, used only for union-tag embedding in composite-list tag words and out-of-line metadata — never for scalar fields (fixed-width LE); max 10 bytes, Truncated if the buffer is exhausted mid-sequence. Framing (ser-runtime/src/framing.rs): header = [segment_count:u32 LE][seg0_len_words:u32 LE]…[padding to word boundary]; write_frame_header/read_frame_header(buf) -> (Vec<u32>, header_byte_len) validates no count overflow; segment 0 is always first, the table is immediately followed by concatenated segment bytes.

Segments (ser-runtime/src/segment.rs): Arena{segments: Vec<Vec<u8>>, current_seg}; alloc_words(n) is a bump allocator growing the current segment or opening a new one past SEGMENT_MAX_WORDS (default 512Ki words = 4 MiB); cross-segment references use far pointers, double-far when the target itself needs relocation; Message::from_segments(segs, root).

Union tags (ser-core/src/union.rs): discriminant is a u16 in the data section at the field's designated offset; payload is either an inline data word or a pointer-section slot per schema; encode_union_tag/decode_union_tag read/write the 2 LE bytes. Test harness (ser-core/tests/wire_format.rs, ser-runtime/tests/framing.rs): scalar round-trip at boundary values (0, MAX, MIN, -1 signed); pointer encode/decode round-trips for all three kinds; framing at 1/2/64 segments with odd/even header padding; varint at 1/2/5/10 bytes plus truncated and overflow cases.

3.3 Deliverable

  • ser-core and ser-runtime compile; all wire helpers pass cargo test.
  • ser-tool wire-dump <file> prints decoded pointer words, segment table, scalar fields in hex + decoded.
  • Byte-for-byte fixture files (.wire binaries) checked into fixtures/phase3/.
  • Wire format spec doc (docs/wire-format.md) auto-generated from ser-tool spec-dump.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p ser-core -p ser-runtime 100% pass, 0 warnings
Scalar round-trip fuzz (proptest, 10K iters) no mismatches
Pointer encode/decode for all 3 kinds no decode errors on valid
Frame header parse correctness matches hand-crafted fixtures
read_u64_le on 1-byte buf returns OutOfBounds
Varint decode on truncated input returns Truncated
ser-tool wire-dump on 2-segment message correct human-readable output

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/phase3/struct_ptr_zero.wire zero-offset struct pointer, 0 dw/pw decode → (0,0,0)
fixtures/phase3/struct_ptr_maxdims.wire dw=65535, pw=65535, offset=max-positive round-trip exact
fixtures/phase3/list_composite_4words.wire composite list, 4 elem, struct tag elem_size=Composite, count=4
fixtures/phase3/far_single.wire single far pointer seg=1 offset=8 Far { double_far:false, seg:1, offset:8 }
fixtures/phase3/far_double.wire double far pointer chain resolves to struct pointer in seg 2
fixtures/phase3/frame_3seg.wire 3-segment message header length = 16 bytes, counts match
fixtures/phase3/varint_boundaries.bin 1/2/5/10-byte varints decoded values match expected u64 array
fixtures/phase3/varint_truncated.bin cut after first byte of 2-byte sequence SerError::Truncated
fixtures/phase3/union_tag_nonzero.wire union discriminant = 7 decode_union_tag → 7
fixtures/phase3/scalar_le_boundaries.wire u64::MAX, i64::MIN, f64::NaN, 0.0 exact LE byte sequence verified

3.6 Pitfalls

  • Offset sign extension: struct/list pointer offset is i30 (signed 30-bit); naively casting bits [31:2] as i32 without sign-extending bit 29 yields wrong negative offsets. Debug: compare decoded offset with hand-computed expected; sign-extend explicitly with ((bits >> 2) as i32) << 2 >> 2.
  • Far pointer double-far indirection: double-far landing pad is two words (far ptr + struct ptr) in target segment; single-far is one pointer directly targeting data. Confusing them silently miscomputes base address. Debug: ser-tool wire-dump --verbose shows ptr chain.
  • List count field only 29 bits: passing count > 536_870_911 silently truncates. Validate in encode_list_ptr; return SerError::SchemaInvalid on overflow. Signal: ser-telemetry counter encode.list_count_overflow.
  • Segment table padding: header must be padded to word (8-byte) boundary; odd segment count leaves 4-byte gap. Missing pad shifts all segment offsets by 4, causing global misparse. Test: odd-count framing fixture.
  • Composite list tag-word counted in element words: the tag word is included in the count field (as word count), not as a separate prefix. Misinterpreting it as element count inflates element count by 1 and shifts all element offsets. Reference: Cap'n Proto encoding spec, sec. list composite.
  • Union data clobbering: two union arms both writing to the same data-section word at different byte offsets can corrupt each other if writes are not isolated. The builder must zero the entire union word before writing the active arm's payload.
  • Little-endian on big-endian hosts: tests must explicitly verify byte order; u64::to_le_bytes is a no-op on little-endian but non-trivial on BE. CI matrix must include a big-endian target (or qemu cross-test).

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • read_u64_le on aligned pointers: optimizer should emit a single MOV (x86-64 with target-feature=+sse2); ensure no intermediate Vec or heap touch.
  • encode_varint hot path: inline for 1-byte case (value < 128); branch for multi-byte; no allocation.
  • Arena::alloc_words bump path: single pointer increment + bounds check; avoid Vec::push per word (pre-reserve at Arena construction with with_capacity(initial_words * 8)).
  • Framing header write: pre-compute header length before first Vec::extend; one resize call not N individual pushes.
  • Pointer decode: single 64-bit read + bit-mask operations; zero allocations; entire decode in ~4 instructions.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-length segment (0 words): valid (empty struct); frame header encodes as 0; segment slice is empty &[].
  • Pointer offset = 0 (self-referential): struct pointer with offset=0 targets the word immediately after the pointer (standard convention); NOT the pointer word itself. Offset=-1 is special (pointer-to-pointer) — document as reserved/SerError::BadPointer.
  • SegmentId = 0: always root segment; far pointer targeting seg=0 is valid; no special-casing needed in framing.
  • count=0 list: valid; produces empty list Reader; no iteration; still needs valid elem_size_tag.
  • f64 NaN wire encoding: NaN bits preserved exactly (no normalization); user must not compare NaN equality; accessor returns bitwise-exact stored f64.
  • Message with 1 segment (common case): frame header is exactly 8 bytes; no padding needed; fast path in read_frame_header.

3.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Corrupt frame header (wrong count) read_frame_header returns Truncated/OutOfBounds single message rejected caller sees SerError; no state mutation log + discard; increment decode.frame_error metric
Negative segment length in header validation in read_frame_header (u32 check) parse aborts SerError::SchemaInvalid discard message
Far pointer to nonexistent segment Phase-5 validation catches before accessor single decode BadPointer error reject, telemetry
Arena OOM (alloc_words fails) Vec::try_reserve or capacity check encode fails, message not produced propagate as SerError caller must handle; no partial message written
Segment > SEGMENT_MAX_WORDS Arena allocation guard encode rejected before wire SerError::SchemaInvalid reduce message size

3.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (ser-telemetry): wire.bytes_framed (counter), wire.segment_count (histogram), wire.far_ptr_count (counter), encode.list_count_overflow (counter), decode.frame_error (counter).
  • Tracing: tracing::trace! at segment allocation (seg_id, byte_len); tracing::debug! at far-pointer resolution chain.
  • Logging: tracing::warn! if varint decode sees 10-byte sequence (near-max; possible malicious input).
  • ser-tool wire-dump --format=json: machine-readable pointer dump for CI conformance diffing.
  • Deployment: no runtime configuration for wire format — format is compile-time frozen; version is embedded in SchemaHash. Format changes require new project version tag.
  • Migration: incompatible format changes require bumping WIRE_FORMAT_VERSION constant in ser-core; runtime rejects mismatched version at framing layer.

16. Phase 4 — Builder / encode path — arena + segment allocation, message Builder API, writing data + pointers, nested structs/lists, canonical (deterministic) encoding mode

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement ser-encode crate: Builder<'a, T> arena-backed API for constructing messages from schema-derived types.
  • Implement Arena (bump allocator over Vec<Segment>): word-aligned allocations, multi-segment promotion via far pointers, deterministic segment sizing.
  • Implement StructBuilder: write data-section scalars (LE), write pointer-section (struct/list) pointers, initialize new nested StructBuilder, initialize ListBuilder.
  • Implement ListBuilder<E>: typed element list; packed scalar list for primitive types; composite list for struct elements.
  • Implement canonical encoding mode: fixed field-write order (ascending FieldId), zeroed padding, no dead space between sections, stable pointer offsets → byte-identical output for logically-equal messages.
  • Constraint: Builder API must be usable both from codegen-emitted code (Phase 7) and from the reflective builder path; zero heap allocation for the common single-segment case if arena is pre-sized.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P3 (arena allocation), P5 (canonical encoding), I1 (canonical byte equality), I3 (arena monotonic), ADR-06 (fixed-width integers), ADR-10 (system allocator); B3 §6 (arena allocators), R1 (Cap'n Proto — multi-segment + far pointer design).

Arena (ser-encode/src/arena.rs): Arena{segs: Vec<Segment>, cur: SegmentId, pos: usize}; new(initial_words) pre-allocates segs[0] to initial_words*8 zeroed bytes; alloc(words) -> AllocResult{seg,word_offset} bumps pos, and on overflow (pos+words > cur_capacity) calls push_segment(max(words, SEGMENT_GROW_WORDS=512, tunable via ArenaConfig)), sets cur, and emits a far-pointer slot in the previous segment; finish(root) copies segs into Message::segments (no extra alloc for a single segment).

Struct builder (ser-encode/src/builder.rs): MessageBuilder{arena}; init_root::<T>() allocates the root struct's words and returns a StructBuilder (the root pointer lives in Message::root, not in-buffer); StructBuilder<'a,T>{arena,seg,data_start,ptr_start} exposes set_u8/set_u64 (byte/word-aligned writes via schema Field::offset), set_bool (bit-packed read-modify-write on a shared data byte), init_struct(field) (allocates the child, encodes a struct pointer in the parent's pointer section), init_list(field,count) (allocates the list body, encodes a list pointer), set_union(field,arm,..) (writes the u16 tag, zeros the rest of the union word, then the payload), and set_text/set_bytes (allocate len(+1 for NUL) word-padded bytes and write a Byte-element list pointer).

List builder (ser-encode/src/list_builder.rs): ListBuilder<'a,E>{arena,seg,elem_words,base,count,cur}; scalar lists are contiguous packed bytes (set(idx,val) direct write, no per-element pointer); composite lists prefix a struct tag word (word count, not element count) and get_mut(idx) returns a StructBuilder at base+1+idx*elem_words; pointer lists (EightBytesPointer) store a 64-bit pointer per slot via set_struct(idx); finish() asserts cur==count.

Canonical mode (ser-encode/src/canonical.rs): a CanonicalMode flag on MessageBuilder enforces fields written in ascending FieldId order; padding bytes zeroed (free — the arena is zeroed at alloc); no far pointers (single segment required unless allow_multi_segment_canonical); exact list element counts (no over-alloc); pointer offsets deterministic from allocation order. canonical_encode::<T>(value, schema) does a reflective walk emitting canonical bytes; finish_canonical() validates single-segment and returns framed bytes.

Runtime entry points (ser-runtime/src/encode.rs): encode::<T>(builder_fn) constructs a MessageBuilder, invokes the closure, calls arena.finish(); encode_canonical::<T>(..) is the canonical-mode counterpart.

Test harness: builder_basic.rs (all scalar types, decode + verify), nested.rs (depth-5 nesting, pointer-chain correctness), list_types.rs (scalar/composite/pointer lists), canonical.rs (two independent encodes of an equal message byte-compare identical), multiseg.rs (forced segment overflow, far pointer written, Phase-5 decode), and a proptest round-tripping random struct field values through encode→decode.

4.3 Deliverable

  • ser-encode crate compiles; MessageBuilder and StructBuilder APIs work end-to-end.
  • encode() and encode_canonical() usable from ser-runtime public API.
  • Benchmark (ser-bench/benches/encode.rs): encode throughput for simple struct (8 scalar fields) and 1000-element list.
  • ser-tool encode-example <schema.szs> generates a dummy message and prints wire hex.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p ser-encode 100% pass
Canonical encode determinism bitwise identical output, 100K proptest iters
Encode + decode round-trip (all scalar types) field values match
Nested struct depth=5 encode+decode no pointer errors
1000-element composite list encode+decode correct count + all values
Multi-segment encode (forced overflow) Phase-5 validates successfully
Simple struct encode throughput ≥ 500 MB/s at zero-alloc single-seg
set_text with empty string 1 word allocated (NUL byte + padding)

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/phase4/simple_struct.wire 8-field struct, all WireTypes decode field values match simple_struct.expected.json
fixtures/phase4/canonical_pair_a.wire canonical encode, message A byte-identical to canonical_pair_b.wire
fixtures/phase4/canonical_pair_b.wire same logical message, different build order see above
fixtures/phase4/nested_depth5.wire struct nested 5 levels depth=5 validates, all fields readable
fixtures/phase4/list_scalar_u64_1000.wire 1000-element u64 list all elements correct, count=1000
fixtures/phase4/list_composite_100.wire 100-element composite struct list each elem's fields correct
fixtures/phase4/multiseg_forced.wire two-segment message with far pointer Phase-5 decode succeeds, far ptr resolves
fixtures/phase4/text_utf8.wire UTF-8 text field, 200 chars bytes match input string + NUL
fixtures/phase4/union_arm1.wire union set to arm=1 tag=1, arm-1 payload correct, arm-0 data zeroed
fixtures/phase4/union_arm0_default.wire default arm (arm=0) tag=0, data correct

4.6 Pitfalls

  • Pointer offset computation: struct pointer offset is in WORDS relative to the word AFTER the pointer word. Off-by-one (using the pointer word address instead of next-word address) shifts all child data by one word, silently corrupting layout. Debug: ser-tool wire-dump --verbose shows computed vs expected offsets.
  • Bool bit packing clobber: set_bool uses read-modify-write on a byte shared with other bool fields; if two adjacent bool fields are set in wrong order without re-reading, a race (or encode-path bug) clears a previously-set bit. Enforce single-threaded Builder usage; debug_assert byte value pre/post.
  • Composite list tag word miscounted: tag word encodes count = total words in list body (not element count); writing element count instead causes Phase-5 to misparse element boundaries. Test: decode immediately after encode; check element stride.
  • Text field not NUL-terminated: set_text must append \0; forgetting causes out-of-bounds read in C FFI or language bindings expecting C strings.
  • Far pointer allocation order: when a struct crosses segment boundary, the far pointer must be allocated in the OLD segment's pointer section, pointing to the landing pad in the NEW segment; allocating in reverse order produces a circular reference. Enforce: Arena::alloc returns prior segment's pointer slot before bumping to new segment.
  • Canonical mode with multi-segment: canonical encoding promises single-segment; silently emitting multi-segment produces non-canonical bytes and breaks hash/dedup. Return SerError::SchemaInvalid if canonical mode triggers segment overflow (unless allow_multi_segment_canonical).
  • Over-allocated list (count mismatch): allocating 100 elements but writing 90 leaves 10 uninitialized (zeroed) elements visible to decoder. ListBuilder::finish must assert cur == count; in debug builds, panic; in release, zeroed elements decode as default values (may be acceptable but must be documented).

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Arena bump path hot: alloc_words must reduce to pointer-increment + branch in steady state; no Vec::push per allocation; pre-reserve initial_words * 8 at construction.
  • Bool bit-pack: read-modify-write costs extra read; group adjacent bool writes; profile with perf stat.
  • set_text allocation: text body is separately allocated after struct allocation; two arena bumps per text field. Optimize: allocate text body in same segment as struct when possible (check remaining capacity before struct alloc).
  • Canonical reflective walk: reflective encode traverses schema at runtime; for hot paths, prefer codegen-emitted Builder (Phase 7) which eliminates schema lookup per field.
  • Nested struct alloc order: child allocated after parent pointer slot; offset computed post-alloc; no speculative alloc needed. Depth > 64 triggers DepthExceeded to prevent stack overflow in encode.
  • finish_canonical single-segment check: O(1) — just checks arena.segs.len() == 1.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty struct (data_words=0, ptr_words=0): valid; zero words allocated; struct pointer encodes dw=0, pw=0, offset=0 (points to next word, which may be end-of-segment — valid if no actual read).
  • List of count=0: valid; allocates list pointer only; no element words; ListBuilder::finish with cur==0 is OK.
  • Struct with all-default values: if all fields match schema defaults and canonical mode, encoder may omit (zero-fill) non-pointer fields (no-op since arena is zeroed). Pointer fields pointing to empty structs still allocate.
  • Union arm change after partial write: Builder is write-once per field; no API to overwrite a union arm post-set_union. Enforce with debug_assert on tag field: if tag word non-zero before write, panic.
  • set_bytes with 0-length slice: allocates 0 bytes + word padding (1 word min); list pointer count=0; decoder returns empty &[u8].
  • Arena initial_words = 0: immediately allocates first segment on first alloc_words; valid; slightly higher amortized cost.

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
alloc_words OOM Vec::try_reserve → Err encode aborts, no message emitted SerError returned caller retries with smaller message or pre-allocated arena
Wrong pointer offset Phase-5 decode BadPointer or OutOfBounds single message decode fail SerError fix encode logic; fixture regression test
Canonical non-determinism CI proptest canonical round-trip diff silent hash/dedup break CI gates on bitwise equality fix field-write ordering to ascending FieldId
Composite list tag miscounted Phase-5 OutOfBounds on element access all elements unreadable decode rejects message fix ListBuilder tag-word computation
Text not NUL-terminated Phase-5 text accessor reads extra byte single field corrupt field returns SerError::OutOfBounds or UB (if unsafe) fix set_text; add NUL explicitly

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: encode.message_bytes (histogram), encode.segment_count (histogram), encode.arena_realloc (counter — segments added beyond initial), encode.duration_ns (histogram), encode.canonical_single_seg_fail (counter).
  • Tracing: tracing::debug! per MessageBuilder::finish; tracing::trace! per Arena::alloc in debug builds only (too hot for production).
  • Logging: tracing::warn! if canonical mode falls back to multi-segment.
  • Deployment: Builder API is additive; new fields require schema evolution (Phase 8); old builders produce messages readable by new decoders (forward compat via defaults).
  • Rollback: removing a Builder call for a deprecated field is safe if field is marked optional in schema and decoder defaults it.
  • Migration: codegen re-run required when schema adds fields (Phase 7); existing builder code still compiles but won't set new fields (they decode as defaults).

17. Phase 5 — Zero-copy decode + validation pass — bounds/alignment/pointer-target validation as the trust boundary, then accessor Readers that cast directly into the buffer (no copy/alloc), lazy field access

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement ser-decode crate: single validation pass over raw &[u8] that enforces ALL safety invariants before any field is accessed; post-validation field access is infallible at runtime (no alloc, no copy, no OOB possible).
  • Validation pass checks: buffer bounds, word alignment at every pointer dereference, pointer-target within declared segment, far-pointer chain depth ≤ 2, recursion/nesting depth ≤ MAX_NESTING_DEPTH (default 64), total traversed words ≤ MAX_TRAVERSAL_WORDS (amplification defense), no pointer cycles.
  • Implement StructReader<'a> and ListReader<'a, E>: lifetime-checked views into the validated buffer; field access via direct slice cast (no memcpy, no heap alloc).
  • Implement Reader<'a, T> newtype for typed access; codegen emits concrete accessor methods wrapping StructReader.
  • Implement lazy access: reading field F only touches the words for F; unrequested fields are never touched (cache-friendly partial reads).
  • Constraint: post-validation, all unsafe pointer casts are encapsulated in ser-decode internals behind public safe APIs; caller sees only &'a str, &'a [u8], scalar values, and nested StructReader<'a> / ListReader<'a, E>.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P6 (validate-then-view), P7 (trust boundary), I5 (post-validation safety), I6 (max nesting depth = 64), I7 (max traversal words), ADR-17 (unsafe encapsulation); P11 (Cap'n Proto 2018 security advisory — traversal amplification), R4 (rkyv — check_bytes approach), §6.5 Bounded Resource Usage.

Validation (ser-decode/src/validate.rs): ValidationState{visited_words: BitSet, depth: u32, traversal_count: u32} tracks visited word ranges to detect cycles. validate_message(buf, schema) parses the frame header, validates each segment length against buf, then calls validate_pointer(root_ptr, seg=0, depth=0). validate_pointer(ptr, seg, segs, state) dispatches by kind: struct — check data_words+ptr_words fit in-segment, word-offset aligned, recurse into the pointer section; list — check count*elem_size words fit, and for Composite validate the tag word plus each element's pointer section; far (single) — validate the landing pad in the target segment, recurse once; far (double) — validate the landing pad (far ptr + struct ptr), recurse. Every call increments traversal_count (> MAX_TRAVERSAL_WORDS → DepthExceeded) and depth on recursion (> MAX_NESTING_DEPTH → DepthExceeded), and marks visited words (already-marked → BadPointer, a cycle). validate_text/validate_bytes check NUL-termination / bounds only; Misaligned fires whenever byte_offset % 8 != 0 for a word-sized read.

Readers (ser-decode/src/reader.rs): ValidatedMessage<'a>{buf,segs,root} is produced only by a successful validate_message. StructReader<'a>{seg,data_start,ptr_start,data_words,ptr_words} exposes get_u64/get_u8/get_bool as direct unchecked casts into the data section (already validated), get_struct (reads the pointer word, resolves far pointers in ≤2 hops, both within the validated message, returns a new StructReader), get_list (resolves the list pointer into a ListReader), get_text/get_bytes (resolve a byte-list pointer and cast the range as &'a str/&'a [u8] — true zero-copy; UTF-8 validity is established during the validation pass, not the accessor), get_union_tag (reads the u16 discriminant), and a default-value fallback when a field's data section is smaller than the schema expects (evolution, Phase 8).

List reader (ser-decode/src/list_reader.rs): ListReader<'a,E>{seg,base,count,elem_size}; len(); get(idx) bounds-checks against the already-validated count and casts directly; iter() yields elements lazily with no intermediate Vec; composite lists compute get(idx) -> StructReader at base+1+idx*elem_words; bit lists are bit-indexed into packed bytes.

Unsafe boundary (ser-decode/src/unsafe_cast.rs, pub(crate) only — the sole home for every unsafe in the crate): cast_word::<T: Pod>(seg, word_offset) performs the raw pointer cast, valid only post-validation; cast_bytes<'a>(seg, byte_offset, len) builds the zero-copy slice.

Runtime entry points (ser-runtime/src/decode.rs): decode<'a>(buf, schema) -> Result<ValidatedMessage<'a>, SerError> calls validate_message and wraps the result; root_reader<'a,T>(msg) returns the root StructReader.

Test harness: validate_bounds.rs (short segments, misaligned/out-of-segment targets → correct SerError each), validate_cycles.rs (A→B→A → BadPointer), validate_depth.rs (65-level nesting → DepthExceeded), validate_amplification.rs (huge list count, tiny segment → OutOfBounds, no allocation attempt), reader_access.rs (encode via Phase 4, decode, verify every field type), zero_copy_assert.rs (accessor slice pointer falls within the original buffer — proves no copy), lazy_access.rs (reading field 0 does not touch field 1's cache line), and a cargo-fuzz target (fuzz_validate.rs) asserting arbitrary bytes never panic or UB, only valid SerError variants.

5.3 Deliverable

  • ser-decode compiles; validation pass rejects all malformed fixtures; accessors work on all valid fixtures.
  • decode() public API in ser-runtime returns ValidatedMessage or typed SerError.
  • Fuzz target registered with cargo-fuzz; first 1M iterations clean (no panics, no ASAN hits).
  • Benchmark: ser-bench/benches/decode.rs shows validation + root read throughput; zero-alloc verified via custom allocator shim.
  • ser-tool decode <file> <schema.szs> pretty-prints message fields.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p ser-decode 100% pass
Fuzz (cargo-fuzz, 1M iters) no panics, no UB (ASAN/MSAN clean)
All malformed fixture types correct SerError variant each
Cycle detection fixture BadPointer within 500 ms
Depth=65 nesting DepthExceeded, not stack overflow
Amplification (giant list count) OutOfBounds, zero allocation
Zero-copy assert test field &[u8] ptr within input buf
Decode throughput (simple struct) ≥ 1 GB/s validation + read
Zero heap allocations post-validate custom alloc shim: 0 allocs in accessor path
Encode → decode round-trip (all types) field values bit-identical

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected error / output
fixtures/phase5/truncated_header.wire frame header cut at byte 3 SerError::Truncated
fixtures/phase5/oob_struct_ptr.wire struct pointer target past segment end SerError::OutOfBounds
fixtures/phase5/misaligned_ptr.wire pointer target at odd-word byte offset SerError::Misaligned
fixtures/phase5/cycle_ab.wire ptr A→B→A SerError::BadPointer
fixtures/phase5/depth_65.wire 65-level nesting SerError::DepthExceeded
fixtures/phase5/amplification_huge_count.wire list count=536870911, seg=8 bytes SerError::OutOfBounds
fixtures/phase5/far_single_valid.wire valid single far pointer decode succeeds
fixtures/phase5/far_double_valid.wire valid double far pointer decode succeeds
fixtures/phase5/far_bad_seg.wire far pointer to seg=99 (nonexistent) SerError::BadPointer
fixtures/phase5/text_no_nul.wire text list missing NUL terminator SerError::BadPointer or custom text validation error
fixtures/phase5/valid_all_types.wire encode all WireTypes, valid decode all fields correct
fixtures/phase5/zero_copy_bytes.wire large bytes field (1 MiB) accessor returns slice into buf, no copy

5.6 Pitfalls

  • Cycle detection via BitSet: visited_words must track ALL word ranges visited during traversal; if only struct-root words are marked (not pointer-section words), a cycle through list elements escapes detection. Mark every word range entered during validation.
  • Far pointer double-hop depth counting: a double-far pointer constitutes TWO pointer resolutions; if depth counter increments only once, deep double-far chains can exceed nesting limit undetected. Increment depth TWICE for double-far.
  • from_utf8_unchecked safety: safe only if validation pass explicitly validated UTF-8 of the text bytes; if validation only checked bounds + NUL, the unchecked path is UB for non-UTF-8 bytes. Fix: validate UTF-8 in validation pass (std::str::from_utf8), store result as "text validated" in ValidationState; OR use from_utf8 in accessor (small cost). Document which approach is chosen.
  • Composite list tag-word misparse: tag word in composite list looks like a struct pointer (same bit layout); if validation code tries to decode_struct_ptr on it, the tag bits = 0b00 and the "offset" field is actually the element count — produces wildly wrong recursion target. Composite list validation must read tag word as struct tag (offset field interpreted as element word count, not pointer offset).
  • Accessor lifetime unsoundness: StructReader<'a> must hold 'a tied to the original buffer slice; if ValidatedMessage is dropped while a StructReader is live, the borrow checker catches this in safe Rust — but if unsafe transmutes or raw pointers are used to extend lifetime, UB results. Enforce: no unsafe lifetime extension outside unsafe_cast.rs; review gate on all unsafe blocks.
  • Default value for absent field (evolution): when decoder sees a data section smaller than schema's declared data_words (old writer, new schema), it must return the field's DefaultVal rather than reading out-of-bounds. Validation pass must record actual data_words from struct pointer; accessor checks field.offset.word() < actual_data_words before reading.
  • Amplification via deeply nested pointers: a message with 64 levels of nesting where each level points to a 1-word struct uses only 64 words total — but if traversal counter is per-pointer-type not per-word, a pathological structure with shared sub-trees (not cycles, just shared far-pointer targets) can re-visit words O(2^depth) times. Fix: the visited_words BitSet prevents re-visiting, capping total traversal at buffer size.

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Validation pass is O(reachable words): not O(total buffer bytes); unreachable data (padding, orphaned segments) not traversed. Ensures validation proportional to logical message size.
  • visited_words BitSet allocation: for large messages (64 MiB), BitSet = 64 MiB / 64 = 1 MiB of bits = 128 KiB — heap alloc in validation. Use a stack-allocated small-set (first 1024 words inline) with heap fallback for large messages.
  • Accessor get_u64 hot path: 1 pointer add + 1 LE load + 0 branches (bounds already validated); should be 2–3 instructions. Verify with cargo asm.
  • get_struct far-pointer resolution: at most 2 memory reads (one per hop); both hops already in L1 if struct fits in cache line. Chain cost ≤ 2 cache misses worst case.
  • Lazy access: unread fields incur zero cost; iterator over ListReader touches only element words, no stride overhead beyond element size.
  • BitSet marking overhead: set-and-check for each word range; for sequential scan of large list, a range-mark optimization (mark_range(start..end)) amortizes per-word cost to per-range.
  • UTF-8 validation cost: from_utf8 is O(len); for large text fields, this dominates validation. Profile per-field; consider skipping for known-ASCII schemas.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty struct (dw=0, pw=0): valid; all field accesses return defaults; pointer-section iteration visits 0 pointers; no traversal.
  • Null pointer (all-zero word): struct pointer with dw=0, pw=0, offset=-1 (special null convention): must be handled as "absent" → field returns default; NOT treated as self-referential or OOB.
  • List of structs where struct dw < schema dw (old writer): validation records actual dw from tag word; accessor for newer fields returns default. Same evolution logic as top-level structs.
  • Segment containing ONLY padding (all zeros): valid; no pointers into it if no far pointers reference it; validation skips unreachable segments.
  • Message with 1 segment and 0 bytes (empty): root pointer must be null/void; decode returns empty StructReader; no field accessible.
  • Bit list of length 0: valid; no bytes allocated; ListReader::len() == 0; iteration yields nothing.
  • get_list where field is absent (ptr_words too small): returns empty ListReader with count=0 (or type-default list); does not OOB.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Validation OOB miss (bug) ASAN in fuzz / integration accessor reads out-of-bounds → UB fuzz CI gate; fuzzer required before shipping fix validation logic; add regression fixture
Cycle detection miss fuzz infinite-loop / timeout validation hangs MAX_TRAVERSAL_WORDS hard cap exits loop add cycle fixture; fix BitSet marking
from_utf8_unchecked on non-UTF8 Miri in CI UB in accessor Miri gate in CI switch to from_utf8 in accessor
Absent-field not defaulted wrong value returned silent data corruption in caller property test: schema-evolution encode+decode fix accessor to check actual_data_words
ValidatedMessage lifetime escape compile error (safe Rust) n/a in safe code borrow checker correct lifetime annotation
Traversal counter overflow u32 u32::MAX check in validate validation incorrectly passes add explicit overflow check → DepthExceeded fix to u64 or saturating check

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (ser-telemetry): decode.validate_duration_ns (histogram), decode.message_bytes (histogram), decode.validation_rejections (counter, labeled by SerError variant), decode.traversal_words (histogram — amplification proxy), decode.far_ptr_hops (histogram).
  • Tracing: tracing::debug! per decode() call with msg_bytes, seg_count; tracing::warn! on rejection with error + first 64 bytes hex (no secrets); tracing::trace! per pointer resolution in debug builds.
  • Logging: tracing::error! only if internal invariant broken (post-validation accessor OOB — should be impossible; indicates a validation bug; log + abort).
  • Fuzz: cargo fuzz run fuzz_validate must be run in CI on every PR touching ser-decode; crash corpus committed to fuzz/corpus/.
  • Miri: cargo miri test -p ser-decode in CI; catches UB in unsafe_cast.rs.
  • Deployment: MAX_NESTING_DEPTH and MAX_TRAVERSAL_WORDS tunable via DecodeConfig passed to decode(); production defaults conservative (64 / 2Mi words); high-trust internal services may raise limits.
  • Rollback: decode API is backward-compatible; old decoders handle old messages; new decoders handle old messages (defaults for new fields); rollback safe without wire-format change.
  • Schema registry integration (Phase 9): decode() optionally accepts SchemaHash; validates wire SchemaHash against expected before running validation pass; rejects on mismatch → SerError::IncompatibleSchema.

18. Phase 6 — Code generation — emit per-type Reader/Builder accessor code for a target language from the schema; generated-code invariants; the reflective fallback path

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement ser-codegen: given a Schema (validated, layout-computed), emit per-type accessor code for a target language (Rust first; Python/C++ stubs planned).
  • Generated Rust: per-struct Reader<'a> + Builder<'_> wrappers with typed field accessors; per-enum discriminant enum; per-union typed-variant accessors; per-list ListView<'a, T>.
  • Invariants introduced:
    • Generated accessor offsets are constants derived from Layout; they never drift from layout-computer output.
    • Generated code must not contain unsafe except in the single cast_field helper delegating to ser-decode's trust boundary.
    • Reflective fallback: all accessors available dynamically via ser-runtime's ReflectiveReader without code generation.
  • Scope: ser-codegen crate; ser-cli codegen subcommand; integration with ser-schema hash for cache invalidation.
  • Constraints:
    • Generated code compiles with cargo check under MSRV 1.78; zero clippy warnings at deny(warnings).
    • Codegen is hermetic: output depends only on Schema + SchemaHash; no filesystem side-effects during generation.
    • No full source in generated file headers; only // generated by ser-codegen vX.Y from schema <hash>.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P8 (type-safe codegen), P2 (schema-first), I2 (layout determinism — offsets are layout constants), ADR-21 (proc-macro + build.rs), ADR-17 (unsafe only in accessor cast); B5 §8 (compiler backend design), R2 (FlatBuffers — codegen architecture reference).

Crate ser-codegen — modules: codegen::ir (language-agnostic IR — GenType{name,fields,layout}, GenField{name,accessor_offset,wire_type,default}), codegen::rust_emitter (walks IR, emits Rust via string templating — no proc-macro compile-time dependency, simpler audit), codegen::reflective (ReflectiveReader backed by Schema, interprets fields by id/name at runtime), codegen::cache (keyed by SchemaHash, invalidates on mismatch).

IR construction (ir::from_schema(schema) -> Vec<GenType>): maps each TypeDef.kind to an IR variant, copies layout, maps each Field to a GenField with accessor_offset = field.offset; union types emit a Tag enum plus per-arm accessors returning Option<ArmReader<'a>>; list types emit ListView<'a,T> with len()/get(idx).

Rust emitter (rust_emitter::emit(types) -> String): per struct emits pub struct FooReader<'a>{buf,base} + impl<'a> FooReader<'a> with one accessor per scalar field (read_i64_le(self.buf, self.base + OFFSET_FIELD)) and one Result<BarReader<'a>, SerError> accessor per pointer field, plus a FooBuilder<'_> with set_field_name setters; per enum emits #[repr(u16)] with TryFrom<u16>; per union emits FooUnionReader::which() -> Result<FooUnionWhich<'a>, SerError>; every offset is a named constant (const OFFSET_FIELD_NAME: Offset = Offset(N)) derived from Layout, never hand-coded; the file header records // generated by ser-codegen from schema <SchemaHash>.

Reflective fallback (codegen::reflective): ReflectiveReader<'a>{schema,buf,base,type_id}; get_field_by_name(name) looks up the field in schema, reads its offset, and dispatches by WireType into a ReflectiveValue<'a> (enum over all wire scalars plus nested ReflectiveReader); no unsafe — delegates entirely to ser-decode's validated helpers.

Cache + CLI: ser-cli codegen generate --schema <file.szs> --out <dir> [--lang rust]; cache key is SchemaHash — a matching cached file skips re-emission, --force invalidates; ser-telemetry records codegen.emit_ns/codegen.types_emitted/codegen.cache_hit.

Test harness: codegen_tests/ snapshot tests (insta-style golden files); a compile-test that writes generated output to $TMPDIR and runs cargo check; and a round-trip test (generate → compile → encode via Builder → decode via Reader → assert field equality).

6.3 Deliverable

  • ser-codegen crate builds; ser-cli codegen subcommand functional.
  • cargo codegen --schema examples/point.szs --out /tmp/point_gen/ emits point_gen.rs compiling under MSRV 1.78.
  • ReflectiveReader reads all primitive + nested struct fields without generated code.
  • Snapshot golden files cover: struct, enum, union, list-of-scalar types.
  • ser-telemetry reports codegen.emit_ns per invocation.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p ser-codegen 100% pass
Snapshot golden diff 0 unexpected changes
Compile-test of generated Rust cargo check exits 0, 0 clippy warnings
Round-trip encode → decode via generated accessors Field values byte-exact
ReflectiveReader parity vs generated All scalar + nested fields equal
codegen.emit_ns for 50-field struct < 5 ms
Cache hit path Does not re-emit; codegen.cache_hit = true
Generated file unsafe blocks 0 (only delegated via ser-decode)

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/codegen/struct_scalar.szs All scalar WireTypes in one struct Golden struct_scalar_gen.rs.golden
fixtures/codegen/nested_struct.szs Pointer field → nested struct Nested Reader return type; pointer offset correct
fixtures/codegen/enum_type.szs Enum with 0-value + gaps #[repr(u16)] enum; TryFrom impl
fixtures/codegen/union_type.szs Union with 3 arms which() returns typed arm
fixtures/codegen/list_of_scalar.szs List(U64) field ListView<'a, u64> accessor
fixtures/codegen/malformed_no_layout.szs Schema missing layout (layout pass skipped) SerError::SchemaInvalid at emit time

fixtures/codegen/reflective_parity.bin Pre-encoded message; reflective vs generated read All field values identical

6.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug / signal Recovery
Offset constant drift Generated constants silently diverge from a re-numbered schema Cache not keyed on SchemaHash; stale generated file reused ser-tool wire-diff vs expected layout; RUST_LOG=ser_codegen=trace Key cache on SchemaHash; embed hash in file header; runtime verifies match
Union discriminant misalignment Generated which() reads the wrong arm Emitter assumes tag at data-section offset 0, not universally true Reflective reader shows correct tag; generated accessor wrong Emit tag offset as a Layout-derived constant; assert match
unsafe leakage into generated code Validation trust boundary bypassed Emitter template shortcut adds a direct ptr::read grep -r unsafe <generated_dir> in CI CI lint step; route only through ser-decode safe helpers
Snapshot false positives Golden diff churns field order across runs Fields emitted in HashMap order, not FieldId order Golden diff shows order-only changes Sort fields by FieldId before emission (determinism invariant)
Reflective fallback unexercised Reflective path diverges from generated silently Only the generated-accessor path is tested in CI No parity check present Mandate the reflective_parity.bin fixture (6.5) in CI

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • Codegen throughput: large schemas (500+ types) should complete < 1 s; use GenType IR pre-pass to avoid re-scanning schema repeatedly.
  • Generated code compile time: each generated file is a single flat mod; avoid deeply nested generics that bloat monomorphization.
  • Reflective reader field lookup: O(n) name scan per call if using Vec; use HashMap<&str, usize> index built once on first access (lazy, behind OnceLock).
  • Cache I/O: cache read/write on disk; use mmap for large schemas; skip write if hash matches (read-only fast path).
  • String allocation in emitter: use String::with_capacity estimates; avoid repeated push_str reallocations for large structs.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Schema with 0 types → emit empty module; no error.
  • Type with 0 fields → emit empty Reader/Builder; compile-test must pass.
  • Field name is a Rust keyword (type, use) → emitter appends _field suffix; documented in generated comment.
  • FieldId collision across types → impossible post-schema-validation, but emitter asserts uniqueness within type.
  • Schema with circular type reference (e.g., struct A { b: B }; struct B { a: A }) → layout computer breaks cycles via pointer indirection; emitter emits Box-wrapped pointer accessor for recursive types.
  • SchemaHash collision (birthday attack on u64) → cache evicts on compile-test failure; hash logged for diagnosis.
  • Target lang other than rust → returns SerError::SchemaInvalid with message "unsupported target language"; no partial output written.

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Offset constant wrong in generated code Round-trip test fails; field reads return garbage All generated readers for affected type Schema hash invalidates cache; re-generate Fix emitter; add regression fixture
Generated code fails cargo check CI compile-test step Build pipeline blocked Generated output quarantined; reflective fallback Fix template; emit deterministic code
Cache corruption (truncated file) Hash mismatch on read Single generation run Delete cache dir; re-generate Add cache integrity check (CRC on cache file)
Reflective reader OOB on unvalidated buffer SerError::OutOfBounds at field access One reflective read Reflective always validates bounds at access Ensure reflective path uses ser-decode helpers
Emitter panics on unknown WireType variant Process crash; no output Codegen invocation Match arms must be exhaustive; add unreachable! Add WireType::Unknown arm; return SerError::SchemaInvalid

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (via ser-telemetry): codegen.emit_ns (histogram), codegen.types_emitted (counter), codegen.cache_hit (bool gauge), codegen.compile_test_pass (bool gauge in CI).
  • Tracing: RUST_LOG=ser_codegen=debug logs each type name + field count + emit duration.
  • Deployment: generated code checked into consuming repo; CI re-generates and diffs; drift = build failure.
  • Rollback: revert schema .szs file; re-run ser-cli codegen; old generated code re-emitted from old schema.
  • Migration: adding a field → schema hash changes → cache miss → re-generate → new accessor available; old field constants unchanged (append-only).
  • Audit trail: generated file header embeds SchemaHash + ser-codegen semver; grep-able for provenance.

19. Phase 7 — Schema evolution + compatibility — append-only field/offset rules, optional fields + defaults, reserved holes, union extension, forward/backward compat checker

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement ser-evolve: schema-to-schema compatibility checker enforcing append-only evolution rules.
  • Evolution rules (invariants):
    • Append-only field ids: existing FieldId values never removed or repurposed; only new ids appended.
    • Offset stability: existing fields' Offset values immutable across versions.
    • Optional fields + defaults: new fields carry since_version > 0 + default: DefaultVal; old readers skip unknown fields; new readers return default for absent fields.
    • Reserved holes: removed field ids are reserved-marked; layout space preserved; no new field reuses offset.
    • Union extension: new union arms appended; old readers encountering unknown discriminant return SerError::UnknownVariant (not panic).
    • Forward compat: old writer + new reader → new reader returns defaults for absent new fields.
    • Backward compat: new writer + old reader → old reader ignores unknown (appended) fields.
  • Scope: ser-evolve crate; ser-cli evolve check subcommand; integration with ser-schema and ser-runtime.
  • Constraints:
    • Compat check is a pure function of two Schema values; no I/O during check.
    • Must produce structured CompatReport { violations: Vec<CompatViolation>, warnings: Vec<CompatWarning> }.
    • ser-runtime validates schema evolution at decode time when schema pair is available (optional strict mode).

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P9 (append-only evolution), P10 (reserved holes), I4 (offset stability), ADR-07 (evolution rules in IDL); B6 sec. 9 (schema evolution in DDIA), R5 (Apache Avro — writer/reader schema reconciliation), P9 (Cap'n Proto 2015 — append-only field ID design).

Crate ser-evolve — modules: evolve::rules (CompatRule trait: check(old,new) -> Vec<CompatViolation>), evolve::checker (CompatChecker::run(old,new) -> CompatReport, iterates rules), evolve::report (CompatReport, CompatViolation{rule,type_id,field_id,detail}, CompatWarning), evolve::registry (SchemaRegistry{versions: BTreeMap<u32,Schema>}, checks adjacent version pairs), evolve::defaults (eval_default(field,schema) -> WireValue).

Rules (each a CompatRule): FieldIdPreserved (every old field's FieldId exists in new with the same WireType+Offset), NoOffsetReuse (no new field occupies a reserved hole's offset), NewFieldOptional (any field in new absent from old must have since_version>0 + a non-None default), UnionArmAppendOnly (new arms only, none removed, discriminants stable), TypeNotRemoved (TypeIds in old still present or explicitly deprecated in new), EnumValueAppendOnly (existing discriminants immutable, only appended), ReservedHolePresent (a field id dropped from old must have a reserved marker covering its FieldId+Offset in new).

Registry & defaults: SchemaRegistry::register(version, schema) stores the schema and runs the compat check against the prior version, rejecting on violation; get(version); serializes to/from disk (bincode BTreeMap) for ser-cli. eval_default(field) returns the zero-value for scalars, Pointer::null for pointers, or the explicit DefaultVal::Literal(bytes); used by ser-decode whenever field.since_version > reader_schema_version.

CLI: ser-cli evolve check --old <v1.szs> --new <v2.szs> prints a CompatReport (JSON or human-readable), exit code 0=compatible/1=violations/2=warnings-only (strict mode); evolve register --registry <reg.bin> --version <N> --schema <N.szs>.

Runtime integration: DecodeOptions{strict_evolution, reader_version, writer_version} — when strict_evolution is set, validate() runs CompatChecker between reader and writer schema before accepting the message; ser-telemetry records evolve.check_ns/evolve.violations_total/evolve.warnings_total.

Test harness: a proptest classifying random schema mutations as compatible/violating and asserting the checker agrees; table-driven fixtures under fixtures/evolve/ (old.szs/new.szs/expected_compat/violation_codes); a regression suite asserting all previously-compatible schema pairs still pass after checker changes.

7.3 Deliverable

  • ser-evolve crate: CompatChecker::run returns structured CompatReport.
  • SchemaRegistry stores + retrieves versioned schemas.
  • ser-cli evolve check exits with correct code; report contains violation type + field id.
  • ser-runtime strict-evolution mode rejects decode on schema incompatibility.
  • evolve.check_ns telemetry emitted per check.
  • All 7 rule impls passing fixture suite.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p ser-evolve 100% pass
All fixture pairs classified correctly 0 misclassifications
Proptest: 10,000 random schema mutations Checker agrees with oracle ≥ 99.9%
evolve.check_ns for 100-type schema < 2 ms
SchemaRegistry round-trip (write → read) Schemas byte-identical
Old writer + new reader (absent new fields) Returns schema default; no SerError
New writer + old reader (unknown fields) Old reader ignores silently; no SerError
Unknown union discriminant Returns SerError::UnknownVariant; not panic
Reserved hole reuse attempt CompatViolation::NoOffsetReuse emitted

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture pair Purpose Expected
evolve/add_optional_field/{v1,v2}.szs New optional field with default compat=true
evolve/remove_field/{v1,v2}.szs Field removed without reserved hole compat=false; FieldIdPreserved violation
evolve/reuse_offset/{v1,v2}.szs New field at reserved-hole offset compat=false; NoOffsetReuse violation
evolve/add_union_arm/{v1,v2}.szs New arm appended to union compat=true
evolve/remove_union_arm/{v1,v2}.szs Arm removed from union compat=false; UnionArmAppendOnly violation
evolve/change_field_type/{v1,v2}.szs I32 field changed to I64 compat=false; FieldIdPreserved violation

evolve/registry_sequence.bin 5-version registry; all adjacent pairs All pairs checked; first incompatible pair identified
evolve/unknown_discriminant.bin Encoded message with union arm not in reader schema Decode returns SerError::UnknownVariant

7.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug / signal Recovery
Silent forward-compat failure Old reader decodes new message but returns stale/zero data New field added without since_version+default enforcement in checker Accessor returns 0 (zero-default), indistinguishable from a valid zero NewFieldOptional rule enforced at registration; checker rejects missing default
Offset arithmetic error in reserved holes Reserved hole covers the wrong byte range Offset is in words (8 bytes) but the hole is marked in bytes, or vice versa NoOffsetReuse passes when it should fail Offset always in words; unit-tested arithmetic
Schema version integer overflow since_version: u32 wraps on pathological history Automated version bumping has no ceiling check since_version appears smaller than expected; field treated as legacy Registry rejects since_version > MAX_SCHEMA_VERSION (u32::MAX-1 sentinel)
CompatChecker O(n²) rule application Check time exceeds SLA on large schemas Naive nested iteration over all type pairs per rule evolve.check_ns spikes; profiler shows rule iteration hot Index old schema by TypeId+FieldId for O(1) lookup; O(n) total
Registry read with future format Older ser-evolve cannot read a newer registry file No format-version header on the registry file Deserialization error on load Registry file embeds a format-version header; mismatch → SerError::IncompatibleSchema

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • Check latency: CompatChecker::run must be < 2 ms for 100-type schemas; index-based lookups mandatory.
  • Registry lock contention: if registry is behind RwLock in a long-running service, write-lock on register blocks all reads; use ArcSwap<SchemaRegistry> for lock-free reads.
  • Default evaluation overhead: eval_default called per-field per-decode for absent fields; cache default values in Schema at parse time; avoid re-evaluation on hot path.
  • Proptest generation: schema mutation space is large; bound proptest to 10,000 cases in CI; use --test-threads=4 for parallelism.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Schema with 0 types: compat check passes trivially; no violations.
  • Both schemas identical: compat check passes; 0 violations, 0 warnings.
  • since_version = 0 on a field that didn't exist in v0 → violation (field must have since_version > 0 if added later).
  • Union with 0 arms in old schema → any arms in new schema are valid appends.
  • Field with WireType::Void removed → reserved hole required even for void fields (id space preserved).
  • Two consecutive violations on same field id (e.g., both type change and offset change) → both CompatViolation records emitted; not deduplicated.
  • Registry register race (two concurrent writers at same version) → BTreeMap behind Mutex; second writer returns SerError::SchemaInvalid (duplicate version).

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Incompatible schema deployed without check strict_evolution decode failure at runtime All messages decoded with new schema Circuit-break: fall back to reflective + old schema Enforce evolve check in deploy pipeline
Registry file corrupted Deserialization error on load Service cannot load schemas Restore from backup registry Registry writes are atomic (tmp + rename)
False-negative compat (violation missed) Field reads wrong data; no error Silent data corruption for affected fields Strict mode + round-trip tests catch Add rule regression fixture; re-run checker
eval_default returns wrong type Type mismatch at accessor Absent field returns garbled bytes Type-checked DefaultVal enum Unit test each WireType default
UnknownVariant not returned on unknown discriminant Panic or UB Reader crash match on discriminant must have catch-all arm Fuzzer covers unknown discriminant path

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: evolve.check_ns (histogram), evolve.violations_total (counter), evolve.warnings_total (counter), evolve.registry_versions (gauge).
  • Tracing: RUST_LOG=ser_evolve=info logs each schema pair check + verdict; =debug logs per-rule result.
  • Deployment: evolve check runs in CI pre-merge; violations block merge; warnings require explicit --allow-warnings flag.
  • Migration: schema registry stored alongside service binary; version pinned in deploy config; rollback = redeploy prior registry + binary.
  • Compatibility matrix: maintain compat_matrix.md (auto-generated by ser-cli evolve matrix) showing which version pairs are compatible.
  • Audit: CompatReport serialized to JSON + stored per deploy; postmortem can replay checker against historical schema pair.

20. Phase 8 — Variable-width + advanced types — strings/bytes, nested lists, list-of-struct, maps, packed scalar lists, optional/nullable, default-value elision

8.1 Objectives

  • Extend ser-encode, ser-decode, ser-layout, ser-schema to support: WireType::Text (UTF-8 string), WireType::Bytes (raw bytes), nested List<List<T>>, List<Struct<T>>, map encoding (as List<Entry<K,V>>), packed scalar lists, optional/nullable fields, and default-value elision.
  • Invariants introduced: Text/Bytes pointers → List<U8> on wire (Text always valid UTF-8 post-validation, NUL counted but not exposed to the accessor; Bytes has no encoding constraint beyond bounds); nested lists (List<List<T>>) are an outer list of pointers, each resolving to a List<T>, recursion bounded by MAX_NESTING_DEPTH; List<Struct<T>> is contiguous struct data sections followed by the pointer section, repeated count times; maps are sorted List<Entry<K,V>> with scalar/Text keys, deterministic ascending-wire-byte order; WireType::PackedList(elem) varint-encodes integral-scalar elements, decoder unpacks into a temporary slice (single allocation, not zero-copy — explicit API distinction); optional/nullable fields use Field.default = DefaultVal::Null with a null pointer value and an Option<T> accessor; default-value elision omits the pointer (writes null) when the field value equals the schema default, and the decoder returns the default on a null read.
  • Scope: changes span ser-schema, ser-layout, ser-encode, ser-decode, ser-codegen; ser-runtime exposes new APIs.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P1 (binary-native), P9 (append-only), I8 (text is UTF-8 validated), ADR-06 (fixed-width default, packed opt-in = ADR-30), ADR-30 (packed integer mode); B7 §5 (type system expressiveness), R3 (Protocol Buffers — nullable + default value handling), §8.11 Schema Evolution Worked Example.

ser-schema extensions: WireType::Text/Bytes fully parsed from IDL; WireType::List(Box<WireType>) supports nesting (List(List(U64))); WireType::Map{key,val} desugars during layout to List(Struct(Entry<K,V>)); new WireType::PackedList(Box<WireType>) restricted to integral scalars + Bool; Field.nullable (pointer field may carry a null pointer, accessor returns Option<T>) and Field.elide_default (encoder skips writing when value == default) flags added; validator rejects PackedList(Struct(..)) and non-scalar/Text map keys.

ser-layout extensions: Text/Bytes are always 1 pointer word with 0 data-section contribution; List<T> is 1 pointer word with the inner allocation computed at encode time (dynamic, not layout time); List<Struct<T>> size = count*(data_words+ptr_words)*8 computed recursively; maps layout as List<Entry<K,V>> where Entry is a synthetic struct added to Schema.types at layout time; PackedList stores one pointer word with data size deferred (varint-dependent); nullable fields keep their normal layout, with the null pointer (offset=0/count=0, or the special null struct tag) as a valid value.

ser-encode extensions (ser-encode/src/variable.rs): write_text validates UTF-8 and writes bytes + NUL as List<U8>; write_bytes writes raw bytes with no NUL; write_list_of_list allocates the outer pointer list and recursively calls write_list per inner slice; write_list_of_struct allocates one contiguous data+ptr block and writes each struct inline; write_map sorts entries by ascending key wire-bytes before writing as List<Entry>; write_packed_list varint-encodes each element into a packed byte stream stored as List<U8> tagged packed-list; write_field_if_not_default compares the value to Field.default and writes a null pointer when equal and elide_default=true.

ser-decode extensions (ser-decode/src/variable.rs + accessors_variable.rs): validate_text bounds-checks the List<U8> and validates UTF-8 + NUL; validate_nested_list recurses with a decrementing depth, returning DepthExceeded at 0; validate_list_of_struct validates each inline element; validate_map validates the List<Entry> and enforces monotone-ascending key order; validate_packed_list decodes and bounds-checks the varint stream. Accessors: read_text/read_bytes cast to &'a str/&'a [u8] post-validation (zero-copy); read_list_of_struct returns a lazy StructListView; read_packed_list allocates a Vec<i64> (documented, not zero-copy); read_nullable returns the supplied default on a null pointer, else calls the read function.

ser-codegen extensions: Text→&'a str, Bytes→&'a [u8], List<Struct<T>>→StructListView<'a,TReader<'a>>, PackedList→Vec<i64> (documented allocation), nullable→Option<T> wrapper with a null check before dispatch, map→MapView<'a,K,V> with get(key) via binary search on the sorted list.

Test harness: ser-encode/tests/variable_types.rs (round-trip each new type), ser-decode/tests/validate_variable.rs (malformed inputs per validator), ser-bench/benches/variable.rs (throughput for read_text/read_list_of_struct/read_packed_list).

8.3 Deliverable

  • ser-encode + ser-decode + ser-layout + ser-schema all extended; cargo test passes.
  • read_text returns &'a str zero-copy; read_bytes returns &'a [u8] zero-copy.
  • read_packed_list returns Vec<i64> with documented allocation; benchmark in ser-bench.
  • Map round-trip: encode sorted entries, decode with MapView::get (binary search).
  • validate_nested_list rejects depth > MAX_NESTING_DEPTH; returns SerError::DepthExceeded.
  • Default-value elision: null pointer written for default-valued optional fields; decoder returns default.
  • ser-codegen emits correct accessor signatures for all new types.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p ser-encode -p ser-decode -p ser-layout 100% pass
read_text round-trip (UTF-8 + emoji) Byte-identical output
validate_text on invalid UTF-8 SerError::BadPointer or custom Utf8Error
validate_nested_list at MAX_NESTING_DEPTH + 1 SerError::DepthExceeded
Map round-trip: 1000-entry map, get 100 random keys All values correct
read_packed_list throughput (1M elements) > 200 MB/s decode
read_text throughput (1 MB string) > 1 GB/s (memcpy-free cast)
