8.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug / signal Recovery
read_text lifetime trap Use-after-free if an unsafe cast bypasses the lifetime Reader<'a,T>'s 'a not actually tied to the owning buffer Borrow-checker error in safe Rust; UB if bypassed Tie 'a to the owning Message/&'a [u8] in the API signature
Packed-list amplification Huge Vec allocated during decode Varint-encoded count not validated against segment bounds first OOM or latency spike; decode.alloc_bytes spikes Validate byte-count against segment size before allocating; OutOfBounds on failure
Map sort-order not enforced at encode validate_map rejects on decode with no clear encode-time signal Sort lives only in write_map; direct Builder calls bypass it Decode-time rejection, confusing at encode time validate_map enforces the sort invariant; ser-tool diagnoses unsorted maps
Nested-list pointer cycle Stack overflow in the validator Depth counter not threaded through far-pointer resolution in validate_nested_list Stack overflow before the depth cap engages Thread the depth counter through every recursive validate call (MAX_NESTING_DEPTH=64)
Elision of a non-zero default confuses bridges A JSON/RPC bridge reads a null pointer as "missing" rather than "default" Elision is a wire-level optimization invisible to downstream systems Bridge output differs from direct decode Elide only when elide_default=true; document the semantic for bridge authors
List<Struct> element stride error All elements after the first are corrupt Layout stride miscomputed for nested union types Round-trip test fails past element 0 validate_list_of_struct checks each element at stride*i; compare stride to Layout.data_words+ptr_words

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • read_text / read_bytes: pure pointer cast + length check; should achieve memory-bandwidth throughput (> 10 GB/s for cached data); no allocation.
  • read_packed_list: varint decode is compute-bound; SIMD not required for MSRV 1.78 but loop-unrolling helps; allocation per call → avoid on hot path; document "not zero-copy".
  • validate_text UTF-8 check: std::str::from_utf8 on large strings is O(n); cache validation result in ValidatedMessage flag; do not re-validate on each accessor call.
  • Map get binary search: O(log n) per lookup; for hot maps, caller should cache result or iterate MapView once.
  • List-of-struct iteration: sequential access is cache-friendly (contiguous struct data); pointer section follows data section; pointer fields cause random access if structs contain pointers.
  • Nested-list validation: depth-first traversal; deep nesting → recursion stack; use iterative stack if MAX_NESTING_DEPTH > 64.
  • Default-value elision savings: large optional struct fields with defaults can save significant wire bytes; encoder should elide aggressively when elide_default=true; measure wire-size reduction in ser-bench.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty string (""): write_text("") writes 1-byte list (null terminator only); read_text returns "".
  • Empty bytes (&[]): write_bytes(&[]) writes 0-byte list; read_bytes returns &[].
  • List<List<T>> with 0 outer elements: outer pointer count = 0; validation passes; iterator yields nothing.
  • List<Struct<T>> with 0 elements: valid; len() = 0; no struct data written.
  • Map with 0 entries: valid; get always returns None.
  • Map with duplicate keys: validate_map rejects (strict monotone ascending; equal key = violation) → SerError::BadPointer.
  • Packed list with 0 elements: valid; read_packed_list returns empty Vec.
  • Nullable pointer in non-pointer field (scalar): invalid; Field.nullable only meaningful for pointer-typed fields; schema validator rejects.
  • elide_default on a field with DefaultVal::None: encoder cannot elide (no defined default); write_field_if_not_default falls through to always write.
  • Text field longer than segment capacity: write_text allocates new segment via arena; far pointer emitted automatically.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Invalid UTF-8 in Text field validate_text → SerError Message rejected Validation gate (trust boundary) Log raw bytes for debug; reject message
Packed list OOM amplification SerError::OutOfBounds or OOM Decoder process heap Count validated before alloc Return error; no allocation attempted
Map sort-order violation validate_map → SerError::BadPointer Message rejected Validation gate ser-tool diagnose; re-encode sorted
Nested list depth exceeded SerError::DepthExceeded Message rejected Validation gate Log depth; limit nesting in schema
List-of-struct stride wrong Corrupt field reads for elements > 0 All structs after first Round-trip test + fixture Fix layout stride; add regression fixture
Default elision + missing default in schema Decoder returns zero (zero-default) instead of intended non-zero default Incorrect data returned Type-check default at schema registration Enforce DefaultVal present when elide_default=true

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (via ser-telemetry): encode.text_bytes (histogram), decode.text_validate_ns (histogram), decode.packed_alloc_bytes (histogram), decode.nested_depth_max (gauge), decode.map_entries (histogram).
  • Tracing: RUST_LOG=ser_decode=debug logs each variable-width field validation + byte range.
  • Packed list: documented in public API as "not zero-copy; allocates on each call"; callers on hot paths should cache or avoid.
  • Map sort invariant: enforced at both encode (sort in write_map) and decode (validate sort); deterministic sort = canonical encoding compatible.
  • Deployment: no wire-format break from adding new WireType variants if old readers skip unknown pointer fields (evolution-safe); Text/Bytes/PackedList variants must be in schema from day 1 or added via evolution rules.
  • Rollback: if PackedList encoding is removed, old writers cannot produce it; old readers with DepthExceeded/BadPointer on unrecognized packed-list pointer → graceful error, not crash.
  • Monitoring alert: decode.packed_alloc_bytes p99 > 10 MB → alert; indicates amplification risk or pathological message.

21. Phase 9 — Cross-language + endianness + conformance — fixed wire endianness, canonical-form interop fixtures, generated readers in a second language read the same bytes

9.1 Objectives

  • Fix wire endianness: all scalar fields little-endian on wire; all platforms must produce/consume identical bytes.
  • Implement canonical form: byte-identical output for logically-equal messages (fixed field order, zeroed padding, no dead space, sorted union tags).
  • Generate reader/builder accessors for a second target language (Python or C reference impl) via ser-codegen; those accessors must read bytes produced by ser-encode without any modification.
  • Publish a canonical conformance fixture suite: .szs schema + encoded .bin + decoded .json for every primitive type and compound type; third-party impls validate against these.
  • Establish a cross-platform CI matrix (x86-64, aarch64, wasm32) verifying bit-identical output.
  • Constraint introduced: all new layouts and pointer encodings must be endianness-annotated in ser-core; no host-endian aliases allowed in the public API.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P5 (canonical encoding), I1 (byte-identical canonical output), I2 (layout determinism), ADR-04 (little-endian fixed), ADR-26 (reproducible builds = platform-stable output); B4 §7 (cross-platform data interchange), R1 (Cap'n Proto — conformance test suite design).

Endianness (ser-core/src/endian.rs): read_u16/u32/u64_le, write_u16/u32/u64_le as #[inline(always)] wrappers over from_le_bytes/to_le_bytes (no byteorder dep needed); every Offset/FieldId/TypeId/Pointer wire repr annotated #[repr(C)] with // wire: little-endian u32; CanonicalFlags{zeroed_padding,sorted_fields,no_dead_segments} added for the canonical pass.

Canonical pass (ser-encode/src/canonical.rs): canonical_encode(msg, schema, flags) -> Vec<u8> visits fields in FieldId ascending order, zeroes all padding, collapses unused pointer slots to null, and strips trailing empty segments; it is a separate pass over an already-built Message (the builder itself stays flexible-order), returning SchemaInvalid if the schema hasn't been through ser-layout yet.

Python backend (ser-codegen/src/python_backend.rs, new): emit_python(schema) -> String emits dataclass-like class FooReader readers whose properties call struct.unpack_from('<H', buf, base+offset) (little-endian format strings); Python readers must accept any .bin ser-encode produces for the same schema; the Rust backend is untouched, both sharing a CodegenBackend trait (fn emit(&self, schema, td) -> String).

Schema hash (ser-schema/src/hash.rs): SchemaHash = 64-bit FNV-1a over the canonical serialization of Schema (type names + field ids + offsets, sorted by TypeId then FieldId) — deterministic across platforms.

Cross-platform CI (.github/workflows/conformance.yml): matrix [x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu, x86_64-apple-darwin, wasm32-unknown-unknown]; cargo run -p ser-tool -- fixture-check fixtures/conformance/ compares generated .bin against stored golden .bin via sha256sum, failing on mismatch.

Fixture generator (ser-tool/src/fixture_gen.rs): generate_fixtures(schema_dir, out_dir) encodes canonical form and writes .bin + .json (reflective decode) per .szs, stored in fixtures/conformance/ under git LFS.

Benchmark (ser-bench/benches/canonical.rs): canonical_encode on 1 KB / 64 KB / 1 MB messages, reporting MB/s; regression gate ≥ 400 MB/s on x86-64.

9.3 Deliverable

  • ser-encode canonical mode: canonical_encode() callable, tested on all primitive + compound types.
  • Python backend in ser-codegen: emit_python() produces runnable Python 3.10+ readers.
  • Fixture suite: fixtures/conformance/ — ≥ 20 .szs/.bin/.json triples covering all WireType variants.
  • CI matrix: 4 platforms produce identical .bin SHA-256 for every fixture.
  • ser-tool fixture-check: CLI subcommand for third-party validation.
  • SchemaHash deterministic across platforms.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Gate
cargo test -p ser-encode canonical_ all pass
cargo test -p ser-codegen python_ Python readers decode all fixture .bin correctly
SHA-256 of each fixture .bin identical on x86-64 / aarch64 / wasm32 100% match
canonical_encode throughput ≥ 400 MB/s on x86-64 release
Fixture coverage ≥ 20 distinct schemas; all WireType variants present
SchemaHash determinism test identical hash on LE + BE target cross-compile
Python reader round-trip test encode in Rust, decode in Python, assert field equality
ser-tool fixture-check exits 0 on all stored fixtures CI enforced

9.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug Signal Recovery
Host-endian u32 leak Correct on x86, wrong on BE cross-compile u32::from_ne_bytes instead of from_le_bytes CI aarch64 BE cross-compile SHA mismatch in matrix Replace with from_le_bytes
Padding non-zero in canonical Hash mismatch for logically-equal msgs Builder wrote struct alignment pad without zeroing xxd diff of two canonical encodes canonical_verify test fail Explicitly zero pad range in canonical_encode
Python struct format endian mismatch Python reader returns wrong scalar values struct.unpack_from('>H', ...) instead of '<H' Python unit test on known bytes Decimal value differs Fix format string to <
SchemaHash platform divergence Cache/registry collision across platforms Non-deterministic hash input order (HashMap iteration) Log input bytes before FNV Hash value logged Switch schema field iteration to sorted BTreeMap
wasm32 alignment trap Misaligned on WASM in valid buffer WASM memory.grow returns unaligned base; arena base not 8-aligned console.error trap WASM host exception Assert base % 8 == 0 in arena init
Canonical encode on un-laid-out schema SchemaInvalid panic instead of Err Caller skipped ser-layout Backtrace shows layout field None Missing layout log Document precondition; return Err(SerError::SchemaInvalid)

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • canonical_encode walks all fields twice (layout + write); for large messages keep a single-pass write with deferred pad-zero using buf.fill(0) over pad ranges.
  • Python backend is emit-time only (code gen); no runtime performance impact on Rust paths.
  • Fixture SHA-256 check in CI: use incremental hash (sha2 crate or sha256sum binary); do not load entire LFS tree into RAM.
  • SchemaHash computation is O(types * fields); cache hash in Schema after first compute; not on hot path.
  • Cross-compile matrix: use cross (QEMU); aarch64 emulation is 5-10x slower; limit matrix to release builds, not debug.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty struct (zero data words, zero ptr words): canonical form = 8-byte struct pointer + 0-byte data; Python reader must handle zero-length structs without IndexError.
  • Message with all-default field values: canonical encoding must still emit data section (no elision of default-value structs).
  • SchemaHash collision: two different schemas producing same hash; mitigated by 64-bit FNV + full field coverage; collision treated as IncompatibleSchema.
  • wasm32 lacks 64-bit atomic support; SchemaHash computation uses regular u64 arithmetic, no atomics needed here.
  • Python codegen for union: discriminant read must use the union tag offset, not field 0 offset; off-by-word bug common.
  • Fixture .bin under git LFS: if LFS not configured, fixture-check gets pointer file, not bytes; CI must install LFS before checkout.

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery Operator visibility
Endianness bug in production encoder SHA mismatch in CI matrix All messages unreadable on BE hosts Block merge Fix from_le_bytes, re-run CI CI diff log
Canonical encode divergence canonical_verify test fails Hash-based dedup broken Warn + fall back to non-canonical Fix zeroing pass canonical_mismatch_total counter
Python reader wrong field value Python integration test fail Cross-language consumers receive corrupt data Block codegen release Fix format string Test output diff
LFS misconfiguration CI fails at fixture-check with pointer file Conformance CI blind Fail loudly with error Add LFS install step CI log: pointer file detected

9.10 Operational Notes

  • ser-telemetry: add canonical_encode_duration_us histogram; tag by message size bucket (1KB/64KB/1MB).
  • Fixture check CLI: ser-tool fixture-check --dir fixtures/conformance/ --verbose prints pass/fail per fixture with SHA.
  • Schema registry (future): SchemaHash is the key; Phase 9 lays the hash foundation; registry deferred to ser-tool Phase 13+.
  • Deployment: canonical encode is opt-in flag; existing callers unaffected; enable per-message-type in config.
  • Migration: if canonical form encoding changes (e.g., new zeroing rule), bump CANONICAL_VERSION constant in ser-core; older decoders reject CANONICAL_VERSION > known.
  • Rollback: revert ser-encode canonical pass is safe; non-canonical messages still valid.

22. Phase 10 — Performance — zero-copy fast paths, alignment for SIMD-friendly access, builder arena reuse, copy-vs-view tradeoffs, batching, allocation profile

10.1 Objectives

  • Instrument and optimize the encode/decode/validate hot paths to hit stated throughput targets.
  • Arena reuse: Builder arenas reset without deallocation; amortize segment allocation across messages.
  • SIMD-friendly alignment: data sections aligned to 64 bytes (cache line) when the schema layout allows; enables auto-vectorized list scans.
  • Establish copy-vs-view policy: document and enforce when Reader returns a borrowed view vs. when it must deep-copy (e.g., lists of primitives are views; callers who need owned data call explicit to_owned()).
  • Batching: bulk encode/decode API (encode_batch, decode_batch) amortizes framing overhead.
  • Allocation profile: ser-encode must produce ≤ 2 allocations per message for common cases (single-segment path); zero allocations in ser-decode accessor path.
  • Constraints: no unsafe added beyond existing validated cast sites; SIMD intrinsics via safe wrappers (std::simd nightly gate, or portable-simd feature flag only in ser-bench, not production path).

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P11 (zero-allocation decode path), P3 (arena reuse), P12 (copy-vs-view policy), I3 (arena monotonic — reset-not-free), ADR-10 (system allocator + jemalloc opt-in); B8 §4 (CPU cache behavior), B3 §8 (allocation amortization), P1 (Drepper "What Every Programmer Should Know About Memory" §3 — cache line alignment).

Arena reuse (ser-encode/src/arena.rs): Arena{slab: Vec<u8>, cursor, segments: Vec<(usize,usize)>} — single slab pre-allocated via with_capacity(n), segments are (start,len) slices into it; reset() sets cursor=0 and clears the segment vec without freeing the slab, for reuse across messages; alloc_aligned(size,align) bumps the cursor to the next alignment boundary then advances by size; Builder::reset() delegates to Arena::reset. A BuilderPool (src/pool.rs) with acquire()/release() behind a Mutex, capped at MAX_POOL_SIZE (default 64), pools Builders across messages.

Batching (ser-encode/src/batch.rs, ser-decode/src/fast_path.rs): encode_batch(msgs, out) encodes all messages into out contiguously (single allocation), appending standard per-message framing and returning byte offsets. decode_batch(buf, schema) parses the framing, validates each message, and returns a Vec of zero-copy Readers into buf; a single-segment fast path (validate_single_segment) skips the SegmentId map allocation and inlines pointer resolution to direct offset arithmetic when segments.len()==1.

Cache-line alignment (ser-core/src/layout.rs): Layout::cache_line_aligned() -> bool is true when (data_words*8) % 64 == 0; when a schema option requests it, ser-layout pads data_words up to the next 8-word (64-byte) boundary, warning if the padding exceeds 25% of the struct.

Benchmarks (ser-bench/benches/throughput.rs, criterion with throughput(Bytes(n))): encode_1k/64k/1m, decode_validate_1k, decode_access_1k, arena_reuse_1k, batch_encode_1k_x100, targeting (x86-64 release, Zen3-class):

Benchmark Target
encode_1k ≥ 1.5 GB/s
encode_64k ≥ 2.0 GB/s
decode_validate_1k ≥ 2.0 GB/s
decode_access (field read) ≤ 5 ns/field
arena_reuse vs fresh ≥ 3x speedup
batch_encode_1k_x100 ≥ 10% better than 100x single

Telemetry & accessors: ser-telemetry/src/metrics.rs adds histograms encode_duration_us/decode_duration_us/validate_duration_us/arena_alloc_bytes/arena_reuse_count and gauges builder_pool_size/builder_pool_hit_rate. ser-decode/src/accessor.rs: get_u64 is an inline, branch-free u64::from_le_bytes read; get_list_u32 returns a zero-copy &'a [u32] slice via the already-validated pointer chase; copy_struct is the explicit opt-in deep-copy escape hatch (OwnedStruct).

10.3 Deliverable

  • Arena::reset + BuilderPool: measurable via arena_reuse_1k bench showing ≥ 3x vs. fresh Arena.
  • encode_batch / decode_batch: API available; batch bench shows ≥ 10% improvement over loop of singles.
  • Cache-line alignment option: schema flag propagates to ser-layout; layout test verifies padding.
  • All criterion benchmarks in ser-bench; CI runs on each push, stores baseline, fails on >10% regression.
  • ser-telemetry metrics exported to Prometheus-compatible /metrics endpoint (via ser-tool --serve).

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Gate

cargo bench -p ser-bench decode_validate_1k ≥ 2.0 GB/s
cargo bench -p ser-bench decode_access ≤ 5 ns/field

10.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug Signal Recovery
Arena slab reuse with stale bytes Reader reads garbage from previous message reset did not zero slab; builder re-uses slab offsets Valgrind / Miri use-of-uninitialized Decode returns wrong field values Zero slab on reset (or track written ranges)
Pool grows unbounded under bursty load Memory RSS grows without bound release never called on builder from dropped future builder_pool_size gauge high OOM Implement Drop on pooled builder handle
SIMD flag on non-portable build Compile error on MSRV 1.78 stable portable-simd is nightly; linked in production crate Build fail CI stable toolchain fail Gate behind #[cfg(feature="simd-bench")] in ser-bench only
Batch decode OOB on malformed concatenation OutOfBounds on second message Framing length field of message 1 wrong; cursor overruns Log cursor position per message Truncated error Validate each frame header before advancing cursor
Cache-line padding > 25% Layout bloat silent Struct with few fields padded to 64B ser-layout warning suppressed Field density drops Emit compile-time warning; require opt-in annotation
Criterion baseline missing in CI Bench runs but no regression check benches/baseline/ not committed CI prints "no baseline" Regression blind Commit baseline JSON; CI step fails if missing
get_list_u32 on non-u32 element type Returns garbled slice Accessor doesn't check element WireType Access of wrong type returns non-panicking garbage Downstream logic error Check element type in accessor; return Err or panic in debug

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Cache locality: struct data section contiguous in slab; pointer section immediately follows; pointer chase to nested struct crosses cache line if nesting is deep; flatten hot nested structs in schema when possible.
  • Arena alloc: bump pointer in single slab = no syscall, no lock; pool mutex only on acquire/release (not on per-field write).
  • Batch framing overhead: framing header is 4 + 4*N bytes (count + per-segment sizes); for N=100 messages, negligible vs. payload.
  • decode_batch vector: allocates Vec<Reader> of length N; for N > 1000, consider streaming iterator API to avoid O(N) allocation.
  • Tail latency: P99 latency dominated by slab growth (first message in fresh arena); warm pool eliminates this; monitor arena_reuse_count / encode_duration_us P99.
  • from_le_bytes inline: compiler generates movzx + byte-swap on BE, native load on LE; no branch.
  • Lock contention on pool: under high parallelism, pool Mutex contends; shard by thread-id (N shards = N hardware threads) using thread_local BuilderPool.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Arena reset called while borrowed Builder still alive: compile error (Rust lifetime); enforce Builder not cloneable.
  • Zero-length list: decode_batch with empty buf returns empty Vec<Reader>; not an error.
  • encode_batch with 0 messages: returns Ok(vec![]), writes nothing to out.
  • Cache-line alignment on struct with 0 data words (pointer-only struct): align to 8 bytes (one pointer word), not 64 bytes.
  • Pool MAX_POOL_SIZE exceeded: release drops the builder (deallocates slab); no panic; gauge decremented.
  • get_list_u32 on list with count = 0: returns empty slice &[]; not OOB.
  • Arena slab capacity exceeded mid-message: alloc_aligned must either grow slab (new segment) or return Err(SerError::SchemaInvalid) — growing is the correct path; document max message size.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery Operator visibility
Throughput regression > 10% CI criterion gate All encode/decode callers slower Block merge Profile with perf/flamegraph, fix hot path Criterion HTML report artifact
Arena stale-byte read Miri / Valgrind in test suite Corrupt field values; data integrity failure Gate on cfg(test) zero-on-reset Add explicit zero pass decode_corrupt_total counter
Pool OOM builder_pool_size gauge + RSS growth Process OOM Drop oldest pooled builder on overflow Tune MAX_POOL_SIZE pool_drop_total counter
Batch framing parse error Truncated or OutOfBounds in decode_batch All messages in batch rejected Return error with message index Resend from source batch_frame_error_total counter

10.10 Operational Notes

  • Expose Arena::stats() -> ArenaStats { alloc_count, total_bytes, reuse_count } for telemetry.
  • ser-telemetry counters: encode_total, encode_bytes_total, decode_total, decode_bytes_total, validate_reject_total, arena_reuse_total, pool_hit_total, pool_miss_total.
  • Criterion JSON baselines committed to benches/baseline/; updated intentionally with cargo criterion --save-baseline new.
  • Deployment: BuilderPool is optional; default path creates Builder per message; pool opt-in via ser_runtime::set_global_pool(pool).
  • Flamegraph generation: cargo flamegraph -p ser-bench --bench throughput produces flamegraph.svg; attach to performance regression tickets.
  • MSRV guard: portable-simd feature must be cfg(nightly); stable build must not require it; CI tests both stable MSRV 1.78 and nightly.

23. Phase 11 — Safety hardening — untrusted-buffer validation depth, recursion/segment limits, amplification (small bytes -> huge logical) defense, unsafe-cast soundness, fuzz-driven bounds

11.1 Objectives

  • Harden ser-decode validation pass as the sole trust boundary: every byte of an untrusted buffer is validated before any accessor can read it.
  • Enforce recursion depth limit (MAX_NESTING_DEPTH = 64), total segment count (MAX_SEGMENTS = 512), and total message size (MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES = 512 MiB) as non-negotiable hard limits.
  • Amplification defense: a message claiming a list of 2^32 elements from 8 bytes of wire data must be rejected; all count fields validated against segment bounds before acceptance.
  • Audit every unsafe block in ser-decode and ser-encode; document each with a SAFETY: comment; add #[forbid(unsafe_code)] to all other crates.
  • Fuzz ser-decode with cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer); target: validate entry point; no panics, no UB, no OOB, only Err(SerError::*). Constraint: zero new unsafe blocks in Phase 11; existing unsafe reduced or eliminated where possible.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P6 (validate-then-view), P7 (trust boundary), I5 (post-validation safety), I6 (max nesting 64), I9 (max segments 512), I10 (max message 512 MiB), ADR-17 (unsafe encapsulation), ADR-18 (sanitizers); P11 (Cap'n Proto 2018 advisory — traversal limits CVE), R4 (rkyv check_bytes trust model), §6.5 Bounded Resource Usage.

Validation context (ser-decode/src/validation.rs): ValidationCtx{depth: u8, seg_visit: [bool; MAX_SEGMENTS], total_bytes_visited: u64} is a stack context threaded through recursive pointer resolution. validate(buf, schema, opts) parses the framing header (segment count ≤ MAX_SEGMENTS, summed segment size ≤ MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES, every segment size > 0), then calls validate_ptr(root, ctx), which increments ctx.depth (returning DepthExceeded past MAX_NESTING_DEPTH), resolves and alignment-checks the target word, and recurses; after validation, visited segments are marked in seg_visit (unvisited segments are a warning, not an error, for forward compat). ValidationOpts{max_depth,max_segments,max_bytes,reject_far_ptr} lets callers tighten limits. validate_list_count(count,elem_size,seg_len) rejects when count as usize * elem_size > seg_len — the amplification check. validate_far_ptr requires the target to land within its declared segment (landing in an already-visited segment is allowed for double-far layout; single-level cycle detection uses seg_visit).

Unsafe audit (ser-decode/src/unsafe_audit.rs): every unsafe block carries a // SAFETY: [precondition] validated in validate() at line N comment; the pattern is always unsafe { &*(buf.as_ptr().add(offset) as *const T) } only after bounds+alignment are verified; #[deny(clippy::undocumented_unsafe_blocks)] is set crate-wide; safe_cast::<T>(buf,offset) (checking offset+size_of::<T>()<=buf.len() and align_offset) is the single cast site; every other crate carries #[forbid(unsafe_code)].

Limits (ser-decode/src/limits.rs): MAX_NESTING_DEPTH=64, MAX_SEGMENTS=512, MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES=512MiB; SegmentBudget tracks remaining bytes across segments, deduct(n) returning OutOfBounds when exhausted.

Fuzz targets (fuzz/fuzz_targets/): fuzz_validate.rs calls ser_decode::validate on arbitrary bytes and must never panic; fuzz_encode_decode.rs generates an ArbitraryMessage, encodes, mutates bytes, and validates, asserting a clean encode always validates; fuzz_schema_parse.rs fuzzes the IDL parser for panics; run 1 hour locally to seed the corpus, then 60 s/PR in CI.

Encoder-side bounds (ser-encode/src/bounds_check.rs): Builder::alloc_data(words) checks words*8 <= arena.remaining() returning OutOfBounds on overflow; pointer_write validates the target offset stays within the same segment, requiring a far pointer for cross-segment writes.

Telemetry & CI: ser-telemetry/src/security.rs adds validate_reject_{depth,oob,misalign,amplification,truncated}_total counters under a security_ label prefix; .github/workflows/fuzz.yml runs a daily 1-hour fuzz_validate job uploading corpus + crash artifacts on failure; .github/workflows/miri.yml runs cargo miri test -p ser-decode on every PR.

11.3 Deliverable

  • validate() with depth/segment/byte-count limits; rejects all known amplification patterns.
  • safe_cast<T> as sole unsafe cast site in ser-decode; all other crates #[forbid(unsafe_code)].
  • Fuzz corpus: ≥ 1000 seeds; 1-hour run with no crash; CI 60-second fuzz gate.
  • Miri clean: cargo miri test -p ser-decode passes.
  • Security metrics: 5 labeled counters in ser-telemetry.
  • ser-tool validate <file>: CLI subcommand; exits 0 if valid, 1 + human-readable error if not.

Check Gate
cargo miri test -p ser-decode 0 UB findings
cargo fuzz run fuzz_validate -- -max_total_time=3600 0 crashes, 0 panics
cargo clippy -p ser-decode -- -D clippy::undocumented_unsafe_blocks 0 warnings
grep -r 'unsafe' ser-schema/ ser-layout/ ser-encode/ ser-codegen/ ser-evolve/ ser-tool/ ser-telemetry/ 0 results (forbid enforced)

Fixture Purpose Expected

fuzz_seed_depth_65.bin 65 levels of struct nesting DepthExceeded
fuzz_seed_seg_count_513.bin segment count field = 513 OutOfBounds (framing)

11.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug Signal Recovery
Depth counter not reset between sibling fields DepthExceeded on valid wide struct with many fields Depth counter incremented per-field not per-nesting-level Unit test: struct with 100 flat fields False DepthExceeded Reset depth on return from validate_ptr, not increment per field
Amplification via list-of-list Nested list claims O(2^32) elements from few bytes Inner list count not re-validated against segment budget Fuzz input SegmentBudget deduct slow to catch Apply SegmentBudget::deduct at every list level, not just top
safe_cast alignment check uses pointer arithmetic Misaligned on WASM where pointer width is 32-bit buf.as_ptr() as usize wraps on 32-bit WASM CI Misaligned false positive Use buf.as_ptr().align_offset(align_of::<T>()) instead
Far pointer double-far not validated Far pointer landing page itself contains malformed pointer Double-far (two-hop) path skips validation on second hop Fuzzer finds crash BadPointer not returned Explicitly validate landing page pointer in validate_far_ptr
Miri slow on large fuzz inputs CI Miri target times out Miri is 10-100x slower; large seed corpus in unit tests Miri wall-clock log Timeout Run Miri on unit tests only (small inputs); fuzz separately
#[forbid(unsafe_code)] breaks a dependency Crate with unsafe in its impl causes build error Forbid applies transitively to inline macros? Build error Compiler error forbid applies to the crate's own code, not deps; verify behavior
Segment budget double-counting Budget underflows on far pointer resolution Far pointer resolution counts bytes twice Add budget audit log deduct returns spurious OutOfBounds Track visited segments; deduct only once per segment

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Validation pass is O(message_size); it must complete before any accessor; keep it as fast as possible: ≥ 2.0 GB/s throughput (Phase 10 target held here).
  • ValidationCtx on stack (not heap): depth: u8 + seg_visit: [bool; 512] = 513 bytes; fits in stack; no allocation.
  • SegmentBudget::deduct is a subtraction + compare; branch-free on modern CPUs.
  • Fuzz: libFuzzer runs in-process; fuzz target must not hold global state between iterations; use lazy_static schema only if thread-safe.
  • Miri is for unit tests only; do not add Miri to fuzz loop (incompatible).
  • Security counters in ser-telemetry: use AtomicU64::fetch_add (no lock); no performance impact on hot path.
  • Depth limit at 64: max stack depth from validate_ptr recursion = 64 frames × ~200 bytes/frame = 12.8 KB stack; well within default 8 MB stack.

11.8 Edge Cases

  • Pointer with offset = 0: valid (points to first word of segment); not a null pointer; null is represented by a specific sentinel value (all-zero struct pointer with zero data/ptr words = canonical null).
  • Segment with id = 0 and size = 0: rejected (OutOfBounds on zero-size segment).
  • List of void elements: count validated but no bytes consumed (elem_size = 0); avoid zero-denominator in budget math.
  • Text field (UTF-8 + NUL): validate NUL at buf[base + count - 1]; if missing, return BadPointer (malformed text).
  • Schema with no fields (empty struct): valid; layout = 0 data words, 0 ptr words; validation pass = success after struct pointer check.
  • Double-far pointer in single-segment message: not possible by definition; validator must return BadPointer if far pointer segment-id == 0 and message has only one segment and offset is not in-segment.
  • Max valid nesting = 64: test with valid_deeply_nested_64.bin; must return Ok(()), not DepthExceeded.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery Operator visibility
Fuzz crash (panic/OOB) CI fuzz gate fails Any process decoding untrusted input is exploitable Halt decode of all untrusted inputs Fix validation gap; re-run fuzz 1h GitHub issue with repro; fuzz_crash_total counter
Miri UB finding cargo miri test fails Potential memory unsafety in production Block merge Audit unsafe block, add bound check CI log; Miri error message
Amplification bypass Adversarial input causes OOM Process OOM kills service MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES global limit (independent of validation) OS OOM kill then restart validate_reject_amplification_total spike; alert
Depth limit bypass 65+ nesting causes stack overflow Process crash (SIGSEGV) Rust default stack guard page Increase MAX_NESTING_DEPTH only if stack size increased Crash log; validate_reject_depth_total
safe_cast misalignment false negative UB: misaligned read returns garbage Silent data corruption Miri catches Wrong field value Fix alignment check in safe_cast
#[forbid(unsafe_code)] regression New unsafe added to guarded crate Policy violation CI clippy gate Build error CI log

11.10 Operational Notes

  • Alerting: alert on validate_reject_amplification_total rate > 10/min (attack signal); alert on validate_reject_depth_total rate > 100/min.
  • Fuzz corpus management: corpus stored in fuzz/corpus/; after each fuzz run, minimize corpus with cargo fuzz cmin; commit minimized corpus.
  • CVE process: any fuzz crash is treated as a security finding; embargo 90 days; patch + fuzz regress in same PR.
  • Deployment: ValidationOpts configurable per-service; default limits are conservative; do not loosen in production without security review.
  • Logging: on validation reject, log { error: SerError, segment_id, offset, depth } at WARN level; do not log payload bytes (privacy).
  • Rollback: validation is in ser-decode; rollback = revert ser-decode crate version; encoder unaffected.
  • Audit trail: validate_reject_* counters feed SIEM; spike = potential adversarial input; correlate with source IP / client-id in service layer.
  • Testing cadence: run cargo fuzz run fuzz_validate -- -max_total_time=86400 (24h) before each major release; store corpus snapshot tagged with release version.
  • Miri CI: runs on every PR to ser-decode; not on full workspace (too slow); gate enforced in .github/workflows/miri.yml.

24. Phase 12 — Observability + tooling — encode/decode/validate metrics, message inspector/pretty-printer, schema registry, wire-diff tool, tracing

12.1 Objectives

Goal Scope Constraint
Structured metrics for encode/decode/validate paths ser-telemetry + tap hooks in ser-encode, ser-decode, ser-runtime Zero overhead when telemetry disabled (feature-gated, no-op stubs)
OpenTelemetry-compatible spans for pipeline stages ser-telemetry spans: parse, layout, encode, validate, decode Span context injected via TelemetryContext; no forced tokio dep
CLI inspector: pretty-print any wire buffer given a schema ser-tool binary Must work with reflective (non-codegen) path; no schema = hex dump fallback
Wire-diff: compare two serialized messages field-by-field ser-tool wire-diff subcommand Output structurally diff'd JSON or annotated hex; schema-driven
Schema registry: store/retrieve/version schemas by SchemaHash ser-tool + optional ser-schema API On-disk (files) + optional HTTP endpoint; no mandatory DB dep
Rejection counters exposed as Prometheus-style gauge/counter ser-telemetry::metrics Pull-model (scrape endpoint); push model out of scope
Tracing integration for distributed pipeline correlation ser-telemetry::trace tracing crate facade; structured fields: msg_len, seg_count, schema_hash, op

Architectural constraints: all telemetry hooks are #[inline] no-ops behind feature = "telemetry" (cold-path only); ser-telemetry has no knowledge of wire-format internals and hooks receive opaque measurement structs; the schema registry read path is lock-free (RwLock) while writes are serialized; inspector and wire-diff are operator tools, not hot-path — latency guarantees do not apply to them.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P14 (observable internals), ADR-12 (tracing crate), ADR-13 (metrics facade), ADR-22 (schema registry); B9 §6 (distributed tracing fundamentals), B6 §1 (reliability through observability), P13 (USE method for serialization bottlenecks).

ser-telemetry crate — modules: lib.rs (re-exports, feature gate), metrics.rs (MetricsSink trait + AtomicMetrics impl), trace.rs (TelemetrySpan, TelemetryContext, otel bridge), hooks.rs (EncodeHook/DecodeHook/ValidateHook), registry.rs (in-process SchemaRegistry, persisted by ser-tool). MetricsSink::record_encode/decode/validate take EncodeEvent{schema_hash,msg_bytes,seg_count,duration_ns}, DecodeEvent{schema_hash,msg_bytes,validation_passes,validation_failures,duration_ns}, ValidateEvent{result,depth_max,ptr_count,duration_ns}; AtomicMetrics holds one AtomicU64 per counter with a Prometheus-text Display impl; TelemetrySpan wraps tracing::Span (enters on construction, exits on drop); TelemetryContext{span,sink: Arc<dyn MetricsSink>} is held as Option<Arc<TelemetryContext>> on ser-runtime and passed into encode/decode calls.

Hooks: Builder::finish() calls ctx.sink.record_encode(ev) plus an "encode.complete" span event; validate() calls record_validate(ev) plus span events at depth milestones; ser-runtime::decode() wraps the whole operation in a TelemetrySpan with a nested validate child span. Every hook is gated #[cfg(feature = "telemetry")], compiling to nothing without the feature.

ser-tool inspect --schema <file.szs> --input <wire.bin> [--format json|hex|tree]: reads wire.bin as a Message, resolves segments, and runs a reflective decode (ser-tool/src/inspector.rs::inspect(msg,schema,fmt)) that walks TypeDef fields by FieldId, reading data-section scalars and pointer-section targets; output modes are json (nested field:value), tree (indented box-drawing), and hex (annotated hexdump with field offsets); with no schema it falls back to an annotated hex dump of segment boundaries and pointer offsets.

ser-tool diff --schema <file.szs> --a <a.bin> --b <b.bin> [--output diff|patch]: decodes both messages via the reflective path and recursively compares field trees, producing DiffNode::{Same, Changed{field,a_val,b_val}, Added{field}, Removed{field}, ListLenChanged{field,a_len,b_len}} (ser-tool/src/diff.rs::diff_msgs); diff output is a colored/annotated field-level diff, patch output is an RFC-6902-subset JSON patch document.

Schema registry (ser-tool/src/registry.rs): SchemaRegistry{store: RwLock<HashMap<SchemaHash,SchemaEntry>>}, SchemaEntry{hash,schema,registered_at,label}, persisted as <SchemaHash>.szs files under --registry-dir; ser-tool schema-registry push/pull/list/check-compat subcommands, where check-compat loads two schemas by hash and runs ser-evolve::CompatChecker::check(a,b), printing a pass/fail table.

Prometheus + tracing: ser-telemetry/src/prometheus.rs::render_metrics(sink) -> String renders text-format 0.0.4 with no HTTP dependency in the crate itself; ser-tool serve-metrics --port 9090 wraps it with tiny_http. ser-telemetry::trace uses tracing::instrument with structured key=value fields, controlled at runtime via RUST_LOG (e.g. ser_runtime=debug,ser_decode=trace); span hierarchy is runtime.decode → decode.validate → decode.access[field_name].

12.3 Deliverable

  • ser-telemetry crate compiles with/without the telemetry feature, no-op in the cold path; ser-tool inspect produces JSON/hex/tree output for any valid wire buffer + schema pair; ser-tool diff produces a field-level diff for two wire buffers under the same schema; ser-tool schema-registry push/pull/list/check-compat commands are functional.
  • AtomicMetrics::prometheus_text() is scrape-ready, confirmed by parsing with the prometheus text parser; spans are visible in a local jaeger-ui via the opentelemetry-jaeger exporter in an integration test; cargo test -p ser-telemetry -p ser-tool passes and cargo clippy is clean.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
record_encode / record_decode overhead (telemetry feature on) < 50 ns per call (atomic increment + timestamp)
Telemetry feature OFF overhead 0 ns — verified by asm inspection of hot path
ser-tool inspect on 1 MB message completes < 500 ms
ser-tool diff on two 100 KB messages completes < 200 ms
Schema registry round-trip (push + pull + verify hash) hash matches SHA-256(schema bytes)
Prometheus text output parses without error prometheus::TextFormat::parse() returns 0 errors
Span hierarchy depth ≥ 3 visible in test exporter validated by tracing-test subscriber
cargo test --features telemetry passes 100% test pass, no panics
Reflective decode correctness vs codegen decode field-by-field equality on 500 random messages

fixtures/inspect/nested_struct.bin nested pointer traversal tree with indented child struct

12.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Telemetry overhead leaks to hot path encode latency regresses 5-10% under load AtomicU64::fetch_add not compiled away without feature gate perf stat shows atomic ops in flamegraph verify #[cfg(feature)] wraps call sites; check cargo asm
Schema registry hash collision (birthday) wrong schema returned for hash SchemaHash(u64) birthday probability at scale registry returns schema that fails validation extend to SchemaHash(u128) or check canonical schema bytes
Inspector infinite loop on cyclic pointer tool hangs, CPU spins far pointer forms cycle through message segments timeout + depth counter in reflective decoder enforce MAX_DEPTH = 64 in reflective walker
Wire-diff misses struct padding fields diff reports same when values differ padding bytes read as zero in both field-level byte comparison vs. semantic comparison compare only declared fields, not raw data-section bytes
Prometheus text render with NaN/Inf counters scrape endpoint returns malformed text AtomicF64 accumulation overflow or NaN propagation prometheus::TextFormat::parse() error gate float metrics; use integer nanoseconds only
Span context not propagated across thread boundary distributed trace shows broken chains TelemetryContext not sent with async task spans missing parent in Jaeger use tracing::Span::in_scope + explicit context passing
check-compat false pass on union arm renaming operator believes wire is safe; runtime panic compat checker compares arm ids not names add name-change detection to ser-evolve::CompatChecker log warning when arm name changes even if id is stable

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Hot-path cost: AtomicU64 increment is ~3–5 ns per call (cache-hot, negligible contention below 1M ops/s); Instant::now() is ~20–30 ns, so batch to one call per op boundary, not per-field.
  • Operator-tool cost (acceptable, not hot-path): the reflective decoder allocates a HashMap<FieldId,Value> (O(n_fields)); wire-diff's recursive tree compare allocates one heap DiffNode per difference within MAX_DEPTH; inspector on 10 MB+ messages should stream JSON output rather than build it all in memory.
  • Registry & scrape: schema-registry RwLock reads contend under high lookup rate — cache SchemaEntry in thread-local storage after first lookup; Prometheus rendering formats strings only on scrape, never on the encode path; the tiny_http scrape endpoint is single-threaded and will queue above ~100 Hz, which is acceptable for operator tooling.

12.8 Edge Cases

Edge Case Handling
Empty message (0 segments) inspector: "empty message"; diff: identical to other empty
Message with 64 segments (at limit) inspector iterates all segments; no special case needed
Schema with 0 fields (empty struct) inspector prints {} without error
wire-diff on messages with different root TypeId error: "schema root type mismatch"; exit code 2
Registry push of duplicate hash (same schema) idempotent: no error, no duplicate stored
Registry push of hash collision (different schema) error: "hash collision detected"; abort, log both schemas
inspect --format hex on binary with all-zero data section valid output: annotated hex, no false alarm
Telemetry sink Arc drop during encode Option<Arc<...>> checked per call; no-op if sink dropped
RUST_LOG set to trace in production tracing crate filters at runtime; overhead ~ 50 ns per disabled span
Concurrent schema registry write + read RwLock::write() blocks readers; starvation possible under write flood — log warning if write hold > 100 ms

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
ser-telemetry panics in metrics hook panic in hook propagates to encode caller encode call fails wrap hook in catch_unwind (telemetry only); log + disable sink restart with telemetry feature disabled
Schema registry file corrupted on disk SchemaHash computed from file doesn't match stored hash wrong schema returned validate hash on load; refuse corrupted entry re-push schema from source; delete corrupted file
Inspector crashes on malformed pointer tool exits with panic, no output operator tooling only; no prod impact add std::panic::catch_unwind in CLI entry use --lenient flag to skip bad pointers and continue
Prometheus scrape endpoint OOM tiny_http thread killed by OOM scrape fails; no prod impact limit metric label cardinality; cap render buffer reduce cardinality; add ulimit to tool process
wire-diff large message (1 GB) OOM diff process killed operator tooling only stream diff instead of loading both messages add --max-size guard; refuse > 256 MB by default
Tracing exporter drops spans distributed trace incomplete monitoring gaps; no data loss OTEL SDK has retry buffer; log drop count increase exporter batch size; alert on drop counter

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Postmortem guidance: instrument the scrape endpoint with encode_count/validation_failures from day 1 — failures in validation are the primary leading indicator of wire-format corruption or schema mismatch.
  • Metrics export: GET /metrics on --port (default 9090); counters reset on restart; label set: schema_hash, op={encode|decode|validate}, result={ok|err}.
  • Logging: RUST_LOG=ser_runtime=info for prod; debug for encode/decode stage boundaries; trace for per-field access (never in prod).
  • Tracing: enable via OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT; batch size 512; flush on ser-runtime drop.
  • Schema registry dir: default ~/.ser-schemas/; override SER_SCHEMA_REGISTRY_DIR; files named <SchemaHash>.szs.
  • Deployment: ser-telemetry feature flag controlled at compile time; ship two binaries (with/without) or gate at startup via env.
  • Rollback: telemetry hooks are additive; disabling feature or unsetting SER_TELEMETRY=1 reverts to zero overhead.
  • Migration: upgrading ser-telemetry never changes wire format; safe to deploy independently of ser-runtime.
  • Alert thresholds: validation_failures > 0 in 5-min window → page; encode_duration_p99 > 1 ms → investigate.
  • Inspector in CI: ser-tool inspect as post-encode smoke test in integration pipeline; compare against golden JSON.

25. Phase 13 — Conformance, differential, fuzzing, benchmarks — differential vs a copying serializer (Serde/bincode) and vs Cap'n Proto/FlatBuffers semantics; fuzz the validator; encode/decode/access benches

13.1 Objectives

Goal Scope Constraint
Differential correctness vs bincode (Serde-based copying serializer) ser-bench/src/differential.rs Same logical values in/out; wire format differs — compare round-trip values, not bytes
Semantic conformance vs Cap'n Proto encoding rules ser-bench/src/capnp_compat.rs Pointer layout, far-pointer form, list element stride, packed encoding rules
Semantic conformance vs FlatBuffers vtable/offset rules ser-bench/src/flatbuf_compat.rs vtable layout, optional field absence, union discriminant position
Fuzz the validation pass for memory safety + panic-freedom ser-decode fuzz targets via cargo-fuzz / libFuzzer Must never: OOB access, panic, infinite loop, or UB on arbitrary input
Encode/decode/access micro-benchmarks vs bincode + capnproto-rust ser-bench criterion harness 64-byte struct, 1 KB message, 4 KB list, 64 KB nested; encode + decode + field-access separately
Zero-allocation guarantee on access path verified by allocator instrumentation ser-bench/src/alloc_check.rs GlobalAlloc counter must read 0 during accessor calls post-validate
Coverage-guided fuzzing corpus management fuzz/corpus/ Seed corpus from wire format golden files; minimize after each CI run

Architectural constraints: ser-bench depends only on ser-runtime's public API (no internal crate access); fuzz targets live in fuzz/ and are not compiled by a normal cargo build; the differential harness wraps a bincode encode of the same struct and decodes both, asserting field equality via PartialEq; Cap'n Proto conformance is tested at the encoding-spec level (pointer byte layout), not the RPC level.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P15 (conformance as specification), P6 (validate-then-view — fuzz must target validation boundary), I11 (differential correctness), ADR-15 (proptest), ADR-16 (cargo-fuzz), ADR-20 (criterion); B2 sec. 9 (comparative benchmarking methodology), R1 (Cap'n Proto pointer conformance spec), R2 (FlatBuffers vtable conformance spec), R7 (ser-bench — differential design), §8.11 worked example as differential seed.

Differential harness (ser-bench/src/differential.rs): a shared test schema PersonMsg{id:u64,name:Text,age:u32,scores:List<i32>} is round-tripped two ways — the bincode path via serde::Serialize/bincode::serialize, and the ser-runtime path via Builder<PersonMsg> encode + validate()/Reader<PersonMsg> decode — then every field is asserted equal (reader.id()==person.id, etc.); a proptest property checks round-trip equality for any PersonMsg; DifferentialResult{ours_bytes,theirs_bytes,round_trip_ok,alloc_count} tracks the byte-size ratio per test.

Cap'n Proto conformance (ser-bench/src/capnp_compat.rs): encodes known structs with ser-runtime and compares the byte layout against Cap'n Proto's spec tables — struct pointer word [offset:30|0b00:2|data_words:16|ptr_words:16], list pointer word [offset:30|0b01:2|elem_size:3|count:29], far pointer [offset:30|0b10:2|landing_pad:1|seg_id:32] — plus little-endian scalar layout and zeroed canonical padding; ser-tool's reflective decoder is a second cross-check; fn check_pointer_layout(buf, pointer_offset) -> PointerKind parses and asserts each form.

FlatBuffers vtable conformance (ser-bench/src/flatbuf_compat.rs): maps FlatBuffers vtable semantics onto ser-runtime Layout — an absent optional field must read its default (Reader::field_opt() → None/default), the union discriminant sits at offset 0 of the union data section with arm data at offset 1 — and cross-checks field values (not wire bytes, since formats differ) against the flatbuffers Rust crate for the same logical message.

Fuzz targets (fuzz/fuzz_targets/): validate_arbitrary.rs (fuzz_target!(|data: &[u8]| { let _ = ser_runtime::validate(data); })), validate_large_seg.rs (seg_count = u32::MAX → OutOfBounds/Truncated), validate_deep_ptr.rs (pointer chains depth 1000+ → DepthExceeded), decode_reflective.rs (fuzz the reflective inspector), schema_parse.rs (fuzz the IDL parser on arbitrary text); all targets run via cargo fuzz run, 60 s per target in CI, corpus under fuzz/corpus/{target_name}/ seeded from fixtures/wire/; crash triage uses cargo fuzz fmt for a minimized reproducer added to fixtures/fuzz_regressions/; coverage target is llvm-cov branch coverage ≥ 95% on the validation pass.

Benchmark harness (ser-bench/benches/{encode,decode,compare}.rs): Criterion groups encode_64b/encode_1k/encode_4k_list/encode_64k_nested (build PersonMsg, call finish(), measure bytes-out and ns/call) and validate_64b/validate_1k/validate_4k_list plus access_64b/access_1k (pre-encoded buffer, validate() then 1000× reader.id()); compare.rs runs the same logical message through bincode::serialize and capnproto-rust::Builder side-by-side, reporting ns/op + bytes and throughput(Throughput::Bytes(msg_len)) for MB/s comparison.

Zero-allocation verifier (ser-bench/src/alloc_check.rs): a CountingAllocator (GlobalAlloc wrapper with an AtomicUsize count) asserts alloc_count_before == alloc_count_after across a validate() call and across 1000× reader.field_scalar(); Text/Bytes accessors are checked to return borrowed &'a str/&'a [u8] slices (no clone), and the list accessor's ListView<'a,T> is checked to allocate no Vec on iteration.

13.3 Deliverable

  • cargo fuzz run validate_arbitrary -- -max_total_time=60 completes without crash/OOB/panic, and cargo bench -p ser-bench runs all groups with a Criterion HTML report in target/criterion/.
  • Differential harness passes 10,000 proptest cases with zero round-trip mismatches; Cap'n Proto pointer layout matches spec on 50 fixtures; FlatBuffers optional-field-absence default reads are verified for all field types.
  • Zero-allocation access confirmed by CountingAllocator on the validated-buffer accessor loop; coverage report shows ser-decode validation pass ≥ 95% branch coverage; CI job ci/fuzz.sh runs all 5 targets × 60 s and fails on any new crash.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Fuzz: validate_arbitrary — 0 crashes/panics/OOB in 60 s Required for merge
Fuzz: validate_deep_ptr — DepthExceeded returned, no stack overflow Required for merge
Differential: round-trip equality for all field types across 10,000 proptest cases 0 mismatches
Pointer layout conformance (struct/list/far) 100% match to Cap'n Proto spec on 50 fixtures
encode_64b throughput ≥ 500 MB/s (Criterion, no telemetry)
validate_64b throughput ≥ 1 GB/s
access_64b (scalar field read, post-validate) ≤ 5 ns per field
vs bincode encode: our encode ≤ 2× slower (bincode has no schema overhead)
vs capnproto-rust encode same struct within 20% ns/op
Zero-alloc access: CountingAllocator delta on 1000 accessor calls 0 allocations
llvm-cov branch coverage on ser-decode validate ≥ 95%
cargo test -p ser-bench --test differential 0 failures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
fixtures/wire/struct_64b.bin encode_64b benchmark seed + conformance 64-byte data section, pointer section = 0 words
fixtures/wire/struct_1k.bin 1 KB message with nested structs validated without error; field values match source
fixtures/wire/list_4k.bin list of 512 i64 scalars elem stride 8 bytes; count = 512 in list pointer
fixtures/wire/nested_64k.bin deeply nested struct (depth 32) validates in < 1 ms; depth_max = 32 in telemetry

benches/golden/encode_64b_ns.txt benchmark regression floor Criterion regression test ≤ 10% slower than golden

13.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Fuzz target panics on unwrap() in validate libFuzzer reports crash, not error unwrap/expect in validation path compile fuzz with panic=abort; grep for unwrap in ser-decode replace all unwraps with ?/SerError returns
Differential mismatch on f32 NaN equality proptest failure on NaN fields NaN != NaN in Rust PartialEq add special-case: both NaN → equal; bit-pattern compare use f32::to_bits() comparison in differential test
Benchmark result variance masks regression Criterion CI shows "no change" despite 20% slower system noise > signal; Criterion sample count too low increase sample count to 200; enable --bench in CI on quiet machine use criterion::SamplingMode::Flat + noise filter
Cap'n Proto conformance: off-by-one in pointer offset conformance test fails on struct pointer decode Cap'n Proto offsets are word-relative from word after pointer compare with spec diagram byte-by-byte add offset_words diagram to capnp_compat.rs comments
CountingAllocator counts allocations in tracing macros alloc check fails even with zero-copy path tracing span creation may allocate string disable telemetry feature in alloc-check tests #[cfg(not(feature = "telemetry"))] in alloc check
Fuzz corpus explosion (millions of files) CI disk full libFuzzer generates corpus aggressively cargo fuzz cmin after each CI run; cap corpus at 10,000 files schedule weekly corpus minimize + dedup
Schema parse fuzz: exponential blowup on deeply nested types fuzz target OOM recursive type defs trigger O(2^n) layout computation add cycle detection + max recursion = 64 in ser-layout ser-schema validation rejects self-referential types without indirection

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Benchmark methodology: Criterion itself allocates per-iteration, so use iter_custom with pre-allocated buffers to measure only encode/decode; cold-cache runs inflate latency, so raise warm-up from the 3 s default to 5 s on NUMA machines; separate throughput benchmarks (large batch, 10,000 calls/sample) from latency benchmarks (single call, high sample count).
  • Build & tooling cost: linking bincode+capnproto-rust+ser-runtime in one differential test binary bloats link time — split into separate test binaries; cargo fuzz build with opt-level=3+sanitizers takes ~3 min, so cache it in CI; proptest shrinking on failure can take minutes for complex types — cap with max_shrink_iters=1000.
  • Hot-path checks: CountingAllocator's AtomicUsize fetch per alloc is acceptable in tests, never in prod; ListView::get(i) computes base + i*stride branch-free — verify no bounds check survives in --release.

13.8 Edge Cases

Edge Case Handling
Fuzz input length = 0 validate returns SerError::Truncated immediately; no read
Fuzz input length = 1 (partial frame header) SerError::Truncated; no pointer parse
Differential on empty Text field (len=0) both paths return "" — assert equal
Differential on max-u64 field bincode: little-endian; ser-runtime: little-endian; equal
Cap'n Proto conformance: zero-sized struct data_words=0, ptr_words=0; pointer still valid
FlatBuffers conformance: union with no arm set discriminant = 0 (None arm); field reads return default
Benchmark on machine with AVX-512 ensure no SIMD intrinsics in encode/decode; pure scalar — verify with RUSTFLAGS=-C target-cpu=baseline
validate_large_seg: seg_count crafted to u32::MAX frame header size overflow → SerError::OutOfBounds before any segment read
Fuzz: pointer cycle (A → B → A) recursion depth counter hits limit; SerError::DepthExceeded
Proptest shrinking produces zero-field struct differential test must handle 0-field schema gracefully

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
New fuzz crash discovered CI cargo fuzz run exits non-zero; artifact saved validation safety broken block merge; add crash to fuzz_regressions/ fix root cause; re-run full fuzz suite 10 min before re-merge
Differential mismatch in CI cargo test fails; field + values logged correctness bug in encode or decode isolate to field type; check layout offset computation bisect to offending schema change; fix offset in ser-layout
Benchmark regression > 10% Criterion CI baseline comparison fails performance degradation in prod non-blocking for correctness; blocking for release profile with perf/flamegraph; identify hot allocation or branch
CountingAllocator detects allocation in access path test failure: alloc_count_after > alloc_count_before zero-copy guarantee violated block merge trace allocation via backtrace in CountingAllocator::alloc; fix accessor
Cap'n Proto conformance test fails on pointer layout test failure + byte diff logged wire format incompatible with capnp readers non-blocking for own format; blocking if interop required fix pointer word encoding in ser-encode::write_pointer()
Fuzz: stack overflow on deep recursion SIGSEGV in fuzz target UB / safety violation add explicit depth counter before any recursive call MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 64; return DepthExceeded

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Postmortem guidance: any fuzz crash is treated as P0 (root cause typically an unsafe cast without a bounds check, detected by the CI fuzz job); fix is to add the bounds assertion before the cast, add the crash to the regression corpus, and backport to all release branches.
  • CI jobs: fuzzing runs in a separate ci/fuzz.yml job (60 s per target, artifacts uploaded on crash, blocks merge) with corpus committed to fuzz/corpus/ (minimized; raw corpus stays in CI cache); coverage runs cargo llvm-cov --lcov to lcov.info uploaded to codecov, gated at 95% for ser-decode; the Criterion baseline is stored under benches/baselines/, compared on every PR, alerting above a 10% regression; proptest failures log their seed via PROPTEST_SEED for reproducibility.
  • Deployment & versioning: fuzz and bench are dev/CI-only tools, gated behind [[bench]]/[dev-dependencies], never shipped in the release binary; bincode/capnp crate versions are pinned in Cargo.lock so differential results reproduce across machines.
  • Triage & tracking: any new crash runs cargo fuzz fmt <artifact> for a minimized reproducer, filed with the input + stack trace; benchmark results are committed to benches/history/YYYY-MM-DD.json by CI and plotted via ser-tool bench-report; recommend a local 24-hour cargo fuzz run -j8 before major releases, with OSS-Fuzz for continuous fuzzing.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries

UNTRUSTED                          TRUST BOUNDARY                     TRUSTED
---------                          --------------                     -------
wire bytes (network/file/IPC) ---> [ser-decode VALIDATION PASS] ---> Reader<'a,T> accessors
schema IDL from disk          ---> [ser-schema hash + validate]  ---> Schema object in memory
operator config / CLI flags   ---> [ser-cli input parsing]       ---> runtime configuration
codegen templates             ---> [ser-codegen template engine] ---> emitted accessor code
  • Validation pass = sole point of trust elevation; anything that bypasses it is a security defect.
  • Post-validation Reader<'a,T> accessors are considered trusted; their correctness depends entirely on the validation pass being sound.
  • Schema objects must not be constructed from untrusted bytes without running ser-schema hash + structural validation.

Attack Surface Inventory

Vector Threat Mitigation Telemetry Recovery
Malformed wire bytes OOB pointer → read beyond segment Validation: offset + size <= segment.len() validation_rejections_total{reason=out_of_bounds} reject Message; return SerError::OutOfBounds
Crafted far pointer chain Infinite loop / exponential fan-out Max pointer-indirection depth = 64; far pointer must stay in-message validation_rejections_total{reason=bad_pointer} SerError::BadPointer
Oversized segment count Memory exhaustion on Vec<Segment> alloc Max segments per message = 512; max total message bytes = 512 MiB (configurable) validation_rejections_total{reason=truncated} SerError::Truncated
Deep nesting / recursion bomb Stack overflow in validator Explicit depth counter; max recursion depth = 64 (configurable, hard cap 128) validation_rejections_total{reason=depth_exceeded} SerError::DepthExceeded
Misaligned data section Undefined behaviour in cast buf.as_ptr() as usize % align == 0 checked in validation; MIRI in CI validation_rejections_total{reason=misaligned} SerError::Misaligned
List count amplification count * elem_size overflow → OOB count.checked_mul(elem_size)? with overflow guard validation_rejections_total{reason=out_of_bounds} reject
Schema hash collision Wrong schema accepted as compatible 64-bit SchemaHash; store full canonical schema bytes alongside hash in registry schema_mismatch_total SerError::IncompatibleSchema
Replay / stale message Old message re-accepted after schema evolution Schema version field in framing header; evolve-checker rejects field-id conflicts schema_version_mismatch_total decode returns SerError::IncompatibleSchema
Malicious IDL Codegen template injection IDL parser emits typed AST; codegen uses static templates, not eval; no shell expansion audit log in ser-tool reject IDL
Supply-chain (crate deps) Compromised transitive dep Cargo.lock pinned; cargo deny + cargo audit in CI; minimal dep surface CI gate rebuild from audit-clean tree
Operator misconfiguration Too-large depth/size limits Limits validated at startup; out-of-range → fatal error with log startup error metrics restart with corrected config
Persistent corruption (file store) Truncated or bit-flipped stored message Per-message CRC-32C in framing header (optional, default on) crc_failures_total discard message; alert
Privilege escalation ser-tool inspector reading arbitrary file paths CLI validates paths against allowlist; no privilege dropping needed (tool runs as caller) audit log reject
Panic in unsafe cast Memory corruption if validation skipped #[deny(unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn)]; all casts gated behind fn assume_validated(token: ValidationToken) panic hook → metrics crash + restart

Memory-Corruption Risk Specifically

  • ser-decode contains the only unsafe blocks in the entire stack (pointer casts for zero-copy reads).
  • Invariant: every unsafe cast is preceded by a call to ValidationToken::assert_bounds() — a zero-sized proof type that can only be constructed inside the validation pass.
  • MIRI runs in CI on the full decode path with fuzz-generated inputs; any UB is a P0 defect.
  • unsafe block count tracked per crate; PRs that add unsafe without justification are blocked by CI lint.
  • ptr::read_unaligned prohibited; all casts require alignment proof from layout determinism invariant.
  • Sanitizer targets: RUSTFLAGS="-Zsanitizer=address,undefined" in nightly CI job covering ser-decode + ser-encode.
  • Fuzz corpus stored in ser-bench/fuzz/corpus/; cargo fuzz run fuzz_decode runs continuously in CI.

Resource Exhaustion / DoS

  • CPU: validation is O(message bytes) with constant per-pointer work; no backtracking or regex.
  • Memory: validation is zero-alloc (borrows &[u8]); builder uses arena with configurable max-size.
  • File descriptor: no FDs opened during encode/decode; ser-tool opens schema registry files (bounded by config).
  • Thread: validation is single-threaded per message; parallelism is caller's responsibility.
  • Configurable limits (all checked at validation entry, not mid-parse): max_message_bytes (default 512 MiB), max_segments (default 512), max_pointer_depth (default 64), max_list_count (default 2^28).
  • Limits enforced before any allocation; reject with SerError::Truncated or SerError::DepthExceeded immediately.
  • Rate limiting: not in scope for ser-runtime (transport layer); ser-telemetry provides per-source rejection counters for upstream limiters.

Amplification Defense

  • total_bytes_validated tracked and compared against message_framing.declared_size; mismatch rejected.
  • Segment cross-references bounded: far pointer must name a segment id < total_segments; checked during pointer traversal.
  • No recursive schema definitions allowed (ser-schema rejects cycles during type resolution).
  • List element size validated: elem_stride * count must fit in declared segment bytes with no overflow (checked_mul + bounds check).
  • Pointer targets must be non-overlapping within a segment (strict no-aliasing; overlapping pointer targets rejected as SerError::BadPointer).
  • Double-far pointer chain length bounded at 2 hops (Cap'n Proto convention); longer chains rejected.

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy

F1 — Validation Rejection (expected, benign path)

  • Symptom: decode() returns Err(SerError::*) on a message from a peer.
  • Root causes: wire corruption; version mismatch; malicious input; framing bug in sender.
  • Detection: validation_rejections_total counter incremented per error variant.
  • Automatic recovery: caller drops the message; no state mutation occurred.
  • Manual recovery: capture raw bytes via ser-tool wire-diff; identify sender crate version.
  • Forensic artifacts: rejection variant + segment id + byte offset logged at WARN; raw bytes optionally snapshotted.
  • Mitigation: upgrade sender; fix framing; add fuzz corpus entry.

F2 — Schema Incompatibility

  • Symptom: SerError::IncompatibleSchema; field reads return wrong defaults or fail.
  • Root causes: reader schema newer than writer schema (missing required field); field id reuse (evolution violation); incorrect schema hash.
  • Detection: schema_mismatch_total; ser-evolve compat check in CI.
  • Automatic recovery: decode aborts before any accessor is called; no partial read.
  • Manual recovery: run ser-tool schema-diff <old.szs> <new.szs>; identify conflicting field ids.
  • Forensic artifacts: both schema hashes logged at ERROR; stored schema bytes in registry.
  • Mitigation: enforce append-only field ids via ser-evolve gate in CI/CD.

F3 — Arena / Builder OOM

  • Symptom: Builder returns Err(SerError::OutOfBounds) when arena limit reached.
  • Root causes: encoding larger message than arena capacity; nested struct loops in builder code (application bug).
  • Detection: encode_arena_exhausted_total; encode latency spike.
  • Automatic recovery: builder drops all segments; error propagated to caller.
  • Manual recovery: increase ArenaConfig::max_bytes; profile message size distribution.
  • Forensic artifacts: arena high-water-mark metric; stack trace in WARN log.
  • Mitigation: set max_bytes = 2x p99 message size; add size budgets in schema definitions.

F4 — Segment Framing Corruption (persistent store or transport)

  • Symptom: SerError::Truncated; CRC-32C mismatch; partial segment.
  • Root causes: disk bit-flip; network truncation; writer crash mid-write; off-by-one in framing code.
  • Detection: crc_failures_total; truncated_messages_total; framing unit tests.
  • Automatic recovery: message discarded; caller receives error.
  • Manual recovery: ser-tool inspect --raw <bytes> to identify intact segments; reconstruct from replica.
  • Forensic artifacts: segment id, declared length, actual length logged; raw bytes snapshotted (configurable).
  • Mitigation: write-ahead pattern: write to temp file, fsync, rename; CRC verification on read.

F5 — Layout Nondeterminism (platform/compiler divergence)

  • Symptom: message encoded on platform A rejected/misread on platform B; integration tests fail across targets.
  • Root causes: ser-layout bug allowing platform-dependent padding; endianness error; word-size assumption.
  • Detection: cross-platform integration test: encode on x86-64, decode on aarch64 (CI matrix); golden-byte fixtures.
  • Automatic recovery: none; layout bugs are silent data corruption.
  • Manual recovery: compare ser-tool inspect output on both platforms; diff layout dump.
  • Forensic artifacts: Layout struct logged at schema load time; golden fixture files in ser-bench/fixtures/.
  • Mitigation: layout determinism tests in CI; fuzz layout with property-based tests; explicit little-endian read/write helpers.

F6 — Codegen Drift (generated accessors out of sync with schema)

  • Symptom: generated Reader accessor reads wrong field offset; silent wrong-value reads.
  • Root causes: schema updated, codegen not re-run; codegen template bug; schema hash not embedded in generated file.
  • Detection: generated file embeds SCHEMA_HASH: u64; runtime checks hash at startup; mismatch → panic or error.
  • Automatic recovery: runtime refuses to use accessor set with wrong hash.
  • Manual recovery: re-run ser-cli codegen; verify hash matches.
  • Forensic artifacts: hash mismatch logged at ERROR with both hashes.
  • Mitigation: CI step: re-run codegen after any .szs change and verify no diff.

F7 — Panic in Unsafe Accessor (validation soundness bug)

  • Symptom: process crash (segfault / panic) during Reader field access after validation passed.
  • Root causes: validation pass missed an OOB case; UB in cast; platform alignment assumption wrong.
  • Detection: MIRI in CI; cargo fuzz targets for decode; crash dumps.
  • Automatic recovery: process restarts (supervised); message not re-processed (dead-letter queue).
  • Manual recovery: reproduce with minimal fuzz corpus entry; file P0 bug; hotfix unsafe cast.
  • Forensic artifacts: crash dump; fuzz corpus entry; backtrace.
  • Mitigation: MIRI, sanitizers (ASAN/UBSAN via -Zsanitizer), property tests, fuzzing; strict unsafe code review.

F8 — Schema Registry Unavailability

  • Symptom: ser-tool or ser-evolve cannot fetch schema; compat checks skipped.
  • Root causes: registry service down; network partition; file system full.
  • Detection: schema_registry_errors_total; health-check endpoint.
  • Automatic recovery: cached schema used if present (TTL configurable); degrade to hash-only check.
  • Manual recovery: restore registry from backup; re-register schemas from .szs files.
  • Forensic artifacts: registry access log; last-known-good schema cache.
  • Mitigation: local schema cache; registry replicated; ser-tool embeds schema hash in generated code.

F9 — Encode/Decode Latency Regression

  • Symptom: p99 encode or decode latency exceeds SLO threshold; throughput drops.
  • Root causes: memory allocation regression; validation O(N²) bug; pointer-chasing cache miss.
  • Detection: encode_duration_seconds / decode_duration_seconds histograms; ser-bench differential harness.
  • Automatic recovery: none (performance, not correctness).
  • Manual recovery: run ser-bench against prior baseline; profile with perf/flamegraph; bisect commits.
  • Forensic artifacts: ser-bench reports; pprof flame graphs in CI artifacts.
  • Mitigation: ser-bench gate: PR blocked if p50/p99 regresses > 5% vs baseline.

F10 — Evolution Invariant Violation (field id reuse)

  • Symptom: reader misinterprets field as different type; silent data corruption after schema change.
  • Root causes: developer reused a deleted field id; reserved hole not marked in IDL; ser-evolve gate bypassed.
  • Detection: ser-evolve compat-check compares old and new schema; field-id registry in schema hash.
  • Automatic recovery: ser-evolve blocks schema publish if violation detected.
  • Manual recovery: revert schema change; assign new field id; re-publish.
  • Forensic artifacts: ser-evolve diff report; schema version history in registry.
  • Mitigation: ser-evolve required in CI for any .szs change; field id monotonically increasing, stored in IDL.

F11 — Stale Cached Schema (registry cache divergence)

  • Symptom: reader uses outdated schema; new optional fields silently read as defaults even when sender writes real values.
  • Root causes: schema registry cache TTL too long; registry update not propagated; cache invalidation bug.
  • Detection: schema_registry_cache_age_seconds gauge; alert if age > TTL * 2.
  • Automatic recovery: cache miss forces synchronous fetch; stale cache returns warning metric.
  • Manual recovery: ser-tool schema flush-cache; force registry sync.
  • Forensic artifacts: cache hit/miss ratio metric; last-fetch timestamp logged.
  • Mitigation: short TTL (60 s default); schema hash checked on every decode; mismatch triggers re-fetch.

F12 — Benchmark Regression False Positive

  • Symptom: CI blocks PR due to reported latency regression; actual code is correct and fast.
  • Root causes: noisy CI runner; CPU frequency scaling; GC in benchmark harness (not applicable — Rust; but system noise applies); benchmark isolation failure.
  • Detection: ser-bench reports confidence interval; CI gate requires regression > 5% AND p-value < 0.05.
  • Automatic recovery: re-run benchmark 3 times; median used.
  • Manual recovery: run ser-bench locally on dedicated hardware; compare against pinned baseline.
  • Forensic artifacts: ser-bench raw timing CSV committed to CI artifacts.
  • Mitigation: pin benchmark runner to isolated CPU; disable turbo boost; use criterion with 100-sample minimum.

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy (ser-telemetry)

Metric Type Labels SLO Relevance
encode_duration_seconds Histogram schema_hash, message_type Encode p99 < 200 µs (target)
decode_duration_seconds Histogram schema_hash, message_type Decode p99 < 100 µs (target)
validate_duration_seconds Histogram schema_hash Validation overhead < 50% of decode
validation_rejections_total Counter reason (SerError variant) Rejection spike → alert
encode_bytes_total Counter schema_hash Throughput accounting
decode_bytes_total Counter schema_hash Throughput accounting
encode_arena_exhausted_total Counter — Arena sizing alarm
schema_mismatch_total Counter schema_hash Evolution violation alarm
crc_failures_total Counter segment_id Storage integrity alarm
schema_registry_errors_total Counter operation Registry availability
unsafe_cast_invocations_total Counter crate Debug / audit
message_size_bytes Histogram schema_hash Capacity planning
  • Expose via Prometheus /metrics endpoint in ser-runtime; optional OTLP export.
  • Histograms use exponential buckets: [1µs, 2µs, 5µs, 10µs, 50µs, 100µs, 500µs, 1ms, 5ms, 10ms, 50ms].

Tracing Strategy

  • tracing crate integration in ser-telemetry; spans per encode/decode/validate call.
  • Span attributes: schema_hash, segment_count, message_bytes, pointer_depth_max.
  • ser-tool commands emit spans for schema load, compat check, codegen.
  • OTLP exporter configurable; default no-op (zero overhead in production unless enabled).
  • Sampling: head-based, 1% default for encode/decode; 100% for validation rejections and errors.

Structured Logging

  • All log lines: JSON (tracing-subscriber with fmt::json()).
  • Mandatory fields: timestamp, level, crate, schema_hash, message_type, error (if present).
  • Log levels: ERROR for rejections + schema mismatches; WARN for near-limit conditions; INFO for startup/shutdown; DEBUG for per-message detail (disabled in prod).
  • No raw message bytes in logs by default (configurable --log-raw-bytes); bytes only in DEBUG level.

Dashboard Design

  • Panel 1: encode/decode throughput (bytes/s, messages/s) — 1-min rolling.
  • Panel 2: encode/decode/validate latency heatmap (p50, p95, p99, p999).
  • Panel 3: validation rejection rate by error variant (stacked bar).
  • Panel 4: schema mismatch rate + CRC failure rate (alert-correlated).
  • Panel 5: arena exhaustion events + message size distribution.
  • Panel 6: ser-tool schema registry availability + latency.
  • Dashboards as code (Grafana JSON) committed to ser-tool/dashboards/.

Alerting Philosophy

  • Alert on symptoms (latency, rejection rate, error rate), not causes.
  • No alert fatigue: each alert must have a runbook link and a suppression window.
Alert Condition Severity Runbook
ValidateRejectionSpike rate(validation_rejections_total[5m]) > 100/s WARNING runbook/validation-rejections.md
SchemaMismatch schema_mismatch_total > 0 (5-min window) ERROR runbook/schema-evolution.md
DecodeLatencyHigh p99(decode_duration_seconds) > 1ms WARNING runbook/latency.md
ArenaExhausted encode_arena_exhausted_total > 0 (1-min window) WARNING runbook/arena-sizing.md
